# Phase 4 decisions and answered questions

## retryWrites: answered 2026-11-25

**Answer.** `retrywrites=false` is required, not a style choice. Azure DocumentDB does not support
retryable writes. The DocumentDB product team confirmed this in an internal sync on 2026-11-23 and is
changing every sample in Learn and the Azure-Samples repositories to `retrywrites=false`; by
2026-11-25 the TypeScript and .NET Learn samples were consistent. The kit's own
`skills/azure-deployment/SKILL.md` (line 291) already says the feature is not supported, while three
other kit files still show `retryWrites: true` (`skills/connection/SKILL.md:60`,
`skills/driver/driver-singleton-client.md:26`, `skills/security/security-tls-required.md:26`).

**Mechanism, from the engine.** Open issue documentdb/documentdb#767 (filed 2026-10-05): the gateway
passes a NULL transaction id to the engine, so the engine's retryable-write de-duplication table is
never used; sending the same `lsid` + `txnNumber` twice applies the write twice. A driver with
`retryWrites=true` therefore gets no exactly-once protection: a retried non-idempotent write (for
example `$inc`) can be applied twice, and a retried insert reports a duplicate key for a write that
succeeded. That is the concrete risk behind "not supported".

**What is not established.** No source shows that `retryWrites=true` by itself produces connection
errors, which is what the kit's azure-deployment sentence claims. Filings must not assert that.

**What Learn says today.** Every quickstart and the Entra ID page carry `retrywrites=false` in the
connection string, but no Learn page (limitations, compatibility, connect) explains why. That gap is
part of the Learn filings L03 (write semantics) and L07 (connection-string reference) as a concrete
ask rather than a question.

**Canonical one-liner for the kit and samples.**
"Keep `retrywrites=false` in every Azure DocumentDB connection string. The service does not support
retryable writes: the server does not de-duplicate a retried write, so a driver retry can apply a
non-idempotent write twice. Handle transient write failures in application code with idempotent
operations (upserts, deterministic keys)."

Sources: DocumentDB product team, internal sync 2026-11-23 and follow-up 2026-11-25;
https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/767;
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs;
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations;
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language.

## Vector indexes (SME topic 6): settled from the docs on 2026-11-25, two items still open

**Settled, cite these pages, no SME needed.**

- Parameter ranges and defaults, all from the vector search page
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/vector-search, per-algorithm tabs): HNSW `m` 2 to 100
  (default 16), `efConstruction` 4 to 1000 and at least 2m (default 64), `efSearch` default 40; IVF
  `numLists` = documentCount/1000 up to 1M documents, start at 1 for demos, `nProbes` default 1 and at
  most `numLists`; DiskANN `maxDegree` 20 to 2048 (default 32), `lBuild` 10 to 500 (default 50),
  `lSearch` 10 to 1000 (default 40) with `k` at most `lSearch`.
- Dimension limits, from three pages that agree: 2,000 without compression; 4,000 with
  `compression: "half"` on HNSW or IVF; 16,000 with product quantization on DiskANN
  (vector-search "Features and limitations"; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/half-precision;
  https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/product-quantization;
  https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations). Filings quote these numbers.
- Build order: HNSW can be created on an empty collection (vector-search, HNSW column); IVF `numLists`
  depends on document count, so create or re-create it after loading; a product-quantization index
  should be created after the collection has data (product-quantization page, note). A new index can
  return empty results for 2 to 3 minutes while it builds
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs-select-algorithm, Troubleshooting).
- One index per path: "Indexing applies to only one vector per path. You can create only one index per
  vector path." (vector-search features list and the limitations page). The quickstart's narrower
  code comment ("no two vector indexes of the same kind on the same field") is treated as imprecise.
- `listIndexes`: the Learn vector-search sample ("Get vector index definitions") shows the options under `cosmosSearch`, but the open-source engine 0.117 returns them under `cosmosSearchOptions` with no `cosmosSearch` key (ENGINE-TESTS.md, vector-index-rules). Not yet checked on the managed service. Until it is, code and kit helpers must read both keys, and the Learn sample may be wrong.
- BSON binary vectors: Learn requires an array of numbers and says a document whose vector has another
  type is not indexed and is silently absent from results (vector-search, DiskANN field table and IVF
  Important box). Engine issue documentdb/documentdb#109 (closed completed 2026-04-28) confirms
  DocumentDB 0.110 stores and returns BSON binary subtype 9 intact, and its closing comment states that
  index-side search on subtype-9 fields is a separate, untracked feature. The experiment observed the
  same: binary vectors inserted, never returned, and a binary query vector rejected with "expected an
  array type field". Filings may state the exclusion as fact and ask Learn to name binary explicitly.

**Answered by the product owner, 2026-11-25.** HNSW dimension limit: 2,000 uncompressed and 4,000
with half-precision for HNSW and IVF, 16,000 only with product quantization on DiskANN, is correct.
The 8,000 figure on the select-algorithm quickstarts
(https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs-select-algorithm and the Python
variant; "Choosing the right algorithm" table and the HNSW bullet, plus the DiskANN "Why default"
sentence about models exceeding 8,000 dimensions) is wrong and the pages should be changed. Filings
state 2,000/4,000/16,000 as fact and the Learn vector item asks for the quickstart correction.
Where to fix it: the quickstarts embed `ai/includes/choosing-algorithm.md` from
github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples (line 12, the HNSW table row; line 25, "some already
exceed 8,000 dimensions"; line 30, "supports embeddings up to 8,000 dimensions"), and the same repo's
`.github/copilot-instructions.md` line 152 repeats "HNSW's 8,000". One PR to that repository fixes
every language's quickstart at once.
Engine test (reported by the team, 2026-11-25): creating a `vector-hnsw` index with
`"compression": "half"` and `"dimensions": 4001` fails with "field cannot have more than 4000
dimensions for vector index"; the same index with `"dimensions": 4000` is created. So the 4,000
half-precision ceiling is enforced by the engine, not just documented.

**Still open for engineering.**

1. IVF above one million documents. The vector-search IVF field table says `numLists` =
   sqrt(documentCount) above 1M; the Important box under it says to use DiskANN instead. Filings follow
   the Important box.

**Not an SME item.** The error returned for a same-name index with different options, and whether an
identical `createIndexes` call is a no-op, are undocumented; filings describe the ensure-index helper as
tolerant of both outcomes rather than asserting a code.

## Full-text and hybrid search (SME topic 7): settled 2026-11-25, one item open

**Settled.**

- Status: preview, not GA. The overview (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/full-text-search-overview,
  dated 2026-11-04, updated 2026-11-17) says full-text search "remains in preview" while `$text` and
  classic text indexes are GA; the DocumentDB blog post of 2026-11-17 (https://devblogs.microsoft.com/documentdb/?p=259)
  says "currently in preview". Enablement is self-serve: portal Settings > Features > Full-text search >
  Enable, or Azure CLI PATCH of `properties.previewFeatures` adding `FullTextSearch`, or Terraform (AzAPI)
  or Bicep, all on the overview page. "Wait 10 minutes before creating a search index or running a
  query." The release-notes line saying preview features need a support request
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes) is stale.
- `compound` is available with `must`, `should`, `mustNot` and `filter`
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/full-text-search-structured; overview "What's
  available today"). Only `compound.minimumShouldMatch` is unsupported. The kit's statement that
  `compound` is unavailable is wrong.
- Cost and tiers: "Both full-text and vector search are included with your cluster at no extra cost"
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/full-text-search-hybrid); the blog post says no
  separate feature charge. The limitations page lists no tier restriction for full-text search. HNSW and
  DiskANN need M30 or higher (limitations page; release note 2025-07-15). The limitations page's free-tier
  line saying HNSW needs M40 is stale.
- RRF constant direction: the hybrid page's tuning bullet says "a larger k for the keyword query (for
  example, k = 60 for vector and k = 10 for keyword) penalizes lower-ranked keyword hits more
  aggressively." The example is right and the word "larger" is wrong: with score 1/(k+rank), a smaller k
  penalizes lower ranks more (k=10: rank 1 scores about 5.5 times rank 50; k=60: about 1.8 times). Doc
  fix, no SME needed.
- `$listSearchIndexes` is supported. The keyword page
  (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/full-text-search-keyword), the structured page,
  the limitations page ("Search-index management supports creation and listing") and the blog post all
  use it to wait for `status: "READY"` and `queryable: true`. The compatibility table row marking it
  unsupported (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language, dated
  2025-11-05) is stale. Our runs never called it, so this rests on documentation only.
- `CommandNotSupported`, code 115, "Search index creation is not supported yet": observed in
  `runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049` when `createSearchIndexes` ran against a
  cluster without the preview enabled. No Learn page documents it; filings may cite the observation and
  ask Learn to document the error next to the enablement steps.

**Still open for engineering.** Whether code 115 is also returned during the ten-minute activation
window, or a different error. Not blocking: filings tell agents to wait ten minutes and to treat code
115 as "feature not enabled yet".

**Learn fixes this topic yields.** Release-notes "support request" sentence; free-tier HNSW tier
(M40 vs M30); the k-direction sentence on the hybrid page; the `$listSearchIndexes` compatibility row;
documenting code 115.

## Throttling and transient errors (SME topic 2): researched 2026-11-25, 7 settled, 4 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Does Azure DocumentDB return error 16500 / HTTP-429-style throttling (TooManyRequests, RequestRateTooLarge) to a MongoDB client? **engine_source.** No. 16500 TooManyRequests is a request-unit (RU) error of the RU-based Azure Cosmos DB for MongoDB: 'The total number of request units consumed is more than the provisioned request-unit rate for the collection and has been throttled' (error-codes-solutions page, applies to the RU-based API, ms.date 2020-07-15, updated 2026-04-29). Azure DocumentDB (vCore) has no request units, and its own troubleshooting page's 'Error Codes' table (ms.date 2025-06-02, updated 2026-04-27) lists no 16500, 429, TooManyRequests or any throttling code. The open-source engine at commit a5f1636 (2026-11-18) has no MongoDB code 16500 anywhere: not in error_mappings.csv, documentdb_errors.csv or pg_documentdb_gw/include/all_error_mappings_oss_generated.csv (the '429' hits are documentdb_errors.csv row 429 = Location5439014, an internal ordinal, and the triage-bot script), and there is no rate-limiting code path in the gateway (the only 'rate_limit' hit is a certificate-rotation unit-test name in error_reply.rs:556). Observed: across 120 runs no agent ever received a throttling error from the server; the only hits for 16500/TooManyRequests/RequestRateTooLarge in events.jsonl are learn_microsoft_docs_search queries and their Cosmos DB RU results (9 run folders by exact string, about 28 with a broader throttling pattern, e.g. runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049, runs/03-substitute-product-finder/20261124-183232), and the 63-64 05-audit.json files that mention 429/throttling are audit-generated best-practice text. The documented resource-pressure signals are timeouts and latency, not throttling codes: 'Default query execution limit: 120 seconds. This limit can be overridden on a per-query basis using maxTimeMS' and 'Maximum transaction lifetime: 30 seconds' (limitations page, ms.date 2026-11-04, updated 2026-11-17); the gateway maps a cancelled query to code 50 ExceededTimeLimit with the message 'The command being executed was terminated due to a command timeout ... Consider increasing the maxTimeMS on the command.' (pg.rs:277-295). The one admission-style error in the engine is PostgreSQL TOO_MANY_CONNECTIONS mapped to code 261 TooManyLogicalSessions, 'There are too many open connections.' (error.rs:518-521, pg.rs:507-511), which the gateway does not retry (query_dispatch.rs classify_retry: TOO_MANY_CONNECTIONS => Retry::None). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cosmos-db/mongodb/error-codes-solutions (row 16500 TooManyRequests; page applies to RU-based API; ms.date 2020-07-15, updated_at 2026-04-29); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-common-issues (Error Codes table; ms.date 2025-06-02, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (MongoDB execution limitations; ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17); github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636269e5062fa22a24bfa4d58d3cc92a0bc7: error_mappings.csv, documentdb_errors.csv, pg_documentdb_gw/include/all_error_mappings_oss_generated.csv (no 16500); github.com/documentdb/documentdb pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/pg.rs:277-295 (QUERY_CANCELED -> ExceededTimeLimit), :507-511 (TOO_MANY_CONNECTIONS -> TooManyLogicalSessions); github.com/documentdb/documentdb pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/error.rs:518-521; github.com/documentdb/documentdb pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/postgres/conn_mgmt/query_dispatch.rs:75-143 (classify_retry); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs (120 runs; grep of events.jsonl and 05-audit.json: observed codeNames BadValue, CommandNotSupported, CannotCreateIndex, IndexOptionsConflict, DuplicateKey; no throttling code)
- Does the engine return the TransientTransactionError error label that drivers' withTransaction retry relies on? **engine_source.** No. The gateway's error reply carries only ok, code, codeName and errmsg: responses/error.rs:64-71 'doc.append("ok" ...); doc.append("code" ...); doc.append("codeName" ...); doc.append("errmsg" ...)' and there are zero occurrences of errorLabels, TransientTransactionError, UnknownTransactionCommitResult or RetryableWriteError anywhere in the repository at commit a5f1636 (2026-11-18). Engine issue #339 'Missing label for transient errors in transactions' (opened 2025-09-30, labels enhancement + gateway, still OPEN, last bot stale-ping 2026-11-17) reproduces this against the managed service (vCore 7.0, HA): vanilla MongoDB returns 'Code 112 ... Write conflict ... Labels: [TransientTransactionError]' while the service returns 'Code 112, Message: Could not acquire lock within transaction, Labels: []', so 'the driver [does] not retry the conflicting one. One of the transactions fails and we lose an update'. The maintainers' triage #698 (closed 2026-08-31) records '#339 TransientTransactionError label VERDICT: NOT FIXED | high - ... CommandError emits only ok/code/codeName/errmsg; zero hits for errorLabels/TransientTransactionError repo-wide; LOCK_NOT_AVAILABLE -> WriteConflict without label'. Issue #428 shows the same for NoSuchTransaction (251): MongoDB returns errorLabels ['TransientTransactionError'], the service returns none. Mechanism: pg.rs:297-315 maps LOCK_NOT_AVAILABLE inside a transaction to WriteConflict (112), and a duplicate key inside a transaction is also surfaced as WriteConflict (pg.rs:245-258); process.rs:449-452 then aborts the server-side transaction on WriteConflict. The MongoDB convenient-transactions spec says 'the driver MUST retry a transaction that fails with an error bearing the "TransientTransactionError" label', so without the label withTransaction() surfaces the 112 to the application and the whole transaction must be re-run by application code. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/339; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/698 (section '## #339 TransientTransactionError label'); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/428; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/error.rs:64-71; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/pg.rs:245-258, :297-315; processor/process.rs:449-452; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/specifications/master/source/transactions-convenient-api/transactions-convenient-api.md; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/transactions-in-applications/
- Does the engine return the UnknownTransactionCommitResult error label? **engine_source.** No. The same evidence applies: the gateway never writes an errorLabels field (responses/error.rs:64-71) and the string does not occur in the repository at a5f1636. The spec's commit retry ('If an UnknownTransactionCommitResult error is encountered for a commit, the driver MUST retry the commit if and only if the error is not MaxTimeMSExpired and the retry timeout has not been exceeded', 120-second limit) therefore never triggers from a server-supplied label. Drivers attach this label themselves for network and server-selection errors on commitTransaction, so a dropped connection during commit may still be retried by the driver; a server-side commit error on this service is not. This research did not verify driver-side labelling behaviour against the service. Sources: github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/error.rs:64-71 (repo-wide grep: no errorLabels/UnknownTransactionCommitResult); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/698 (section '## #339'); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/specifications/master/source/transactions-convenient-api/transactions-convenient-api.md
- What does a client experience during in-region HA failover, and is application-side retry required? **documented.** Learn documents the behaviour but not an error code. High availability page (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated 2026-10-29): 'The switch to standby is done with no downtime for reads. Write operations may require internal service retries during the switch phase. These retries might be seen as write slowness on the application side.' and 'The cluster connection string always stays the same regardless of failovers.' Under-the-hood page (ms.date 2026-10-28): 'Write operations in progress during the failover are retried within the service to ensure continuity.' Reliability page (ms.date 2026-09-21, updated 2026-11-23): 'In-flight requests that weren't acknowledged before failover can fail and must be retried by the client. If your application handles transient faults, these retries typically complete automatically.' and 'For write operations, a brief interruption can occur while failover completes.' and 'You're responsible for configuring your application's driver retry settings to handle transient failures, especially connection interruptions and short write interruptions during failover events ... Configure retries with exponential backoff, and limit the number of retry attempts ... design write operations to be idempotent so that retrying them is safe.' The same page adds that planned maintenance 'can still cause brief transient failures for client operations'. Combined with topic 1 (retrywrites=false is required) and the absence of any RetryableWriteError label from the server (retryable-writes spec: for 4.4+ wire versions drivers retry writes only when the server adds the label), driver-level write retry is unavailable, so the kit must tell agents to retry transient write failures in application code with idempotent operations. Observation: no HA failover occurred during the 120 runs, so no failover error string was observed. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-10-29); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-10-29); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb (ms.date 2026-09-21, updated_at 2026-11-23); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability-replication-best-practices (ms.date 2026-10-28); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/specifications/master/source/retryable-writes/retryable-writes.md; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/findings/phase4/DECISIONS.md (retryWrites section)
- What does a client experience during cross-region promotion / failover (forced, graceful, service-managed)? **documented.** Documented behaviour, no code. how-to-cluster-replica (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated 2026-08-21), graceful promotion: 'During the failover, write requests return a transient error until the switch completes. The global read-write connection string automatically updates to point to the new primary cluster. Applications that use the self connection string of the former primary must be updated to point to the new primary for write operations.' failover-modes (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated 2026-06-02): 'Because writes are paused while the replication lag drains, the application sees a short write-availability gap.' troubleshoot-replication (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated 2026-06-02): 'During the promotion process, the global read-write connection string is updated to point to the newly promoted replica cluster once it's open for write operations.' and 'When you promote the replica cluster in region B, the ex-primary cluster in region A is set to read-only mode, effectively making it the replica cluster for the newly promoted primary cluster.' Reliability page (updated 2026-11-23), region failure: 'Any active requests to the failed primary region might fail. After failover completes, applications should reconnect and retry against the promoted cluster.' and 'Once a promotion is initiated, it typically completes within a few minutes.' and 'Applications that use cluster-specific connection strings might require configuration updates'. Service-managed failover exists and is opt-in (failover-modes; limitations page 'Service-managed failover is opt-in. It must be enabled on the primary cluster before a regional outage occurs'; troubleshoot-replication: 'Brief, transient errors don't trigger a service-managed failover.'). Engine source shows the code a write to a cluster running in replica mode gets: READ_ONLY_SQL_TRANSACTION with is_replica_cluster => IllegalOperation (20), 'Cannot execute the operation on this replica cluster', deliberately terminal ('a retryable code would loop the driver against the same server', pg.rs:455-464); the same SQL state on a non-replica cluster is retried by the gateway with the long policy (query_dispatch.rs classify_retry READ_ONLY_SQL_TRANSACTION => Retry::Long) and, if it persists, surfaced as ExceededTimeLimit (50) 'Exceeded time limit while waiting for a new primary to be elected' (pg.rs:466-470). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-08-21); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/failover-modes (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-06-02); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-replication (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-06-02); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb (updated_at 2026-11-23); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (Cross-region failover; updated_at 2026-11-17); github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/pg.rs:455-470; postgres/conn_mgmt/query_dispatch.rs:75-143
- In the open-source gateway, what decides whether a write to a read-only node gets the terminal IllegalOperation 20 ('replica cluster') path or the retried-then-ExceededTimeLimit 50 ('waiting for a new primary') path? **engine_source.** The replica-cluster flag is derived from PostgreSQL state, not from an Azure-side setting. configuration/dynamic.rs:189-193: 'fn is_replica_cluster(&self) -> bool { (self.get_bool(POSTGRES_RECOVERY_KEY, false) && self.equals_value("citus.use_secondary_nodes", "always")) || self.get_bool("simulateReadReplica", false) }', where POSTGRES_RECOVERY_KEY = "IsPostgresInRecovery" (dynamic.rs:23) is populated from the backend's recovery status at configuration load (pg_configuration.rs:126 'configs.insert(POSTGRES_RECOVERY_KEY.to_owned(), in_recovery.to_string())'). query_dispatch.rs:89-95 then classifies READ_ONLY_SQL_TRANSACTION as Retry::None when request_options.in_replica_cluster_mode() and Retry::Long otherwise, and pg.rs:455-470 maps the two cases to IllegalOperation 20 'Cannot execute the operation on this replica cluster' (terminal by design: 'a retryable code would loop the driver against the same server') and ExceededTimeLimit 50 'Exceeded time limit while waiting for a new primary to be elected' respectively. Consequence in the open-source build: a node that is a standby / in recovery with secondary-node routing (a replica cluster before promotion, or a demoted former primary after it) answers writes with code 20 immediately; a primary that is merely read-only without being in recovery (for example during a graceful drain) is retried by the gateway and, if the budget is exhausted, surfaces code 50. This settles the mechanism for the open-source gateway at a5f1636 only; the hosted gateway's configuration during promotion and its retry budget are not published (see OPEN). Sources: github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs:23, :189-193 (fn is_replica_cluster); github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/pg_configuration.rs:126; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/postgres/conn_mgmt/query_dispatch.rs:89-95; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/responses/pg.rs:455-470
- Which MongoDB stepdown-family codes can the engine emit at all (10107, 189, 11602, 91, 6)? **engine_source.** From the open-source mappings at a5f1636: 10107 NotWritablePrimary, 91 ShutdownInProgress and 6 HostUnreachable exist (all_error_mappings_oss_generated.csv rows 'NotWritablePrimary,M00E8,10107', 'ShutdownInProgress,M00F0,91', 'HostUnreachable,M00EU,6'); 189 PrimarySteppedDown, 11602 InterruptedDueToReplStateChange and 13435 NotPrimaryNoSecondaryOk do not exist in any mapping file. The gateway emits HostUnreachable with 'Could not establish a connection to the server.' when the PostgreSQL connection is lost or refused (error.rs:529-535, :560-575), ShutdownInProgress with 'Request terminated due to shutdown on the server.' for CANNOT_CONNECT_NOW/ADMIN_SHUTDOWN/CRASH_SHUTDOWN (error.rs:522-528, pg.rs:530-537), and NotWritablePrimary only when the engine raises its own ERRCODE_DOCUMENTDB_NOTWRITABLEPRIMARY, with the hedged message 'The server is not currently writable. This may be due to the database disk being full.' (pg.rs:197-205; documentdb_conversion_codes.c:2586). Before any of these reach the client the gateway retries internally: closed connection => Retry::Short; ADMIN_SHUTDOWN, CANNOT_CONNECT_NOW, CONNECTION_FAILURE, non-replica READ_ONLY => Retry::Long (query_dispatch.rs:75-143). None of these replies carries a RetryableWriteError label. Learn documents none of these codes; the troubleshooting table lists only 1, 2, 5, 9, ... 13297 and omits 6, 24, 50, 91, 112, 251, 261 and 10107. Sources: github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/include/all_error_mappings_oss_generated.csv; error_mappings.csv; documentdb_errors.csv; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/error.rs:518-575; responses/pg.rs:197-205, :530-537; pg_documentdb/src/utils/documentdb_conversion_codes.c:2584-2587; github.com/documentdb/documentdb @ a5f1636: pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/postgres/conn_mgmt/query_dispatch.rs:75-143; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-common-issues (Error Codes table)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Which exact error code(s) and messages does a client receive for a write that fails during an in-region HA shard failover on the managed service, and for how long? Why open: Every current Learn page (high-availability, under-the-hood, reliability, troubleshoot-replication) describes the effect ('write slowness', 'brief interruption', 'can fail and must be retried', 'brief, transient errors') without naming a code; the open-source gateway shows candidates (6 HostUnreachable after internal retry, 91 ShutdownInProgress, 50 ExceededTimeLimit 'waiting for a new primary') but the hosted gateway build and its retry budgets are not public, and no run exercised a failover. The 2026-06-02 DocumentDB blog post announcing service-managed failover GA also names no error (blog = supporting evidence only). Best guess: Most failovers are absorbed by the gateway's internal long/short retry and appear as latency; a write that outlives the retry budget surfaces as code 50 ExceededTimeLimit or a network/connection error (HostUnreachable 6), never as 189 PrimarySteppedDown and without a RetryableWriteError label. Filings should say 'treat connection errors and code 50 during failover as transient; retry idempotent writes in application code' and must not quote 10107/189.
2. On the managed service, during a cross-region promotion, is the demoted former primary (and the replica during graceful drain) running with IsPostgresInRecovery=true and citus.use_secondary_nodes=always (so writes get the terminal IllegalOperation 20 'Cannot execute the operation on this replica cluster'), or does it take the retried-then-ExceededTimeLimit 50 path? And what exact code is the 'transient error' named on the how-to-cluster-replica page? Why open: The how-to page says only 'write requests return a transient error' and troubleshoot-replication adds only that the former primary 'is set to read-only mode'; the open-source gateway's rule (dynamic.rs:189-193, now SETTLED above) depends on PostgreSQL recovery state and a Citus GUC whose values on the hosted clusters during each promotion phase are not published, and the hosted retry budget is unknown. Best guess: Source-backed inference from the open-source gateway: writes via the global read-write connection string see a short window of connection errors or code 50 ('Exceeded time limit while waiting for a new primary to be elected') while the primary is draining, then succeed against the new primary once the string flips; writes via the old cluster's self connection string get code 20 IllegalOperation once that node is a standby and never recover until the string is changed. Filings should recommend the global read-write connection string for clusters with a replica and name both codes as the ones to expect.
3. Does the managed service's hosted gateway apply any admission control (per-cluster connection cap or request cap) that surfaces as an error code, and is 261 TooManyLogicalSessions ('There are too many open connections.') what a client sees when the PostgreSQL connection limit is reached? Why open: No Learn page under /azure/documentdb states a connection limit per tier or any throttling behaviour: compute-storage (ms.date 2026-07-27, updated 2026-07-27) lists only vCores and RAM per tier and names CPU, memory, IOPS, storage, network and 'Concurrency' as sizing inputs with no cap; the limitations page mentions only CPU, memory, IOPS and storage; a Learn-restricted search for 'max connections' / 'connection limit' / 'too many connections' returned only Amazon DocumentDB and Cosmos DB SDK pages. The mapping to 261 is in the open-source gateway, but the hosted connection limits per tier are not published. Best guess: There is no request-rate throttling; the only hard admission error is 261 TooManyLogicalSessions when the backend connection limit is exhausted, which is not retried by the gateway. Filings should tell agents to size pools conservatively and treat 261 as 'reduce connections', not 'back off and retry'.
4. Is there a plan or ETA to emit TransientTransactionError (and RetryableWriteError / UnknownTransactionCommitResult) labels in the gateway, so that drivers' withTransaction retry works as on MongoDB? Why open: Issue documentdb/documentdb#339 has been open and assigned since 2025-09-30 with only triage-bot stale pings through 2026-11-17 and no maintainer statement of intent; the triage report #698 marks it NOT FIXED. A gh search of documentdb/documentdb issues and PRs for errorLabels / TransientTransactionError / RetryableWriteError / UnknownTransactionCommitResult finds nothing beyond #339, #428 and #698; the only related open PR, #455 'Add commitTransaction and abortTransaction admin db error handling' (OPEN, updated 2026-11-11, addresses #428's missing-field handling), has zero occurrences of any label string in its diff. Best guess: Not in the near term. The kit should document application-level retry of the whole transaction on code 112 WriteConflict and 251 NoSuchTransaction, with bounded attempts and backoff, instead of relying on withTransaction's label-driven retry.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-common-issues: ms.date 2025-06-02. The Error Codes table omits codes the engine actually emits (6 HostUnreachable, 20 IllegalOperation on a replica cluster, 24 LockTimeout, 50 ExceededTimeLimit, 91 ShutdownInProgress, 112 WriteConflict, 251 NoSuchTransaction, 261 TooManyLogicalSessions, 10107 NotWritablePrimary) and says nothing about throttling. Add those rows and an explicit sentence that Azure DocumentDB has no request-unit throttling and never returns 16500/429, because agents import that guidance from the RU-based Cosmos DB for MongoDB pages (observed in the run logs).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-transactions: ms.date 2023-08-28, updated 2026-11-18. Says 'The callback can run more than once if the driver retries the transaction', but the service does not return the TransientTransactionError label (engine issue #339), so withTransaction() never retries a write conflict; the page should state that code 112 WriteConflict (and 251 NoSuchTransaction) arrive without labels and show an application-level retry loop. It also omits the 30-second transaction lifetime that the limitations page states.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/cross-region-replication: ms.date 2026-10-28, updated 2026-10-29 (the newest of the set) says 'For applications using the global read-write connection string, write operations continue seamlessly throughout the promotion process, maintaining uninterrupted data flow', while failover-modes (updated 2026-06-02) says 'the application sees a short write-availability gap', how-to-cluster-replica (updated 2026-08-21) says 'write requests return a transient error until the switch completes', troubleshoot-replication (updated 2026-06-02) says the global string is updated 'once it's open for write operations', and the reliability page (updated 2026-11-23) says 'applications should reconnect and retry against the promoted cluster'. Documentation inconsistency; engineering question: which is right, and what code is the transient error.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica: 'write requests return a transient error until the switch completes' names no error code or codeName; applications cannot build a retry predicate from it. Name the code (and whether it is the same for forced, graceful and service-managed failover), and whether writes through the former primary's self connection string get the terminal code 20 IllegalOperation.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-replication: ms.date 2026-05-14, updated 2026-06-02. A troubleshooting page for replication that never names an error code: 'the ex-primary cluster ... is set to read-only mode' and 'Brief, transient errors don't trigger a service-managed failover' should say what a write to the read-only ex-primary returns (open-source gateway: 20 IllegalOperation 'Cannot execute the operation on this replica cluster') and what the brief transient errors look like to a driver.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability: Describes failover as 'write slowness' and names no error; the reliability page says in-flight requests 'can fail and must be retried by the client'. Add the error code(s) a client may see and link to retry guidance, so the two pages agree.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb: Updated 2026-11-23: 'Although some Azure DocumentDB APIs include references to same-zone deployment modes, Azure DocumentDB doesn't support same-zone HA deployments', while the high-availability and how-to-scale-cluster pages (updated 2026-10-29 / 2026-11-05) say SameZone can be requested through non-portal APIs and that the service 'falls back to same-zone HA' when zones are unavailable. Inconsistency noticed in passing (not a topic-2 blocker).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language: ms.date 2025-11-05, updated 2026-06-13. Marks startTransaction/commitTransaction/abortTransaction as supported but has no row or note for error labels (TransientTransactionError, UnknownTransactionCommitResult, RetryableWriteError), retryable writes or retryable reads, which are the compatibility facts an application needs for retry logic.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compute-storage: ms.date 2026-07-27. Lists vCores and RAM per tier and names 'Concurrency' as a sizing input but publishes no connection limit per tier and no statement about admission control, so applications cannot size pools against a documented cap or recognise 261 TooManyLogicalSessions. Add the per-tier connection limit (or state that none is enforced) and the error a client sees when it is reached.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/connection/SKILL.md:230`: Retry helper retries only on 'err.code === 16500 || err.code === 429', RU-era Cosmos DB codes that Azure DocumentDB never returns (not in any engine mapping file; not on the Learn troubleshooting table). The helper therefore never retries a real transient error. Replace with network errors, code 50 ExceededTimeLimit, 112 WriteConflict (transactions) and 6/91, with idempotency caveats; treat 20 IllegalOperation and 261 TooManyLogicalSessions as non-retryable.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/connection/SKILL.md:211-219`: 'Azure DocumentDB supports retryable reads' has no Learn source (no page under /azure/documentdb mentions retryReads or retryable reads), and 'For transient errors (network blips, failovers), the driver will automatically retry' is overstated: retrywrites=false is required (topic 1) and the server emits no RetryableWriteError label, so drivers do not retry writes; the reliability page puts retry responsibility on the application.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/high-availability/ha-cross-region-replica.md:15,30`: States 'Failover is *not* automatic across regions' and 'No automatic cross-region failover'. The failover-modes page (ms.date 2026-05-14), how-to-cluster-replica, troubleshoot-replication and the reliability page document opt-in service-managed failover that 'automatically promote[s] the replica cluster when it detects a regional outage'. Needs updating to 'not automatic unless service-managed failover is enabled'.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/high-availability/ha-enable-for-production.md:7`: 'The connection string does not change on failover, so applications keep working transparently' overstates Learn: the reliability page says 'In-flight requests that weren't acknowledged before failover can fail and must be retried by the client' and the HA page says writes may see slowness. Line 12 ('In-flight writes during failover are retried inside the service') matches the under-the-hood page and can stay.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/sharding/sharding-hot-partition-diagnosis.md:20`: Row 'Throttling concentrated on one shard' implies a per-shard throttling signal; no Learn page or engine code defines throttling for this service. Reword as latency/timeout (code 50) concentrated on one shard.

## Host forms: <cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com vs <cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com (filings K01, K03, K10, L05, L06, L07) (SME topic 3): researched 2026-11-25, 9 settled, 3 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- What are the two host forms, and what does Learn call them? **documented.** They are the two connection strings the portal offers on a cluster's Connection strings page. The plain host `<cluster-name>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` is the 'self' connection string (portal field label 'Self (always this cluster)'); the `<cluster-name>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` host is the 'global read-write' connection string. The RBAC/Entra connect page (ms.date 2026-03-11, updated_at 2026-11-23) states: "The host is at either the `*.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` or `*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` domain depending on whether you're using the current cluster or global read-write endpoint." The replica how-to (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-08-21) says: "Copy the self connection string for currently selected cluster to connect to that cluster. (optionally, on the primary cluster only) Copy the global read-write connection string that always points to the cluster available for writes." The Private Link page adds: "On a replica cluster only self connection strings are provided." Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (section 'Get cluster credentials'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica#use-connection-strings; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cross-region-replica-portal (step 'Copy the value from the Self (always this cluster) field'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link (section 'Get connection string to connect')
- When is the `.global.` host required? Only for clusters with a cross-region replica? **documented.** It is never required. It is the string that keeps writes working without an application change when a replica is promoted, and it exists only on the primary (read-write) cluster. Learn: "The `+srv` scheme and the `*.global.*` host ensures that your client is dynamically connected to the appropriate writable cluster in a multi-cluster configuration even if a region swap operation occurs." (RBAC page). "Before promotion, the global read-write connection string targets the primary cluster in Region A and then updates to point to Region B as it assumes write responsibilities." (cross-region-replication, ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-10-29). "If you use self connection string for write operations, you need to update connection string in your application to point to the promoted replica cluster to continue writes to the database after promotion is completed." (how-to-cluster-replica). The DR under-the-hood table marks 'No connection string change' for all three cross-region failover modes with the footnote "† When using the global read-write connection string." So: use `.global.` for writes on any cluster that has or may get a replica; it is optional otherwise. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/cross-region-replication#continuous-writes-read-operations-on-cluster-replicas-and-connection-strings; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica#use-connection-strings; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood (summary table footnote); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/failover-modes (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-06-02: 'The global read-write connection string automatically points to the new primary cluster after the switch completes.')
- Does the plain (self) host work for a single cluster with no replica? Does the `.global.` host? **documented.** Both do, per documentation. The RBAC page states verbatim: "In a single-cluster configuration, you can use either connection string indiscriminately." The plain host is also what the mongosh page, the Cloud Shell page, the Learn training unit and the REST API sample response ('default connection string', api-version 2026-06-01) show. Observation (corrected after the skeptic's check): the plain host form was observed working in this experiment. The loop supplies the cluster through DOCUMENTDB_URI (loop/run_loop.py:157; the project's `.env` holds a URI on the plain `<cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` host and mcp.json derives the MCP endpoint from the same variable), and 91 of 120 run folders contain `documentdb_*` MCP calls with outcome 'ok' against that cluster (documentdb_list_indexes 111, documentdb_list_databases 79, documentdb_find_documents 49, documentdb_sample_documents 21, documentdb_explain_operation 9, documentdb_aggregate 5, documentdb_count_documents 4); 41 of 117 05-audit.json files state the build ran 'against the live cluster'. The 58 `.global.` mentions in events.jsonl come from Learn-search results and kit text that agents read, not from a connection, so the `.global.` form has no observation in this experiment. The earlier report's '0 of 30 / 0 of 14, no connection' sentence was wrong and is withdrawn. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-mongo-shell (ms.date 2025-10-14, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-cloud-shell (ms.date 2025-10-14, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/training/modules/create-configure-azure-documentdb-cluster/3-connect-to-cluster (ms.date 2026-04-09, updated_at 2026-06-19); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/document-db/mongo-clusters/list-connection-strings?view=rest-document-db-2026-06-01 (sample response); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/*/*/events.jsonl (records with tool documentdb_* and outcome ok: 91 of 120 folders; observation); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/*/*/05-audit.json (41 of 117 contain 'against the live cluster'; observation); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/loop/run_loop.py:157 (cluster_uri reads DOCUMENTDB_URI; the configured host is the plain form)
- Should kit and Learn guidance say 'keep whichever the portal gives'? **documented.** Yes, with one qualifier. Documented rule: on a single cluster either string works; on a cluster with a replica, writes should use the global read-write string (available on the primary only) and reads may use either the self string or the global string; replica clusters offer only self strings; after promotion the former primary's self string is read-only. Two further Learn pages support this wording. The training unit (ms.date 2026-04-09, updated_at 2026-06-19): "There are two connection strings available in the portal: one for global read-write access and one the current cluster (Self), for most of these scenarios use the connection string for the current cluster to connect from your client." The Mongoose tutorial (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-24, the newest Learn page on the topic) tells the reader to take the hostname from the cluster's `connectionString` property: it "typically has the form `<cluster-name>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` or `<cluster-name>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`. Use the returned hostname instead of constructing it from the cluster name." Canonical wording for the kit and the L07 reference: "Copy the connection string from the portal (Cluster > Settings > Connection strings) or `az documentdb mongocluster list-connection-strings`. On a single cluster the Self string (`<cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`) and the Global read-write string (`<cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`) are interchangeable. If the cluster has or may get a cross-region replica, use the Global read-write string for writes so that a promotion needs no code change; the Self string of the old primary becomes read-only after promotion. Replica clusters expose only a Self string. Never construct the host from the cluster name; use the string the portal, CLI or REST API returns." Kit default snippets may show the plain host (matching mongosh, Cloud Shell, the training unit and the REST default string) with that note. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica#use-connection-strings; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link (note: 'On a replica cluster only self connection strings are provided.'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cross-region-replica-portal (section 'Write to promoted cluster replica': former primary 'is set to read-only'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/training/modules/create-configure-azure-documentdb-cluster/3-connect-to-cluster; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/tutorial-nodejs-mongoose (section 'Verify the cluster from a terminal')
- Which Learn pages show which form (the inconsistency the filings noticed)? **documented.** All six driver quickstarts hard-code `<your-cluster-name>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` while telling the reader to copy the portal 'Connection string' field and never mention the two fields: Node.js (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27), Python (2026-03-25 / 2026-08-25), Java (2026-03-25 / 2026-08-25), C# (2026-03-25 / 2026-04-27), Go (2026-03-25 / 2026-04-27), Rust (2025-10-14 / 2026-04-27); the RBAC/Entra page's code samples and the Mongoose tutorial's `.env` example also use `.global.`. The plain form appears in how-to-connect-mongo-shell, how-to-connect-cloud-shell, the training unit, how-to-cross-region-replica-portal (primary, replica and promoted replica), the Private Link DNS verification steps (`_mongodb._tcp.<your-cluster-name>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`), the REST Get and listConnectionStrings samples, and the kit/MCP READMEs. The difference between the two strings is explained on several pages (RBAC/Entra 'Get cluster credentials'; how-to-cluster-replica 'Use connection strings'; cross-region-replication; availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood; failover-modes; the Private Link note; the training unit's Note; the Mongoose tutorial), but none of the connect pages a SCRAM user reads first (the six driver quickstarts, quickstart-portal, how-to-connect-mongo-shell, how-to-connect-cloud-shell) mentions that two fields exist. This is a documentation inconsistency of presentation, not of fact: both forms are valid. (The earlier sentence 'Only the RBAC page explains the difference' was wrong and is withdrawn.) Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-python; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-java; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-dotnet; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-go; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-rust; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-mongo-shell; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-cloud-shell; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cross-region-replica-portal; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link#test-dns-resolution; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/training/modules/create-configure-azure-documentdb-cluster/3-connect-to-cluster; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/tutorial-nodejs-mongoose; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cluster-replica#use-connection-strings; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/cross-region-replication#continuous-writes-read-operations-on-cluster-replicas-and-connection-strings
- Does in-region HA failover change the host or connection string? **documented.** No. "High availability uses synchronous replication to ensure zero data loss and includes automatic failure detection and failover. After a failover, the connection string remains unchanged." (replica portal how-to, Tip). The DR under-the-hood summary table marks in-region HA 'No connection string change ✔️' without the global-string footnote, and states "Whether your application connects to a single shard or multishard cluster, it uses a single connection string and endpoint." Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cross-region-replica-portal; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood
- How do the CLI, REST API, ARM/Bicep and Terraform expose the connection string(s)? **documented.** CLI: `az documentdb mongocluster list-connection-strings` (preview) "List mongo cluster connection strings. This includes the default connection string using SCRAM-SHA-256, as well as other connection strings supported by the cluster." REST (api-version 2026-06-01, ms.date 2026-10-23): POST .../listConnectionStrings returns `connectionStrings[]` of `ConnectionString {connectionString, description, name}`; the sample response has one entry on the plain host described as "default connection string". In addition (corrected after the skeptic's check), the REST Get operation (same api-version, ms.date 2026-10-23, updated_at 2026-10-29) defines a read-only property `connectionString` (string): "The default mongo connection string for the cluster." with the sample value `mongodb+srv://<user>:<password>@myMongoCluster.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`, so the default (plain-host) string is also returned by GET, by `az documentdb mongocluster show`, by a Bicep `existing` reference's `properties.connectionString`, and the Mongoose tutorial reads exactly that property with `az rest --method get` at api-version 2025-09-01. Terraform azurerm_mongo_cluster exports `connection_strings` blocks with `name`, `description`, `value` ("The `<user>:<password>` placeholder returned from API will be replaced"). The ARM/Bicep template reference for Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters (latest 2026-06-15-preview, page updated_at 2026-10-20) lists writable properties only and therefore shows no connection-string property; it is read-only output, not absent. None of these references names a global string. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster#az-documentdb-mongocluster-list-connection-strings (ms.date 2026-09-01, updated_at 2026-11-17); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/document-db/mongo-clusters/list-connection-strings?view=rest-document-db-2026-06-01; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/document-db/mongo-clusters/get?view=rest-document-db-2026-06-01 (MongoClusterProperties.connectionString and sample response); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/tutorial-nodejs-mongoose (section 'Verify the cluster from a terminal': `$cluster.connectionString` from `az rest --method get`); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/main/website/docs/r/mongo_cluster.html.markdown (attribute connection_strings); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters
- Does the `allowedHosts` / ALLOWED_HOSTS wildcard `*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` used by the MCP server and the kit also admit the two-label `.global.` host? **documented.** Yes. The MCP server passes the profile's `allowedHosts` unchanged to the driver as `authMechanismProperties.ALLOWED_HOSTS` (src/context/documentdb.ts:37). The MongoDB authentication specification defines the property: "The hostnames may include a leading \"*.\" wildcard, which allows for matching (potentially nested) subdomains." So both `*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` (MCP README, kit README, Mongoose tutorial) and `*.azure.com` (Learn RBAC page) match both host forms. The Mongoose tutorial (updated_at 2026-11-24) adds the reason the pattern must be a wildcard at all: "The driver checks the actual hosts returned by DNS SRV resolution, which can differ from the cluster hostname in your URI. Keep this list restricted to the service domain; don't use `*`." and its troubleshooting row: "The allow list must cover the service hosts returned by DNS SRV resolution, not just the cluster hostname in the URI." The Private Link page shows those SRV targets as `<your-node-hostname>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`. The spec also says ALLOWED_HOSTS "MUST NOT be allowed in the URI connection string" and applies only to the human (browser) OIDC callback, which is consistent with the MCP's Entra flow setting it programmatically. Sources: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/src/context/documentdb.ts:37; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/specifications/master/source/auth/auth.md (MONGODB-OIDC, ALLOWED_HOSTS); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/tutorial-nodejs-mongoose (section 'Connect Mongoose to Azure DocumentDB' and 'Troubleshoot common issues'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link#test-dns-resolution; /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/test/security/connectionProfiles.test.ts:51 (test fixture uses the .global. endpoint)
- Which host form applies behind a private endpoint? **documented.** Learn says either: "Always use `mongodb+srv` connection string to connect to database from a client with a private IP address ... Copy global read-write or self connection string for native DocumentDB authentication or Microsoft Entra ID authentication." The private DNS zone is `privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`, the target sub-resource is `MongoCluster`, and the documented DNS verification queries the plain host's SRV record (`_mongodb._tcp.<your-cluster-name>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`). Note also: "Private Link ... doesn't prevent your Azure DocumentDB endpoints from being resolved by public DNS." Whether the `.global.` host resolves through the private zone is a separate OPEN item below. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link (ms.date 2026-07-27)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Does `az documentdb mongocluster list-connection-strings` (and the REST listConnectionStrings action / Terraform `connection_strings`) return the global read-write string on a single cluster and on a primary with a replica, and what `name` and `description` values identify the self, global and Entra ID strings? Why open: The CLI description promises 'other connection strings supported by the cluster' but no primary source enumerates them. Checked after the skeptic's pointer: the TypeSpec in Azure/azure-rest-api-specs (MongoCluster.tsp lines 376-391) defines `ConnectionString {connectionString, description, name}` with all three fields read-only and `name` `@added(Versions.v2024_07_01)`; the examples for 2026-06-01, 2026-09-01 and 2026-10-15-preview (MongoClusters_ListConnectionStrings.json) all show one plain-host entry described 'default connection string' and omit `name`; the Azure CLI extension source (_list_connection_strings.py) only mirrors the schema and has no output example. The REST Get's read-only `connectionString` is described as 'The default mongo connection string for the cluster.' and uses the plain host, which indicates 'default' means the self string, but nothing says whether the global or Entra ID strings are in the list or how they are named. Best guess: The action returns at least the SCRAM default (plain host); whether the global and Entra ID strings are included, and under what `name` values, needs a live `az documentdb mongocluster list-connection-strings` call on a single cluster and on a primary with a replica, or an engineering answer. Filings should tell agents to read `name`/`description` rather than assume a position in the array, and to prefer `properties.connectionString` from GET when they only need the default string.
2. On a cluster reachable only through a private endpoint, does the `.global.` host resolve to the private IP through the `privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` zone, or only the self host? Why open: The Private Link page says to copy either the global read-write or the self string, but its DNS verification and troubleshooting steps only exercise the plain host and the single `privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` zone; no Learn page shows the global host's SRV or A records in a private zone. Supporting evidence only (not settling): a Microsoft Q&A thread (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/answers/a/12785467, question 2026-05-12, accepted answer 2026-05-18 by a Q&A moderator not identified as Microsoft staff) shows the asker's nslookup resolving `<id>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com -> <id>.global.privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com -> 10.0.4.4`, i.e. the global host has a CNAME into the same privatelink zone and resolved to a private IP; the TCP-timeout problem in that thread is unrelated to DNS. Best guess: Both resolve privately (the Learn page would not say 'copy global read-write or self' otherwise, and the Q&A nslookup shows the global host CNAME-ing into the privatelink zone), so a private DNS zone named `privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` must hold a record for `<cluster>.global` as well as `<cluster>`. Filings about private endpoints should use the self string in examples and cite the Q&A as expected behaviour until Learn confirms it.
3. What exactly does a client on the global read-write string see while a graceful promotion or service-managed failover is in progress (error code / label), and for how long? Why open: how-to-cluster-replica says only that 'During the failover, write requests return a transient error until the switch completes'; troubleshoot-replication (ms.date 2026-05-14, updated_at 2026-06-02) adds only 'During the promotion process, the global read-write connection string is updated to point to the newly promoted replica cluster once it's open for write operations.' and 'Brief, transient errors don't trigger a service-managed failover.' No page names a code or label. This overlaps SME topic 2 (throttling and transient errors). Best guess: Driver-level network or not-writable errors with no retryable label; handle with application-level retry. Record under topic 2.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs (and quickstart-python, quickstart-java, quickstart-dotnet, quickstart-go, quickstart-rust): Each quickstart hard-codes `<your-cluster-name>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` yet instructs the reader to copy 'the value from the Connection string field', while the portal shows two fields (Self and Global read-write) and the mongosh, Cloud Shell, training-unit and REST pages use the plain host. Add the one-sentence explanation from the RBAC page (either works on a single cluster; use global for writes when a replica exists) or switch to the plain host so all connect pages agree.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-mongo-shell and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-cloud-shell (and quickstart-portal): Both say copy 'the Connection string field' (singular) and show only the plain host; neither names the Self / Global read-write fields or links to an explanation. The two strings are explained on the Entra ID page, the replica how-to, the replication concept pages and the training unit, but on none of the connect pages a SCRAM user reads first. The explanation belongs on a connection-string reference (filing L07) linked from every connect page.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-cross-region-replica-portal and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-replication: Inconsistent with how-to-cluster-replica (newer, 2026-05-14/updated 2026-08-21): the replica-portal page says the replica's Connection strings and Global distribution items are under 'Cluster management' while the primary's are under 'Settings', and both pages document promotion as a single 'Promote' button, whereas how-to-cluster-replica documents 'Forced Promote' and 'Graceful Promote' and the Azure CLI `replica promote` command (troubleshoot-replication does describe the three failover modes in prose but still shows the single button). 'Promote replica cluster' on the portal page should also state which connection string keeps working (global) as the concept page does.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/data-api: The curl examples mix `{cluster-name}.data.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` (aggregate, getSchema) and `{cluster-name}.data.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` (listDatabases, listCollections) with no explanation of the two Data API host forms; page ms.date is 2024-11-03 (updated_at 2026-11-05). Pick one or explain both.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-private-link: 'Get connection string to connect' says to copy either the global read-write or the self string, but the DNS verification and troubleshooting sections only test the plain host and the `privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` zone. State whether and how the `.global.` host resolves through the private zone (a Microsoft Q&A thread shows it CNAME-ing to `<id>.global.privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/document-db/mongo-clusters/list-connection-strings?view=rest-document-db-2026-06-01 (and the CLI page for `az documentdb mongocluster list-connection-strings`, and the examples in Azure/azure-rest-api-specs for 2026-06-01, 2026-09-01 and 2026-10-15-preview): The sample response shows a single 'default connection string' entry and omits the `name` field defined in the schema (added in api-version 2024-07-01); the 'other connection strings supported by the cluster' are never enumerated (global read-write, Entra ID). Document the full set with their `name`/`description` values, and cross-reference the Get operation's read-only `connectionString` ('The default mongo connection string for the cluster.') so readers know the two ways to obtain the default string.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control: The Node.js sample sets `ALLOWED_HOSTS: ['*.azure.com']` while the Mongoose tutorial (updated_at 2026-11-24, one day newer) and the MCP README use `*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` and the tutorial explains that the list is checked against SRV-resolved node hosts; both patterns match both host forms (the spec allows nested subdomains), but the pages should agree and the tighter pattern is the better default. Already tracked in filings L05/K03; noted here because the host-form sentence lives on this page.
