_Notes: Final after skeptic review. Topic 3 is settled from Learn alone; no SME needed for the filings. The authoritative sentences are on the RBAC/Entra page (updated 2026-11-23): either string works on a single cluster, the global read-write string follows writes across a replica promotion, and replica clusters expose only self strings; the Learn training unit (2026-04-09/2026-06-19) and the Mongoose tutorial (2026-11-16/2026-11-24) say the same and the tutorial adds 'use the returned hostname instead of constructing it from the cluster name'. Portal labels are 'Self (always this cluster)' and 'Global read-write'. Three sentences from the original report were corrected rather than reopened: (a) the experiment DID connect to the live cluster through the plain host (91 of 120 run folders contain documentdb_* MCP calls with outcome ok, per-tool counts verified; 41 of 117 audits say 'against the live cluster'; the project's .env and DOCUMENTDB_URI use the plain form; the 58 `.global.` mentions in events.jsonl are Learn-search results and kit text, not connections), so the observation is 'plain host works under SCRAM with public access; global host unobserved'; (b) the RBAC page is not the only page explaining the two strings; the accurate gap is that none of the first-contact connect pages (six driver quickstarts, quickstart-portal, mongosh, Cloud Shell) mentions the two fields; (c) the default connection string is also exposed as the read-only `properties.connectionString` of the mongoClusters resource (REST Get, `az documentdb mongocluster show`, Bicep existing reference), so it is not obtainable only through listConnectionStrings; the ARM template reference shows writable properties only. The three OPEN items stay open and are non-blocking: the TypeSpec and the newest API examples (2026-09-01, 2026-10-15-preview) still show one unnamed 'default connection string' entry; a Microsoft Q&A thread (2026-05) showing the global host CNAME-ing to `<id>.global.privatelink.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` is supporting evidence only; troubleshoot-replication adds no error code for the promotion window (topic 2). New since the original report: Mongoose tutorial states ALLOWED_HOSTS is checked against SRV-resolved node hosts and recommends `*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` (the RBAC page's `*.azure.com` is the outlier); troubleshoot-replication also shows a single 'Promote' button; kit file skills/security/security-entra-rbac.md:15 carries the nonexistent `.mongocluster.documentdb.azure.com` suffix in addition to local-env-driven-config.md:68. The engine repository (documentdb/documentdb) contains no reference to the global host; host forms are an Azure service concern, not an engine one. Learn page dates: RBAC 2026-03-11/2026-11-23; how-to-cluster-replica 2026-05-14/2026-08-21; cross-region-replication, how-to-cross-region-replica-portal and availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood 2026-10-28/2026-10-29; failover-modes and troubleshoot-replication 2026-05-14/2026-06-02; how-to-private-link 2026-07-27; mongosh and Cloud Shell 2025-10-14/2026-04-27; quickstart-portal 2025-10-14/2026-10-08; data-api 2024-11-03/2026-11-05; tutorial-nodejs-mongoose 2026-11-16/2026-11-24; training unit 2026-04-09/2026-06-19; CLI reference 2026-09-01/2026-11-17; REST Get and list-connection-strings 2026-10-23/2026-10-29; ARM reference updated 2026-10-20. No connection strings, passwords, cluster names or e-mail addresses appear in this report._

## Driver and ODM version floors (SME topic 4): researched 2026-11-25, 12 settled, 5 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- 4.1 Minimum PyMongo version for MONGODB-OIDC (drafts cite 4.7). **engine_source.** PyMongo 4.7. The driver changelog says: "PyMongo 4.7 brings a number of improvements including: Added support for MONGODB-OIDC authentication. The MONGODB-OIDC mechanism authenticates using an OpenID Connect (OIDC) access token." The 4.7.0 GitHub release was published 2024-04-24 (PyPI upload the same day). Two refinements worth stating next to the floor: the PyMongo OIDC page says "As of PyMongo v4.14, synchronous OIDC requests do not block the asyncio loop" (relevant when OIDC is used with AsyncMongoClient), and the current PyMongo line (4.19/4.20) requires Python >= 3.11 (PyPI requires_python). No Learn page states a PyMongo floor for Entra ID; the Learn console-app how-to installs `pip install pymongo` unpinned and uses pymongo.auth_oidc OIDCCallback. Sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/mongo-python-driver/master/doc/changelog.rst (section 'Changes in Version 4.7.0'); https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-python-driver/releases/tag/4.7.0 (publishedAt 2024-04-24); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/languages/python/pymongo-driver/current/security/authentication/oidc/; https://pypi.org/pypi/pymongo/4.7.0/json (upload 2024-04-24, requires_python >=3.7); https://pypi.org/pypi/pymongo/4.19.0/json (requires_python >=3.11); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-python-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-11-23)
- 4.2 Minimum Node.js `mongodb` driver version for MONGODB-OIDC (drafts cite 6.7). **engine_source.** mongodb 6.7.0. The Node driver release notes (v6.7, published 2024-05-29) say: "Adds support for the MONGODB-OIDC authentication mechanism when connected to MongoDB Server v7.0 and later. The driver supports authentication with Azure machine authentication, GCP machine authentication, callback authentication, and human interaction callback authentication facets." Learn's Entra ID page and Node console-app how-to use the callback facet (`OIDC_CALLBACK` plus `ALLOWED_HOSTS: ['*.azure.com']`) with `npm install mongodb` unpinned. Related floors: Node driver 7.x requires Node.js v20.19.0 or later (v7.0.0 release note: "Updates the minimum Node.js version to v20.19.0"); 6.x requires Node v16.20.1 or later. Sources: https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/node/current/whats-new/ (section 'What's New in 6.7'); https://github.com/mongodb/node-mongodb-native/releases/tag/v6.7.0 (publishedAt 2024-05-29); https://registry.npmjs.org/mongodb (time['6.7.0'] 2024-05-29; latest 7.9.1 engines node >=20.19.0; 6.20.0 engines node >=16.20.1); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-nodejs-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-11-23); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (ms.date 2026-03-11, updated_at 2026-11-23)
- 4.3 Minimum Java driver version for MONGODB-OIDC. **engine_source.** Java driver 5.1. In the driver source, `MongoCredential.createOidcCredential` and the `OIDC_CALLBACK` mechanism property carry Javadoc `@since 5.1` and `@mongodb.server.release 7.0`; `MongoCredential.java` at tags r4.10.0, r4.11.0 and r5.0.0 contains zero occurrences of 'oidc' (and the r5.0.0 AuthenticationMechanism enum has no OIDC member), and the r5.1.0 release (2024-05-02) lists "Enhanced OIDC support". The MongoDB what's-new page's earliest OIDC entry is also 5.1 ("Enhanced support for the MONGODB-OIDC authentication mechanism"); the word 'Enhanced' is misleading because 5.0 had no OIDC at all, but the source settles 5.1 as the floor. Learn publishes a Java callback: how-to-build-java-console-app uses `MongoCredential.createOidcCredential(null).withMechanismProperty("OIDC_CALLBACK", oidcCallback)` with `mongodb-driver-sync` 5.4.0 and `azure-identity` 1.15.4; the Entra ID RBAC page itself has only Python, TypeScript and C# samples (page metadata devlang: python typescript csharp). Sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/mongo-java-driver/master/driver-core/src/main/com/mongodb/MongoCredential.java (createOidcCredential Javadoc: @since 5.1, @mongodb.server.release 7.0; OIDC_CALLBACK_KEY @since 5.1); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mongodb/mongo-java-driver/r5.0.0/driver-core/src/main/com/mongodb/MongoCredential.java (0 matches for 'oidc'; same for r4.10.0 and r4.11.0); https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-java-driver/releases/tag/r5.1.0 (publishedAt 2024-05-02, 'Enhanced OIDC support'); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/java/sync/current/whats-new/ (5.1 section); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-java-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-11-23; mongodb-driver-sync 5.4.0, azure-identity 1.15.4)
- 4.4 Minimum .NET driver version for MONGODB-OIDC. **engine_source.** .NET/C# driver 2.25.0. The GitHub release (2024-04-12) lists "Support of MONGODB-OIDC Authentication mechanism - CSHARP-4448" and "MONGODB-OIDC: Automatic token acquisition for Azure Identity Provider - CSHARP-4474"; the what's-new page for 2.25 says "Added support for the MONGODB-OIDC authentication mechanism and automatic token acquisition for Azure Identity Provider." Learn's .NET console-app how-to implements `MongoDB.Driver.Authentication.Oidc.IOidcCallback` and `MongoCredential.CreateOidcCredential(tokenHandler)` with `dotnet add package MongoDB.Driver` unpinned (.NET quickstart requires .NET 10.0 or later). Note the driver's 3.x line is a different major; 2.25 is the floor, 3.x is current. Sources: https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-csharp-driver/releases/tag/v2.25.0 (publishedAt 2024-04-12); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/csharp/current/whats-new/ (2.25 section); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-dotnet-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-04-29); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-dotnet (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27; '.NET 10.0 or later')
- 4.5 PyMongo floor for `AsyncMongoClient`: 4.9 (beta), 4.11, or 4.13 (GA)? **documented.** All three numbers are real and mean different things. PyMongo 4.9 (2024-09-18): "Adds a new experimental asynchronous API as a replacement for Motor. This API is in beta and is subject to change before the generally available release." PyMongo 4.13 (2025-05-14): "The PyMongo Async API is generally available." PyMongo 4.11 is not an async milestone; it is the floor Beanie 2.x declares (`pymongo>=4.11.0,<5.0.0`). So: AsyncMongoClient exists from 4.9, is GA from 4.13, and Beanie 2 needs >= 4.11. For a kit floor, 4.13 is the defensible 'GA' number; Learn's tested pin is 4.19.0 (which requires Python >= 3.11). The kit's current text 'PyMongo 4.9+' describes the beta. Sources: https://www.mongodb.com/docs/languages/python/pymongo-driver/current/reference/release-notes/ (4.9 and 4.13 entries); https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-python-driver/releases/tag/4.13.0 (publishedAt 2025-05-14); https://pypi.org/pypi/pymongo/4.9.0/json (upload 2024-09-18); https://pypi.org/pypi/pymongo/4.13.0/json (upload 2025-05-14); https://pypi.org/pypi/beanie/2.0.0/json (requires_dist pymongo<5.0.0,>=4.11.0); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-python-beanie (ms.date 2026-11-17, updated_at 2026-11-24: 'tested with Beanie 2.2.0 and PyMongo 4.19.0')
- 4.6 Should the kit recommend Beanie 2.x on `AsyncMongoClient` and mark Beanie 1.x + Motor as not recommended? **documented.** The evidence supports exactly that pairing; only the wording is a maintainer choice. Beanie 2.0.0 (PyPI 2025-07-20; changelog entry dated 2025-07-09, 'Motor -> async pymongo') removed Motor and declares `pymongo>=4.11.0,<5.0.0` with no motor dependency; 2.0.1 adds `!=4.15.0`; 2.1.0 and 2.2.0 require Python >=3.10,<3.14 and pydantic >=2.4,<3. Learn's Beanie quickstart (ms.date 2026-11-17, updated 2026-11-24) says "This example uses Beanie 2 and PyMongo's asynchronous client, not Motor" and "If your app uses Beanie 1 with Motor, stop and update the app to Beanie 2 and PyMongo's AsyncMongoClient before using this setup"; the Beanie tutorial (ms.date 2026-11-16) repeats both sentences and pins beanie==2.2.0 pymongo==4.19.0. Observed in this experiment: Beanie 1.x + Motor stacks failed with `MotorDatabase object is not callable` in runs/20-identity-resolution-multi-agent/20261124-210002 and runs/11-preference-center-consent-store/20261125-064954, and old Motor/Beanie against current PyMongo failed with `cannot import name '_QUERY_OPTIONS' from 'pymongo.cursor'` in runs/07-customer-review-sentiment-pipeline/20261125-054042, runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049 and runs/11-preference-center-consent-store/20261124-204614 (observations from events.jsonl). Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/beanie/2.0.0/json; https://pypi.org/pypi/beanie/2.0.1/json; https://pypi.org/pypi/beanie/2.2.0/json (requires_python <3.14,>=3.10); https://beanie-odm.dev/changelog/ (2.0.0 'Motor -> async pymongo'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-python-beanie; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/tutorial-python-beanie (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-24); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/20-identity-resolution-multi-agent/20261124-210002/events.jsonl; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/11-preference-center-consent-store/20261125-064954/events.jsonl; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049/events.jsonl
- 4.7 Correct Motor deprecation and end-of-life dates. **documented.** Deprecated May 14, 2025; end of life May 14, 2026; critical fixes only until May 14, 2027. The Motor page states: "As of May 14, 2025, Motor is deprecated in favor of the GA release of the PyMongo Async API in the PyMongo library. We will not add new features to Motor, and we will provide only bug fixes until it reaches end of life on May 14, 2026. After that, we will fix only critical bugs until final support ends on May 14, 2027." Motor's last PyPI release is 3.7.1, uploaded 2025-05-14. The kit's 'May 2024' deprecation and 'critical fixes through May 2026' are wrong under every source. Caveat for filings: the PyMongo migration guide says "Motor will be deprecated on May 14th, 2026" and JIRA MOTOR-1463 uses the same 2026 wording, so MongoDB's own docs disagree by one year on the word 'deprecated'; the Motor page and README are the product's own statement and the dates for EOL (2026) and final support (2027) agree everywhere. Sources: https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/motor/; https://github.com/mongodb/motor (README deprecation notice); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/languages/python/pymongo-driver/current/reference/migration/ (Warning under 'Migrate From Motor'); https://pypi.org/pypi/motor/json (3.7.1 uploaded 2025-05-14)
- 4.8 Minimum driver version per language for server versions 5.0 to 8.0. **documented.** Azure DocumentDB offers compatibility versions 5.0, 6.0, 7.0 and 8.0 (Learn compatibility page, 'feature support across versions 5.0-8.0'; the Learn FAQ states it explicitly: "Azure DocumentDB supports MongoDB wire protocol versions 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, and 8.0"). Both pages add that any wire-protocol driver can connect and name no driver versions. The only published per-version driver matrix is MongoDB's, which is about MongoDB Server, not Azure DocumentDB; read it as the wire-protocol floor. Fully compatible (✓) with 5.0 through 8.0: PyMongo 4.9 to 4.20 (4.4-4.8 are partial for 8.0); Node.js driver 6.9 to 7.9 (5.7-6.8 partial for 8.0; 5.0-5.6 partial for 7.0 and 8.0); Java sync 5.2 to 5.14 (4.10-5.1 partial for 8.0); .NET/C# 2.29 to 3.13 (2.20-2.28 partial for 8.0); Go 2.1 to 2.10; Rust 3.1 to 3.10 (2.6-3.0 partial for 8.0). Mongoose's own table: MongoDB 8.x needs Mongoose ^8.7.0 or ^9.0.0, 7.x needs ^7.4.0|^8|^9, 6.x needs ^7|^8|^9, and 5.x is not listed. Current releases: pymongo 4.20.2, mongodb 7.9.1 (6x tag 6.22.0), mongoose 9.13.1. Observed: the experiment's samples pinned backwards (mongodb ^5.x in 16 of 26 direct-driver Node samples, mongoose ^7.x in 21 of 22) and 13 runs hit `npm ERR! notarget` for nonexistent versions such as mongodb@^5.10.0/^5.11.0/^5.12.0 and mongoose@7.9.3/^7.14.0 (runs listed in notes). Whether Microsoft has its own tested floors for DocumentDB remains open (4.8-open). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-06-13); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq (ms.date 2026-11-06, updated_at 2026-11-07; 'Which MongoDB versions and features does Azure DocumentDB support?'); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/compatibility.md (tabs Python, JavaScript, Java, .NET/C#, Go, Rust; tables quoted in notes); https://mongoosejs.com/docs/compatibility.html; https://registry.npmjs.org/mongodb; https://registry.npmjs.org/mongoose; https://pypi.org/pypi/pymongo/json; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/03-core-banking-transaction-ledger/20261124-200452/events.jsonl (notarget; 12 further run folders in notes)
- 4.9 Whether Node 24 is tested. **documented.** By MongoDB: Node driver 7.0 to 7.9 are marked fully compatible with Node.js v24.x.x; driver 6.11 to 6.21 are marked for v22, v20, v18 and v16 only, and the v24 cell is blank ("A blank cell means that the combination is incompatible or untested"). Learn's Node quickstart requires "Node.js 22 or newer" and the Node console-app how-to "Latest long-term support (LTS) version of Node"; neither names a tested Node or driver version. Observed: the experiment's build environment reported `Node.js, npx available (v24.21.0)` (runs/09-lead-scoring-agent/20261124-204408 and runs/09-trade-settlement-tracker/20261124-201323, readiness report quoted in events.jsonl decisions), so every Node sample was built on Node 24, mostly with mongodb 5.x/6.x pins. Whether Microsoft tested Node 24 against Azure DocumentDB is open (4.9-open). Sources: https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/compatibility.md (JavaScript tab, 'Node.js Compatibility' table: '7.0 to 7.9 | ✓ v24.x.x'; '6.11 to 6.21' blank for v24); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-nodejs-console-app; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/09-lead-scoring-agent/20261124-204408/events.jsonl (readiness_report: 'Node.js, npx available (v24.21.0)')
- 4.10 Tested pins for the LlamaIndex, Java, .NET, Go and Rust quickstarts (what Learn states today). **documented.** Learn states tested versions only for the Python ODM pages: Beanie quickstart and tutorial "tested with Beanie 2.2.0 and PyMongo 4.19.0" on Python 3.13.x; Django quickstart "tested with Django MongoDB Backend 6.1.0 and PyMongo 4.19.0" (Django 6.1.3). Java: quickstart-java pins `mongodb-driver-sync` 5.2.0 (Java 21 or later) while how-to-build-java-console-app pins 5.4.0 plus azure-identity 1.15.4; neither says 'tested'. .NET: `dotnet add package MongoDB.Driver` unpinned, prerequisite .NET 10.0. Go: `go get go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/v2/mongo` unpinned, prerequisite Go 1.18; the page links the driver's API reference, source and pkg.go.dev but no Microsoft sample repository. Rust: `cargo add mongodb` unpinned, prerequisite Rust 1.70. LlamaIndex (query-knowledge-base, ms.date 2026-05-06, updated 2026-11-25): `pip install llama-index llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo ... pymongo` unpinned, although the prose is specific to `llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo` 0.9.0; PyPI shows 0.9.0 (2026-08-31) is the latest and requires `llama-index-core>=0.13.0,<0.15` and `pymongo>=4.6.1,<5`. So the question 'which pins were tested' is settled as 'Learn publishes none for .NET, Go, Rust and LlamaIndex, and two different Java pins'; the underlying tested versions are open (4.10-open). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-java (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-08-25); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-java-console-app (updated_at 2026-11-23); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-dotnet; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-go (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-rust (ms.date 2025-10-14, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-knowledge-base (ms.date 2026-05-06, updated_at 2026-11-25); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-python-django (ms.date 2026-11-17, updated_at 2026-11-24); https://pypi.org/pypi/llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo/json (0.9.0, 2026-08-31)
- 4.13 Does a current Learn page list the Azure DocumentDB compatibility versions explicitly and state a driver compatibility position? (missed source found by the skeptic, confirmed) **documented.** Yes. The Learn FAQ (ms.date 2026-11-06, updated_at 2026-11-07), under 'Which MongoDB versions and features does Azure DocumentDB support?', says: "Azure DocumentDB supports MongoDB wire protocol versions 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, and 8.0, and 99.03% of MongoDB query, projection, update, and aggregation operators." Under 'Are MongoDB drivers and tools compatible with Azure DocumentDB?' it says: "Any driver that supports the MongoDB wire protocol connects to Azure DocumentDB, including the official MongoDB drivers for Node.js, Python, Java, .NET, Go, and other languages. Tools such as MongoDB Compass and the MongoDB shell (mongosh) also work." The 'Is Azure DocumentDB a drop-in replacement' answer adds that "frameworks such as Mongoose and Spring Data connect without code changes". The page names no minimum or tested driver version anywhere (confirmed by reading the full page). This is a second current Learn page (after the compatibility page's table headers) that names the four versions explicitly, so filings about the upgrade page can cite it; it strengthens 4.8-open by showing that the newest Learn statement on drivers is still version-free. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq (ms.date 2026-11-06, updated_at 2026-11-07; sections 'Which MongoDB versions and features does Azure DocumentDB support?', 'Are MongoDB drivers and tools compatible with Azure DocumentDB?', 'Is Azure DocumentDB a drop-in replacement for MongoDB?')
- 4.14 Does Microsoft publish its own driver pins anywhere, and do they agree with Learn? (missed source found by the skeptic, confirmed) **documented.** Microsoft's sample repository Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples (HEAD c7ce4ac, committed 2026-11-24) pins drivers, inconsistently with itself and with Learn. Confirmed at HEAD via the GitHub API: .NET `MongoDB.Driver` 3.2.0 (ai/select-algorithm-dotnet/SelectAlgorithm.csproj) and 3.0.0 (ai/vector-search-dotnet/DocumentDBVectorSearch.csproj); Go `go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver v1.17.6` (the v1 module) in ai/select-algorithm-go/go.mod, ai/vector-search-go/go.mod and ai/vector-search-agent-go/go.mod (go 1.24.0), whereas the Learn Go quickstart installs `go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/v2/mongo` and imports `go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/v2/...`; Python `pymongo>=4.7.0` (ai/select-algorithm-python/requirements.txt) and `pymongo>=4.6.0` (ai/vector-search-python/requirements.txt, whose comment still says 'for connecting to Cosmos DB'); Node `mongodb ^6.18.0` (ai/select-algorithm-typescript and ai/vector-search-typescript/package.json) and `^7.1.0` (ai/vector-search-agent-typescript/package.json). These are vector-search and select-algorithm samples, not the driver quickstarts, and the Learn quickstart pages link to no GitHub sample repository, so this does not settle which versions the quickstarts were tested with (4.10-open stays open). It settles that Microsoft-published pins exist, that they are lower bounds rather than tested versions, and that the Go samples and the Go quickstart target different major versions of the driver (v1 vs v2), which filings can cite as an inconsistency. Sources: https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples (HEAD c7ce4ac7e77e3745edea9771f8ecb1c0ec344378, 2026-11-24; repo metadata updated_at 2026-09-22, pushed_at 2026-11-24); https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples/blob/main/ai/select-algorithm-dotnet/SelectAlgorithm.csproj (MongoDB.Driver 3.2.0); .../ai/vector-search-dotnet/DocumentDBVectorSearch.csproj (3.0.0); https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples/blob/main/ai/select-algorithm-go/go.mod; .../ai/vector-search-go/go.mod; .../ai/vector-search-agent-go/go.mod (go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver v1.17.6); https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples/blob/main/ai/select-algorithm-python/requirements.txt (pymongo>=4.7.0); .../ai/vector-search-python/requirements.txt (pymongo>=4.6.0); https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples/blob/main/ai/select-algorithm-typescript/package.json and .../ai/vector-search-typescript/package.json (mongodb ^6.18.0); .../ai/vector-search-agent-typescript/package.json (mongodb ^7.1.0); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-go (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27; `go get go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/v2/mongo`; links only mongodb.com docs, mongodb/mongo-go-driver and pkg.go.dev)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. 4.8-open Does Microsoft maintain Azure DocumentDB-specific tested driver floors per compatibility version (5.0-8.0), different from MongoDB's wire-protocol tables? Why open: No Learn page names a minimum or tested driver version per server version; the compatibility page and the FAQ (updated 2026-11-07) both say only that any wire-protocol driver connects, and MongoDB's tables describe MongoDB Server, not DocumentDB. Best guess: Filings use MongoDB's fully-compatible rows (PyMongo >= 4.9, Node >= 6.9, Java >= 5.2, .NET >= 2.29, Go >= 2.1, Rust >= 3.1, Mongoose >= 8.7 for 8.0) as the floor, recommend current stable, and ask Learn for a tested-versions table (L01).
2. 4.9-open Has Microsoft tested Node.js 24 against Azure DocumentDB, and with which driver line? Why open: Learn's Node pages require 'Node.js 22 or newer' or 'latest LTS' and state no tested Node or driver version; MongoDB marks only driver 7.x as compatible with Node 24. Best guess: Pair Node 24 with mongodb 7.x (which also requires Node >= 20.19.0); treat mongodb 6.x on Node 24 as untested; the experiment's own builds on v24.21.0 are observations, not a test matrix.
3. 4.10-open What versions were the LlamaIndex, .NET, Go and Rust quickstarts actually tested with, and which Java pin (5.2.0 or 5.4.0) is current? Why open: Those pages install unpinned packages and carry no 'Tested driver version' statement; the two Java pages disagree; the Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples pins (MongoDB.Driver 3.0.0/3.2.0, Go driver v1.17.6, pymongo >=4.6/4.7, mongodb ^6.18/^7.1) belong to different samples that the quickstarts do not link, and disagree with Learn on the Go major version. Best guess: Learn's newer Java page (5.4.0, updated 2026-11-23) over the quickstart's 5.2.0; for the others, filings propose the exact versions resolved at filing time and ask the page owners to confirm, and ask the samples repo to move Go to the v2 module Learn teaches.
4. 4.11-open Should any kit skill state driver version minimums at all? (maintainer decision) Why open: A policy question, not a fact; no source settles it. Best guess: State floors only where a feature is gated (MONGODB-OIDC: PyMongo 4.7, Node 6.7.0, Java 5.1, .NET 2.25; AsyncMongoClient GA: PyMongo 4.13; Beanie 2: PyMongo >= 4.11) and otherwise say 'resolve the current stable release and pin exactly', mirroring Learn's 'Tested driver version' sections. The observed cost of saying nothing was 13 runs failing on nonexistent npm versions and 5 runs failing on Motor/Beanie 1 stacks.
5. 4.12-open Does Azure DocumentDB accept MONGODB-OIDC from drivers whose docs say the mechanism needs 'MongoDB Atlas or MongoDB Enterprise Advanced ... v7.0 or later'? Why open: MongoDB's driver docs scope OIDC to Atlas/EA on 7.0+; Learn shows it working on DocumentDB but says nothing about the cluster compatibility version (5.0/6.0) needed for Entra ID. Best guess: Works on all DocumentDB versions because the gateway, not the engine, implements auth; filings do not assert this and ask Learn to state whether Entra ID needs a particular compatibility version.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (ms.date 2026-03-11, updated_at 2026-11-23): Shows MONGODB-OIDC code for Python, TypeScript and C# with no minimum driver version (PyMongo 4.7, mongodb 6.7.0, MongoDB.Driver 2.25, Java 5.1) and no Java sample; the Java callback exists only on how-to-build-java-console-app.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-python (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-08-25): Requires 'Python 3.12 or later' and installs `pip install pymongo` unpinned with no tested version and no sync/async guidance, while the Beanie and Django quickstarts (Nov 2026) require Python 3.13.x and pin pymongo==4.19.0 (which itself needs Python >= 3.11).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-java vs https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-java-console-app: The quickstart pins mongodb-driver-sync 5.2.0 (updated 2026-08-25); the console-app how-to pins 5.4.0 (updated 2026-11-23). Two Learn pages, two pins, neither marked as tested; the newer page is the console-app how-to.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-nodejs (ms.date 2026-03-25, updated_at 2026-04-27): Requires 'Node.js 22 or newer' and installs `npm install mongodb` unpinned; MongoDB lists only driver 7.x as compatible with Node 24, so a reader on Node 24 with a 6.x lockfile is in an untested combination. No tested driver version is stated.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-knowledge-base (ms.date 2026-05-06, updated_at 2026-11-25): The install command is unpinned although the behaviour described is specific to llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo 0.9.0 (pymongo >=4.6.1,<5; llama-index-core >=0.13,<0.15); no Python prerequisite and no tested versions.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-dotnet, quickstart-go, quickstart-rust: Driver installs are unpinned (`dotnet add package MongoDB.Driver`, `go get .../v2/mongo`, `cargo add mongodb`) with no 'Tested driver version' section like the Beanie and Django quickstarts have.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-go (updated_at 2026-04-27) vs https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples ai/*-go/go.mod (HEAD c7ce4ac, 2026-11-24): Learn teaches the Go driver v2 module (`go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/v2/mongo`); all three Go samples in Microsoft's own samples repo pin the v1 module (`go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver v1.17.6`), whose import paths and `mongo.Connect` signature differ. Neither side says which line Microsoft tests against DocumentDB. The same repo also pins MongoDB.Driver 3.0.0 and 3.2.0, pymongo >=4.6.0 and >=4.7.0, and mongodb ^6.18.0 and ^7.1.0 across sibling samples, and one requirements.txt comment still reads 'for connecting to Cosmos DB'.
- https://www.mongodb.com/docs/languages/python/pymongo-driver/current/reference/migration/ vs https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/motor/ (MongoDB docs, supporting not settling): The migration guide says 'Motor will be deprecated on May 14th, 2026'; the Motor page says it was deprecated May 14, 2025 with end of life May 14, 2026 and final support May 14, 2027. Filings cite the Motor page and note the discrepancy.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-upgrade-cluster (ms.date 2024-07-22, updated_at 2026-04-27): Does not list which compatibility versions exist (5.0, 6.0, 7.0, 8.0) or upgrade paths; the version list appears in the compatibility page's table headers and, explicitly, in the FAQ (updated 2026-11-07: 'supports MongoDB wire protocol versions 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, and 8.0'), which the upgrade page could cite or repeat. Belongs with topic 11 but surfaced here.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/connection/SKILL.md lines 45-49 (clone commit 4f41c15, 2026-11-23)`: Says 'MongoDB announced Motor's deprecation in May 2024 ... Motor receives only critical fixes through May 2026 and is end-of-life after that'. Correct dates: deprecated May 14, 2025; end of life May 14, 2026; critical fixes until May 14, 2027. It also gives 'PyMongo 4.9+' for AsyncMongoClient, which is the beta release; GA is 4.13 and Beanie 2 requires >= 4.11.
- `documentdb-mcp/package.json line 38 (clone commit b967704, 2026-09-16)`: Declares `"mongodb": "^6.3.0"` while the README and tool_list.md recommend `authMode=entra` (MONGODB-OIDC). The OIDC floor is 6.7.0; the declared range admits 6.3.0-6.6.x, which cannot authenticate with OIDC. The committed package-lock.json resolves 6.19.0, so it works today, but the range is wrong.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/compat/package.json line 14 and docs/installation/*.md ('node --version # must be v20.x or higher')`: The compatibility harness pins mongodb 6.20.0; MongoDB marks driver 6.x untested on Node 24, which is the runtime the experiment's environment supplied (v24.21.0). Either pin 7.x (needs Node >= 20.19.0) or state the Node range the compat project was run on.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/driver/SKILL.md line 9`: Claims patterns 'apply across Node.js, Python, Java, Go, and .NET' but no file in skills/ or rules/ states a driver version, an OIDC floor or the Beanie 2 pairing; the only version text is the connection skill's incorrect Motor note. Not a contradiction, a gap the K07/L01 filings fill.

## Microsoft Entra ID specifics (SME topic 5): researched 2026-11-25, 10 settled, 2 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Must readWriteAnyDatabase and clusterAdmin be granted together for non-admin Entra identities, or are they separate roles? **engine_source.** Together, per the current Learn RBAC page and the engine as shipped. Learn (how-to-connect-role-based-access-control, ms.date 2026-03-11, updated_at 2026-11-23): "You can't assign readWriteAnyDatabase and clusterAdmin roles separately - they must be granted together for full read-write access." and, in the secondary-identity procedure, "Both roles are required together; clusterAdmin in this pair doesn't grant user-management privileges." The engine enforces it: pg_documentdb/src/commands/users.c ValidateAndObtainUserRole() accepts exactly {clusterAdmin, readWriteAnyDatabase} -> ApiAdminRoleV2, {readAnyDatabase} -> ApiReadOnlyRole, and {readWriteAnyDatabase} alone only when IsReadWriteAnyDatabaseRoleAvailable() (GUC documentdb.enable_readwrite_any_database_role_enforcement plus a role OID, role_utils.c lines 903-908), whose compiled default is false (see the separate settled item on that flag); clusterAdmin alone or any other mix is rejected with "Roles specified are invalid. Only readAnyDatabase or readWriteAnyDatabase+clusterAdmin are allowed built-in roles." (users.c lines 1795-1970). Filings should say: grant both; readAnyDatabase is the only standalone built-in. Caveat: the secondary-users page (updated 2026-11-17, older) already describes readWriteAnyDatabase and clusterAdmin as individually assignable; see doc_fixes and the open item. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (sections 'Manage DocumentDB administrative Microsoft Entra ID identities and native users' and 'Manage DocumentDB secondary (nonadministrative) Microsoft Entra ID identities'); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/users.c (ValidateAndObtainUserRole, comment block 'Accepted built-in role combinations' and switch on userRoles); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/utils/role_utils.c (IsReadWriteAnyDatabaseRoleAvailable)
- What is the open-source engine's default for the standalone readWriteAnyDatabase role (flag EnableReadWriteAnyDatabaseRoleEnforcement), and does Learn already describe the role as standalone? **engine_source.** Engine default: off, with an announced plan to turn it on. pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c lines 114-117: "/* Added in v0.116, Pending stabilization, enable on v1.3 */ #define DEFAULT_ENABLE_READWRITE_ANY_DATABASE_ROLE_ENFORCEMENT false"; the GUC is documentdb.enable_readwrite_any_database_role_enforcement, described (lines 1071-1078) as "Whether standalone readWriteAnyDatabase role assignment and collection table access are enabled." Separately, Learn's secondary-users page (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-17) already treats the role as standalone: the 'Choose an access scope' table reads "Read and write data across databases | Assign the readWriteAnyDatabase built-in role. | Cluster" and "Perform supported cluster administration | Assign the clusterAdmin built-in role. | Cluster", and an Important box says "For users assigned the readWriteAnyDatabase role, an extra step is currently required to apply the role privileges to all existing collections" (a validate/repair loop over each database's collections, which is the 'collection table access' half of the flag). This settles what the engine ships and what each Learn page says; whether Azure runs with the flag on remains open. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c (lines 114-117 and 1071-1078); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/secondary-users ('Choose an access scope' table and the Important box under 'Native roles'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (statement that the roles must be granted together, updated_at 2026-11-23)
- Accepted --type values for az documentdb mongocluster microsoft-entra-user assign (User, ServicePrincipal, managed identity?) **documented.** Exactly two values. CLI reference (documentdb extension, requires azure-cli 2.75.0+, group in preview; ms.date 2026-09-01, updated_at 2026-10-06): "--type -t  The Microsoft Entra principal type of the user. Allowed values: User, ServicePrincipal." There is no managed-identity value; the Learn RBAC page states for the ARM surfaces that you "use principal type user for a human identity or servicePrincipal for a service principal or managed identity", and for a user-assigned managed identity to pass `az identity show ... --query principalId` as the object ID. So a managed identity is registered with --type ServicePrincipal and its principalId (see the next item for the source chain). The Learn CLI example only shows --type User with --role db=admin role=root; the assign command is described as "an idempotent upsert". Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster/microsoft-entra-user#az-documentdb-mongocluster-microsoft-entra-user-assign; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (CLI pivot of 'Manage DocumentDB administrative Microsoft Entra ID identities' and the object-ID paragraph above it)
- Is the CLI's --type ServicePrincipal the documented way to register a managed identity, and does the CLI accept the identity's principalId as --object-id? **documented.** Yes, by composition of the CLI extension source and the Learn ARM rule. The extension is a thin aaz wrapper over the ARM users child resource: src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/custom.py `_add_principal_type_arg` adds the flat flag `options=["--type", "-t"]` with `enum={"User": "user", "ServicePrincipal": "servicePrincipal"}` and `_build_identity_provider` copies it into `args.identity_provider.microsoft_entra_id.principal_type` (the docstring: "Map the flat --type value into identityProvider.microsoftEntraID.principalType"); the generated aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/entra_user/_assign.py sends that as the required body property `principalType` (enum servicePrincipal|user) and serializes `--object-id` (options -n/--name/--object-id, id_part child_name_1) as the URL path parameter `userName`, i.e. the users/{principalId} segment the REST, Bicep and Terraform pivots also use. Learn's RBAC page (updated_at 2026-11-23) gives the rule for that resource: "Use the returned value as the principal ID in the registration examples, not the application/client ID", with `az identity show ... --query principalId` for a user-assigned managed identity and "get the identity.principalId of the Azure resource that owns it" for a system-assigned one, and "use principal type user for a human identity or servicePrincipal for a service principal or managed identity". Hence: `--type ServicePrincipal --object-id <managed identity principalId>`. Remaining defect is only the CLI help text 'Object ID (client ID)' (doc_fixes). Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/custom.py (_add_principal_type_arg, _build_identity_provider, class UserAssign); https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/entra_user/_assign.py (object_id arg lines 63-69, principal_type lines 96-101, url param userName); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (paragraph 'Before registering an administrator, get the principal's object ID ...' and 'In the REST, Bicep, and Terraform examples, use principal type user ... or servicePrincipal for a service principal or managed identity'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster/microsoft-entra-user#az-documentdb-mongocluster-microsoft-entra-user-assign
- ALLOWED_HOSTS for the OIDC callback: *.azure.com (Learn) or *.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com (MCP repo)? **engine_source.** Both work; the MCP value is narrower and preferable, the Learn value is not wrong. Learn's TypeScript sample on the RBAC page and both JS/TS pivots of how-to-build-nodejs-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-11-23) set ALLOWED_HOSTS: ['*.azure.com']; the MCP README (line 216), docs/design-doc.md and the kit README set allowedHosts ['*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com'], which src/context/documentdb.ts passes through as ALLOWED_HOSTS. Node driver 7.9.1 (src/mongo_client.ts lines ~695-711) validates ALLOWED_HOSTS after SRV resolution for every MONGODB-OIDC credential that has no ENVIRONMENT property, i.e. for custom OIDC_CALLBACK and OIDC_HUMAN_CALLBACK alike, and the default list (src/cmap/auth/mongo_credentials.ts DEFAULT_ALLOWED_HOSTS: *.mongodb.net, *.mongodb-dev.net, *.mongodb-qa.net, *.mongodbgov.net, *.mongo.com, localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1 on main; the spec changelog added the -qa and mongo.com entries on 2025-11-19) contains no Azure domain, so an Azure host without ALLOWED_HOSTS fails with MongoInvalidArgumentError "Host '...' is not valid for OIDC authentication with ALLOWED_HOSTS of ...". Matching (src/utils.ts hostMatchesWildcards): a '*.' pattern matches host === suffix or host.endsWith('.' + suffix), so nested subdomains match and both patterns cover <cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com and <cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com. The driver spec says ALLOWED_HOSTS is a security feature required only for the human flow; pymongo enforces it only when human_callback is set (pymongo/auth_oidc_shared.py line 208), which is why Learn's Python sample needs none. With ENVIRONMENT:azure the Node check is skipped. Observation: the runs' node_modules (mongodb 5.9.2, e.g. runs/16-subscription-billing-engine/20261124-192251/app) carry the same Azure-less DEFAULT_ALLOWED_HOSTS; no run attempted an OIDC connection. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control ('Connect using Microsoft Entra ID in code', TypeScript tab); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-nodejs-console-app (step 5 of 'Connect to the cluster', both pivots); https://github.com/mongodb/node-mongodb-native/blob/main/src/mongo_client.ts (comment 'It is important to perform validation of hosts AFTER SRV resolution' and the isServiceAuth check); https://github.com/mongodb/node-mongodb-native/blob/main/src/cmap/auth/mongo_credentials.ts (DEFAULT_ALLOWED_HOSTS); https://github.com/mongodb/node-mongodb-native/blob/main/src/utils.ts (hostMatchesWildcards); https://github.com/mongodb/specifications/blob/master/source/auth/auth.md (ALLOWED_HOSTS property and changelog); https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-python-driver/blob/master/pymongo/auth_oidc_shared.py (_get_authenticator); /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/README.md:216; /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/src/context/documentdb.ts:37; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/16-subscription-billing-engine/20261124-192251/app/node_modules/mongodb/lib/cmap/auth/mongo_credentials.js (observation)
- Terraform: is authConfig.allowedModes exposed in azurerm_mongo_cluster 4.x, or is azapi the route? **documented.** azurerm exposes it. azurerm_mongo_cluster has `authentication_methods` (allowed values NativeAuth and MicrosoftEntraID; "administrator_username is required when authentication_methods contains NativeAuth or is not configured"), added in azurerm 4.55.0 (December 04, 2025, #31100; renamed from auth_config_allowed_modes to authentication_methods in #31209, merged 2025-11-28, before release). The same 4.55.0 release added the resource azurerm_mongo_cluster_user (#31205) for Entra administrator registration (identity_provider_type MicrosoftEntraID, principal_type user|servicePrincipal, role name only root). azurerm 5.2.0 made administrator_password optional for Entra-only clusters (#32092); 5.4.0 moved the resource to API 2026-06-01 (#33195); current release is 5.15.0 (2026-11-23). ARM/Bicep: Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters properties.authConfig.allowedModes is a string array of 'MicrosoftEntraID' | 'NativeAuth' (latest API 2026-06-15-preview; GA 2026-06-01). azapi is therefore optional, not required, for both the cluster setting and the admin-user registration; the Learn page's Terraform pivot nevertheless uses azapi for the user and a non-existent `authentication_enabled = true` argument for the cluster (see doc_fixes). Sources: https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/azurerm/latest/docs/resources/mongo_cluster (fetched from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/main/website/docs/r/mongo_cluster.html.markdown); https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/azurerm/latest/docs/resources/mongo_cluster_user (fetched from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/main/website/docs/r/mongo_cluster_user.html.markdown); https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/CHANGELOG-v4.md (## 4.55.0 entries for #31100 and #31205); https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md (## 5.2.0 #32092; ## 5.4.0 #33195; ## 5.15.0); https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/pull/31209; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters (AuthConfigProperties; updated_at 2026-10-20)
- Is there a verified Java OIDC callback? **documented.** Yes, published by Microsoft on Learn (not exercised by this experiment). how-to-build-java-console-app (ms.date 2025-07-21, updated_at 2026-11-23) implements MongoCredential.OidcCallback whose onRequest() calls DefaultAzureCredential.getTokenSync() with scope https://ossrdbms-aad.database.windows.net/.default and returns `new OidcCallbackResult(token)` (no expiry argument), then `MongoCredential.createOidcCredential(null).withMechanismProperty("OIDC_CALLBACK", oidcCallback)`, srvHost `<cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`, SSL enabled, retryWrites(false), connectTimeout 2 minutes; dependencies mongodb-driver-sync 5.4.0 and azure-identity 1.15.4. No ALLOWED_HOSTS is set (the Java driver page documents none for custom callbacks). Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples ai/vector-search-java (IVF.java, HNSW.java, DiskAnn.java) also connects with authMechanism=MONGODB-OIDC. The RBAC page itself only has Python, TypeScript and C# tabs (header search.mshattr.devlang: python typescript csharp). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-java-console-app ('Connect to the cluster' steps 2-5); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-java-vector-search; https://github.com/Azure-Samples/documentdb-samples/tree/main/ai/vector-search-java/src/main/java/com/azure/documentdb/samples; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/java/sync/current/security/auth/oidc/
- Learn's TypeScript sample appears to mix seconds and milliseconds in expiresInSeconds; is it wrong? **arithmetic_or_definition.** Yes. The TypeScript sample on the RBAC page and the TS pivot of how-to-build-nodejs-console-app compute `expiresInSeconds: (tokenResponse?.expiresOnTimestamp || 0) - Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)`. @azure/core-auth documents AccessToken.expiresOnTimestamp as "The access token's expiration timestamp in milliseconds, UNIX epoch time", and the Node driver documents OIDCResponse.expiresInSeconds as "The number of seconds until the access token expires"; subtracting epoch seconds from epoch milliseconds yields roughly 1.7e12 'seconds'. The correct form is in the JavaScript pivot of the same console-app page, `Math.floor((tokenResponse.expiresOnTimestamp - Date.now()) / 1000)`, and in the MCP server (src/context/documentdb.ts line 44, with Math.max(0, ...)). Runtime impact in Node driver 7.9.1 is nil today because TokenCache stores expiresInSeconds but never reads it (src/cmap/auth/mongodb_oidc/token_cache.ts); it is still a contract violation and would matter if the driver started honouring expiry. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (TypeScript tab under 'Connect using Microsoft Entra ID in code'); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-nodejs-console-app (TS pivot step 2 vs JS pivot step 2); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/javascript/api/@azure/core-auth/accesstoken (expiresOnTimestamp); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/node/current/security/authentication/oidc/ (OIDCResponse properties); https://github.com/mongodb/node-mongodb-native/blob/main/src/cmap/auth/mongodb_oidc/token_cache.ts; /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/src/context/documentdb.ts:44
- Can a non-admin user run connectionStatus with showPrivileges and usersInfo on itself? **engine_source.** connectionStatus: yes, per engine source and Azure's release notes. pg_documentdb/src/commands/users.c connection_status() resolves the caller with GetUserId(), performs no privilege check, and returns authInfo.authenticatedUsers, authenticatedUserRoles and, when showPrivileges is true, authenticatedUserPrivileges (lines 1440-1550); the gateway exposes it behind the dynamic setting enableConnectionStatus whose default is true (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs lines 121-123). Azure's Learn release notes (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated_at 2026-06-02), 'August 29, 2025' > 'Engine Enhancements (0829)': "Enhanced usersInfo and connectionStatus commands to return all supported roles (ReadWrite, UserAdmin, Root) and privilege sets (dbAdmin, userAdmin, clusterMonitor, clusterManager, hostManager)." so both commands are documented as present on the Azure service. Engine CHANGELOG v1.2-0 (2026-11-12): "Preserve backward-compatible connectionStatus role reporting ... and emit each client-facing role once." usersInfo: Learn's permissions table gives Entra and native secondary users (read-write and read-only) ListUser = yes, and the engine's usersInfo accepts a named user; `showPrivileges` is rejected with `usersInfo: 1` ("The 'showPrivileges' option is not supported when 'usersInfo' is set to 1.", users.c line 1258) and for a named user depends on EnableUsersInfoPrivileges, whose compiled default is true (pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c lines 98-100: "/* Added in v0.108, enabled in v0.108, remove after v1.6 */ #define DEFAULT_ENABLE_USERS_INFO_PRIVILEGES true"). None of the experiment's runs called either command. Correction to the earlier draft: a Learn page (the release notes) does document connectionStatus for Azure DocumentDB. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/users.c (connection_status, command_users_info); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs (enableConnectionStatus); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c (lines 98-100, DEFAULT_ENABLE_USERS_INFO_PRIVILEGES); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes ('August 29, 2025', Engine Enhancements (0829)); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md (v1.2-0 entries on connectionStatus/usersInfo); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (table 'Security provider | Role | CreateUser | DeleteUser | UpdateUser | ListUser')
- Are custom roles still preview? **documented.** Yes. how-to-configure-role-based-access-control (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-17), Important box: "Custom roles and fine-grained, database- and collection-level privileges are currently in preview." The secondary-users page (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-17) has a 'Preview limitations' section: updateRole unsupported (use grantPrivilegesToRole/revokePrivilegesFromRole), rolesInfo returns no inheritedRoles/inheritedPrivileges, custom-to-custom inheritance unsupported, only find/insert/update/delete actions. The engine gates createRole/dropRole/rolesInfo behind `documentdb.enableRoleCrud` (CHANGELOG v1.0-0, 2026-10-19: "Guarded by documentdb.enableRoleCrud feature flag, disabled by default"; feature_flag_configs.c lines 102-104: DEFAULT_ENABLE_ROLE_CRUD false, "pending stabilization, enable in v1.5"). Custom roles can be assigned to Entra users too: "you can assign the role to a native user or a Microsoft Entra ID user." Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-configure-role-based-access-control; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/secondary-users ('Custom roles', 'Preview limitations'); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md (v1.0-0 createRole/dropRole/rolesInfo entry); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c (lines 102-104)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Does the Azure DocumentDB service run with the engine's standalone readWriteAnyDatabase flag (documentdb.enable_readwrite_any_database_role_enforcement) on, making the RBAC page's 'must be granted together' statement obsolete, or off, making the secondary-users page's standalone table wrong? Why open: The engine's compiled default is false with the comment 'enable on v1.3', and Azure does not publish which engine flags its clusters run with; the two current Learn pages disagree (RBAC page, updated 2026-11-23: pair mandatory; secondary-users, updated 2026-11-17: readWriteAnyDatabase and clusterAdmin each assignable alone, plus a validate/repair step that only makes sense for the standalone role), so no primary source settles the Azure behaviour. Best guess: Azure is at or past the engine's v1.3 line (CHANGELOG already shows v1.2-0 on 2026-11-12) and the secondary-users page was written against the flag-on behaviour; filings nevertheless follow the newer RBAC page and grant both roles, which is accepted in either state, and raise the inconsistency as a doc fix plus the engineering question of the flag's value on Azure.
2. On Azure, does usersInfo {usersInfo: '<self>', showPrivileges: true} return privileges when the caller is a non-admin (read-write or read-only) user? Why open: The engine default for EnableUsersInfoPrivileges is true and Azure's release notes document usersInfo returning privilege sets, but neither source states the non-admin-caller case (users.c runs usersInfo under the caller's PostgreSQL role, and the Learn table only says ListUser = yes), and no run in this experiment issued usersInfo or connectionStatus. Best guess: Roles are returned for self; privileges most likely too since the flag defaults on. Filings should recommend connectionStatus {showPrivileges: true} as the self-check (no privilege check in the engine, documented on Azure) and treat usersInfo privileges as a nice-to-have.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (TypeScript tab) and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-build-nodejs-console-app (TypeScript pivot): expiresInSeconds is computed as expiresOnTimestamp (milliseconds) minus Math.floor(Date.now()/1000) (seconds). Should be Math.floor((expiresOnTimestamp - Date.now()) / 1000) as in the JavaScript pivot of the console-app page.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control (Terraform pivot, 'Enable Microsoft Entra ID authentication'): The azurerm_mongo_cluster example sets `authentication_enabled = true`, which is not an argument of the resource; the correct argument is `authentication_methods = ["NativeAuth", "MicrosoftEntraID"]` (azurerm >= 4.55.0). The user-registration Terraform pivot uses azapi although azurerm_mongo_cluster_user exists since 4.55.0.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control ('Enable Microsoft Entra ID' overview paragraph): Self-contradictory sentence: "A cluster must have native authentication enabled; the supported configurations are native-only or Microsoft Entra ID authentication only or native and Microsoft Entra ID authentication." Entra-only is listed as supported while native is said to be mandatory.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control ('Connect using Microsoft Entra ID in code'): Only Python, TypeScript and C# tabs; no link to the Java, Go or Rust console-app pages. The TypeScript tab sets ALLOWED_HOSTS without explaining that the Node driver rejects Azure hosts for custom OIDC callbacks by default while pymongo does not check ALLOWED_HOSTS for machine callbacks.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster/microsoft-entra-user: --object-id help reads "Object ID (client ID) of the Microsoft Entra principal"; the Learn RBAC page says to use the object ID (principalId), "not the application/client ID", and the CLI extension sends the value as the ARM users/{userName} path segment. The --type help should state that managed identities use ServicePrincipal with the identity's principalId, as the RBAC page states for the REST/Bicep/Terraform pivots.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/secondary-users vs https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control: secondary-users (updated 2026-11-17) lists clusterAdmin as a separately assignable role in its 'Choose an access scope' table ("Perform supported cluster administration | Assign the clusterAdmin built-in role") and readWriteAnyDatabase as standalone ("Read and write data across databases | Assign the readWriteAnyDatabase built-in role", plus an Important box requiring a validate/repair loop for users assigned readWriteAnyDatabase), and gives readWriteAnyDatabase and clusterAdmin the identical description "Full read-write permissions, including database management"; the RBAC page (updated 2026-11-23, newer) and the engine's shipped default say clusterAdmin cannot be assigned alone and must be paired with readWriteAnyDatabase. The engine carries a flag (default off, 'enable on v1.3') for exactly the standalone behaviour the secondary-users page describes. Documentation inconsistency; engineering question: which reflects the Azure service today.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations ('Native DocumentDB secondary users', ms.date 2026-11-04, updated 2026-11-17): Stale against the same-day how-to-configure-role-based-access-control and secondary-users pages: it says "Assigning roles to specific databases or collections isn't supported, only cluster level is supported", "The Roleinfo command isn't supported" and "The Updateuser command now only supports password updates", while the newer pages document database- and collection-scoped custom roles (preview), rolesInfo with showPrivileges, and updateUser with customData.validUntil.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/secondary-users: Says "ARM REST APIs, ARM-based Azure CLI commands, Bicep, and Terraform don't support secondary-user role assignments" without noting that the azurerm_mongo_cluster_user resource and az documentdb mongocluster microsoft-entra-user exist for administrative (root) Entra registration; readers conflate the two.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes ('August 29, 2025') vs https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/secondary-users: The release notes describe usersInfo/connectionStatus returning roles named ReadWrite, UserAdmin, Root and privilege sets dbAdmin, userAdmin, clusterMonitor, clusterManager, hostManager, while the RBAC and secondary-users pages use readWriteAnyDatabase, clusterAdmin, readAnyDatabase and root; no Learn page documents connectionStatus itself or maps the two vocabularies.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-agent-kit/skills/security/security-entra-rbac.md:36-43`: The 'Correct' SCRAM example creates a user with roles [{ role: "readWrite", db: "orders" }]. readWrite is not an accepted built-in role on Azure DocumentDB; the engine rejects it ("Roles specified are invalid. Only readAnyDatabase or readWriteAnyDatabase+clusterAdmin are allowed built-in roles.") and Learn lists only readAnyDatabase, readWriteAnyDatabase+clusterAdmin, root, or a preview custom role created with createRole.
- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-agent-kit/skills/security/security-entra-rbac.md:13`: Example host `prod-ddb.mongocluster.documentdb.azure.com` uses a non-existent domain; Learn hosts are `<cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com` or `<cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com`.
- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-agent-kit/skills/security/security-entra-rbac.md:22-30`: The Node Entra example is a placeholder: authMechanism 'MONGODB-OIDC' with a comment "or the current documented mechanism" and "// credentialProvider: Azure Managed Identity"; it lacks the OIDC_CALLBACK (or ENVIRONMENT:azure,TOKEN_RESOURCE), ALLOWED_HOSTS for Azure hosts, retryWrites:false and the mongodb+srv host, all of which Learn's samples carry.
- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/src/security/connectionProfiles.ts:49 and src/security/configValidation.ts:137`: allowedHosts is optional for authMode 'entra' profiles and passes through undefined. With the Node driver, an Entra profile that omits allowedHosts falls back to DEFAULT_ALLOWED_HOSTS (no Azure domains) and every connection to an Azure DocumentDB host fails with MongoInvalidArgumentError "Host ... is not valid for OIDC authentication with ALLOWED_HOSTS of ..."; validation should require allowedHosts or default it to ['*.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com'].
- `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-agent-kit/skills/azure-deployment/references/terraform-cluster-template.md`: The Terraform template never sets authentication_methods, so Entra ID cannot be enabled from the kit's IaC path even though azurerm has supported it since 4.55.0; the security skill tells users to prefer Entra.
