## Change streams (SME topic 8): researched 2026-11-25, 7 settled, 6 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Is the active change log 400 MB, and is that where resumeAfter stops working? **documented.** 400 MB is documented. The change-streams page (ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-17) says: "Standard change streams are limited to events retained within the active 400-MB change log. Once rotated, older entries are archived and become inaccessible. Historical change streams remove this limitation by transparently retrieving archived logs, enabling stream replay or resumption from past timestamps." The second half of the question is answered differently from how it is posed: the page does not say resumeAfter stops at 400 MB. Its Important box says "Change streams support resumability through the startAt, resumeAfter, and startAtOperationTime parameters. With point-in-time restore (PITR) log integration, stream recovery can extend up to 35 days or the cluster initialization point, whichever is earlier." So the 400 MB boundary is where the active log ends and historical (archived-log) retrieval begins, not where resumption fails. Filings should say: resume within the active 400 MB log is cheap; beyond it the service transparently reads archived logs (more resource use, run in low-traffic periods, keep extra storage on clusters of 128 GB or less). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams (sections 'Recovering Streams from Historical checkpoints' and the Important box under it)
- Do historical streams reach back up to 35 days through point-in-time restore? **documented.** Yes, with a ceiling: "With point-in-time restore (PITR) log integration, stream recovery can extend up to 35 days or the cluster initialization point, whichever is earlier." The 35-day figure matches backup retention on the restore page (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated 2026-10-29): "Backups are retained for 35 days for active clusters, 7 days for active burstable tier (M10, M20, M25) clusters, and 7 days for deleted clusters." The change-streams page also says "Pre-image isn't supported for historical processing of logs, since the system didn't track the extra pre-image information." See the open item on whether the historical window is 7 days on burstable tiers. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams (Important box under 'Recovering Streams from Historical checkpoints'; Limitations list); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-restore-cluster ('Backups' section)
- Can multishard change streams still not be enabled together with the multishard rebalancer? **documented.** Correct, still mutually exclusive as of the 2026-11-16 page. Step 5 of 'Enable change streams from the Azure portal' carries an Important box: "You can't enable change streams for multishard clusters while Rebalancer for multishard clusters is enabled. Before enabling multishard change streams, verify that Rebalancer for multishard clusters is turned off for the cluster." The rebalancer itself is a preview Features-page toggle: how-to-scale-cluster (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated 2026-11-05) says "In preview, data rebalancing needs to be enabled on cluster" via Settings > Features > 'Rebalancer for multishard clusters' > Enable, then sh.startBalancer() / sh.isBalancerRunning() / sh.stopBalancer() from the shell. Neither page says what happens to an existing multishard change stream if the rebalancer is later enabled; the only related limitation is "Cluster topology transition from single-shard to multi-shard breaks change stream resumability." Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams ('Multi-shard change streams (preview)', step 5 Important box; Limitations); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster ('Rebalance data')
- Are multishard change streams and updateDescription still preview? **documented.** Yes, both. The page has the headings "Multi-shard change streams (preview)" and "updateDescription for field-level changes in change streams (preview)". updateDescription is enabled per collection: db.runCommand({ collMod: "exampleCollection", enableUpdateDescription: { enabled: true } }). Pre-images (fullDocumentBeforeChange via collMod changeStreamPreAndPostImages) carry no preview label on the same page. Note a doc inconsistency: the 'Update' tab under 'Monitoring database changes' contains an earlier, unlabelled 'Use updateDescription to process field-level changes' sub-section that shows updateDescription in the event without mentioning preview status or the collMod enablement; the later preview section repeats the same text and adds the enablement step. Filings should treat updateDescription as preview and opt-in per collection. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams (headings 'Multi-shard change streams (preview)' and 'updateDescription for field-level changes in change streams (preview)'; 'Enable updateDescription on the collection')
- Do single-shard clusters need no enablement step? **observed_in_runs.** No enablement step is documented for single-shard clusters, and the experiment observed a change stream working without one. The change-streams page 'Get started' opens collection.watch() directly in six languages with no prerequisite; the only enablement procedure on the page is under 'Multi-shard change streams (preview)' and is scoped to multishard clusters. Observation: run 07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018 ran app/scripts/demo_change_stream.py (Motor/PyMongo, coll.watch(full_document='updateLookup')), inserted a document, and the build log records 'change stream observed the inserted document (printed doc)' and 'Change stream saw: {...}' against the experiment cluster (M30 per the experiment's setup notes) with no portal or CLI feature toggle. Caveat: a second run, 02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233, timed out waiting for an event from scripts/watch_once.js (Node driver); that script inserts immediately after calling watch() without awaiting cursor initialisation, so the timeout is consistent with a client-side race and is not evidence of a service-side enablement requirement. The GA-versus-preview status of single-shard change streams is a separate open item below. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams ('Get started'; 'Multi-shard change streams (preview)'); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018/03-build.md (observation: demo_change_stream.py received the insert event); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018/app/scripts/demo_change_stream.py; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233/03-build.md (observation: watch_once.js timed out; see caveat); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233/app/scripts/watch_once.js
- Is there a CLI, ARM or Bicep property for the multishard toggle? (what exists) **documented.** The property that carries cluster feature toggles exists in every surface, but no primary source names the string for multishard change streams (see open item). ARM/Bicep: Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters properties.previewFeatures, 'String array containing any of: GeoReplicas' in the latest and 2026-06-15-preview reference pages (updated 2026-10-20); the REST spec for stable/2026-06-01, stable/2025-09-01 and preview/2026-06-15-preview defines PreviewFeature with enum ["GeoReplicas"] and x-ms-enum modelAsString: true, so arbitrary strings are accepted. Azure CLI: az documentdb mongocluster create/update --preview-features, "Space-separated list of preview features to enable on the cluster. Preview feature names are free-form strings; refer to the mongo cluster feature documentation for the currently available values." (Learn CLI reference ms.date 2026-09-01, updated 2026-11-17; identical text in the locally installed extension's --help). Terraform azurerm_mongo_cluster: preview_features, "(Optional) The preview features that can be enabled on the MongoDB Cluster. Changing this forces a new resource to be created." (provider docs source, API 2026-06-01). Learn documents the pattern for another feature only: the full-text-search overview enables the preview with az rest PATCH adding "FullTextSearch" to properties.previewFeatures, and AzAPI/Bicep fragments doing the same, and tells azurerm users to put preview_features in lifecycle.ignore_changes when AzAPI owns it. The AzAPI sample embedded in the ARM reference page uses previewFeatures = ["ShardRebalancer"]. The change-streams page itself gives portal steps only (Settings > Features > 'Change streams for multishard clusters' > Enable). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters (MongoClusterProperties table, previewFeatures row; Terraform sample with ShardRebalancer); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/2026-06-15-preview/mongoclusters; https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/2026-06-01/mongoCluster.json (definitions.PreviewFeature); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest (--preview-features on create and update); https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/website/docs/r/mongo_cluster.html.markdown (preview_features argument); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/full-text-search-overview ('Enable full-text search': Azure CLI, Terraform, Bicep); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams ('Enable change streams from the Azure portal')
- Is fullDocument: 'updateLookup' supported on all tiers? **documented.** No tier restriction is documented anywhere, and the option is listed as supported. The $changeStream operator page (ms.date 2025-12-30, updated 2026-01-28) lists fullDocument options 'default', 'updateLookup', 'whenAvailable', 'required' and its Example 1 uses fullDocument: "updateLookup". The change-streams page's C# sample sets FullDocument = ChangeStreamFullDocumentOption.UpdateLookup. The limitations page (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated 2026-11-17) has no change-stream entry under any tier section, and the free-tier page (ms.date 2026-05-18) lists only HA, Microsoft Entra ID, backup/restore, HNSW and DiskANN indexes, and diagnostic logging as unsupported while claiming "command and feature parity". Observation: run 07-lab-results-aggregator opened watch(full_document='updateLookup') on the experiment's M30 cluster and received an insert event; the update path of updateLookup was not exercised by any run. Settled as 'no documented tier restriction', not as a positive per-tier test. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/operators/aggregation/$changestream (canonical URL; the /azure/documentdb/operators/aggregation/$changestream path redirects here) (Parameters table; Example 1); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams (C# tab); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/free-tier ('Restrictions'); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018/03-build.md (observation)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. What error (code and message) does the service return for a resume token that can no longer be resumed, for example one older than both the active 400 MB log and the 35-day historical window? Why open: No Learn page documents any change-stream error. The open-source engine repository's code tables list ChangeStreamBadResumeToken (pg_documentdb_core/include/utils/documentdb_codes.txt line 325, M008Z; mapped to MongoDB code 40647 in error_mappings.csv at the repository root, line 377) but a code search finds it only in the tables, never raised, and the OSS engine has no change-stream implementation (issue documentdb/documentdb#50 is still open, last maintainer status 2026-06-24 'targeted for Q3 2026'; PR #392 was a closed, unmerged proof of concept). MongoDB's ChangeStreamHistoryLost (286) does not appear in the engine's mapping tables. No experiment run exercised an expired token. The only non-table reference is the code-conversion switch in pg_documentdb/src/utils/documentdb_conversion_codes.c, so nothing raises it. Best guess: Filings should tell agents to handle any non-resumable error by re-opening with startAtOperationTime (historical replay) or a fresh stream, and should treat the error code as undocumented rather than asserting 286 or 40647. Ask Learn to document the error next to the resume section.
2. What is the exact previewFeatures string (ARM/Bicep/REST/CLI/Terraform) that enables 'Change streams for multishard clusters', and is non-portal enablement supported at all? Why open: The change-streams page documents portal enablement only. The REST spec enum lists only GeoReplicas (modelAsString, so other strings pass); Learn uses 'FullTextSearch' for full-text search and 'ShardRebalancer' in an AzAPI sample for the rebalancer, which shows the mechanism, but no primary source names the change-streams value. The CLI reference points to 'the mongo cluster feature documentation', and no Learn page lists the accepted values. Supporting-only material checked and rejected: Microsoft Q&A 5866569 (2026-04-21) has a single answer labelled 'AI answer' ('AI-generated content may be incorrect') that PATCHes previewFeatures to ["ChangeStreams"] with API 2024-10-01-preview and says a support request is needed if the cluster reports change streams unsupported; it never mentions multishard clusters and describes the pre-GA gated preview of the base feature, so it must not be cited. The 2026-06-02 DevBlogs post announcing multi-node change streams in public preview names no previewFeatures value or enablement path either. GitHub code search for MultiShardChangeStreams / MultishardChangeStreams returned nothing before the API rate limit. Best guess: By analogy with 'FullTextSearch' and 'ShardRebalancer' a value such as 'MultiShardChangeStreams' is likely, but filings must not assert it; they should document the portal path and the generic previewFeatures PATCH pattern, and ask Learn for the value list. Also warn Terraform users that azurerm preview_features forces cluster recreation (ForceNew), so use AzAPI azapi_update_resource plus lifecycle.ignore_changes as the full-text-search page recommends.
3. Are single-shard change streams generally available, or still preview? Why open: The current change-streams page labels only multishard change streams and updateDescription as preview, implying the base feature is not preview, but no Learn page states GA. The release notes (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated 2026-06-02) last mention change streams as 'Gated Preview' (2024-09-16) and '[Preview] Change stream support for Kafka Debezium connector & Pymongo driver' (2025-02-12), carry a stale note that preview items need a support request, and record no GA entry, no multishard, updateDescription, pre-image or historical-stream entries. The Azure Functions trigger page (ms.date 2025-11-19) lists 'Change stream feature enabled' as a prerequisite for any cluster. Supporting evidence (blog posts, not settling under this workflow's standard): Microsoft DevBlogs, 2025-05-21, Principal Program Manager on the product team, 'We're excited to announce the general availability (GA) of Change Streams' and 'With this GA release, Change Streams is ready for production workloads.' (no enablement step, no shard distinction; editor's note added November 2025 records the rename to Azure DocumentDB); and Microsoft DevBlogs, 2026-06-02, same author, states 'Change Streams reached General Availability', says 'change streams were supported on single-shard clusters only' until recently, and puts multi-node change streams 'now in Public Preview'. The second post does not label updateDescription or pre-images as preview, which differs from the current Learn page (updateDescription is labelled preview there). No Learn page records either statement. Best guess: Treat single-shard change streams as generally available since 2025-05-21 (DevBlogs GA announcement, cited as supporting evidence; no enablement step; no preview label on the current feature page; observed working on the experiment's M30 cluster) and ask the Learn release-notes page to record the 2025-05-21 GA entry and the later preview additions (multi-shard, updateDescription, pre-images, historical streams).
4. Is the historical change-stream window 35 days on every paid tier, or 7 days on burstable tiers (M10, M20, M25), and does it exist at all on the free tier? Why open: The change-streams page states 35 days flat and ties the window to PITR log integration; the restore page says backups are retained 7 days on burstable tiers, and the free-tier and limitations pages say backup/restore is not available on the free tier. Neither page reconciles these for historical change streams. The 2026-06-02 DevBlogs post describes the PITR integration as 'extending recovery to roughly the last month of activity', consistent with 35 days but with no tier qualifier either (supporting only). Best guess: Assume the historical window equals the cluster's backup retention (35 days M30+, 7 days M10-M25, none on free tier) and say so as an assumption, asking Learn to state it.
5. Does enabling the rebalancer on a cluster that already has multishard change streams enabled fail, disable the streams, or silently break resumability? Why open: Only one direction is documented (cannot enable multishard change streams while the rebalancer is enabled). The reverse order and the effect of a completed rebalance on existing resume tokens are not described on either the change-streams or the scale-cluster page. Best guess: Filings should tell agents to treat the two features as mutually exclusive in both directions and to re-open streams with startAtOperationTime after any topology or rebalance operation.
6. Does resumeAfter with a token that has rotated out of the active 400 MB log transparently fall back to archived logs, or must the client switch to startAtOperationTime? Why open: The page says historical streams work 'transparently' and that resumability is supported through startAt, resumeAfter and startAtOperationTime, but its only historical example uses startAtOperationTime; no run exercised a rotated token. Best guess: Read the page literally: resumeAfter works across the boundary with higher resource cost; recommend persisting both the resume token and the last processed operation time so either recovery path is available.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams: updateDescription is described twice: an unlabelled sub-section inside the 'Update' tab ('Use updateDescription to process field-level changes') shows it in a plain update event with no mention of preview status or enablement, while the later 'updateDescription for field-level changes in change streams (preview)' section says it is preview and requires collMod enableUpdateDescription. Readers of the first section will expect updateDescription without opting in.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams: Limitations bullet 'UpdateDescription isn't supported for update events within aggregation pipelines. However, update operators are supported.' is ambiguous (pipeline-style updates vs operator updates; whether $match on updateDescription in the watch pipeline is meant). Also 'Change streams don't support showExpandedEvents' contradicts the $changeStream operator page (canonical URL https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/operators/aggregation/$changestream), which lists showExpandedEvents and allChangesForCluster as accepted parameters without a caveat.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams: Multishard enablement is portal-only; no CLI, ARM/Bicep, REST or Terraform path and no previewFeatures value is given, unlike the full-text-search overview which documents all four. Should also state the behaviour when the rebalancer is enabled after change streams, and cross-link how-to-scale-cluster.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster: 'Rebalance data' documents the preview Rebalancer toggle but does not mention that it cannot coexist with multishard change streams (stated only on the change-streams page). The partitioning page (ms.date 2026-07-27) says logical shards 'are rebalanced across the cluster' after adding physical shards as if automatic, while this page says rebalancing is a manual, preview-gated step (sh.startBalancer()). Newer page (how-to-scale-cluster) should be treated as current; engineering question: is any automatic rebalance performed on shard add?
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes: ms.date 2025-11-20, updated 2026-06-02. Note 'Items tagged as [Preview] require a support request' is stale (features are self-serve on the portal Features page). Change streams last appear as 'Gated Preview' (2024-09-16) and '[Preview]' (2025-02-12) with no GA entry, and there are no entries for multishard change streams, updateDescription, pre-images, historical change streams or the rebalancer preview.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-functions/functions-bindings-documentdb-trigger: ms.date 2025-11-19. Prerequisite 'Change stream feature enabled' linking to the change-streams page implies every cluster needs an enablement step; the change-streams page documents enablement only for multishard clusters. Also states deletes are not supported by the trigger, which readers may generalise to change streams (the change-streams page documents delete events).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters (and 2026-06-15-preview, and the REST spec PreviewFeature definition): previewFeatures is described as 'List of private endpoint connections.' (copy-paste error) and its allowed values list only 'GeoReplicas', while Learn itself uses 'FullTextSearch' (full-text-search-overview) and 'ShardRebalancer' (AzAPI sample on this very page). The az CLI reference says 'refer to the mongo cluster feature documentation for the currently available values' but no Learn page lists them.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-restore-cluster vs https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams: Restore page: backups retained 35 days (M30+), 7 days (M10/M20/M25); change-streams page: historical recovery 'up to 35 days' with no tier qualifier. Clarify the historical window per tier and on the free tier (no backup).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language: ms.date 2025-11-05. $changeStream marked supported on all versions with no footnote that multishard change streams and updateDescription are preview and that showExpandedEvents is unsupported; no row for collMod options enableUpdateDescription / changeStreamPreAndPostImages.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes (cross-reference to Microsoft DevBlogs 2025-05-21 and 2026-06-02): Microsoft's own DevBlogs announced change streams GA on 2025-05-21 and multi-node change streams public preview, historical change streams, pre-images and updateDescription on 2026-06-02, but the Learn release-notes page records none of these; the blog is the only dated public record of the GA. Learn should carry the dates so filings can cite a primary source.

## Transactions, write semantics and bulk operations (SME topic 9): researched 2026-11-25, 14 settled, 8 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Are multi-document transactions supported across shards (logical and physical)? **documented.** Yes. The sharding concept page states: "the service doesn't limit transactions to the scope of a logical shard. Azure DocumentDB supports read and write transactions that are applicable across multiple logical shards and across multiple physical shards in the cluster." The Atlas comparison page row "Multi-document ACID transactions" reads "Yes ... Across collections and shards." The how-to page says Azure DocumentDB "implements these [ACID] principles". Observation caveat: every experiment run executed on a single-shard cluster, so cross-shard behaviour was not observed; the kit's sharding skill statement (cross-shard transactions supported) matches Learn. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/partitioning (ms.date 2026-07-27, updated_at 2026-08-25), section Logical shards, third bullet; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compare-mongodb-atlas (ms.date 2026-11-08, updated_at 2026-11-09), Compatibility and licensing table; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-transactions (ms.date 2023-08-28, updated_at 2026-11-18); documentdb-agent-kit clone skills/sharding/sharding-logical-vs-physical.md:45 and :88-90 (consistent with Learn)
- Is the transaction lifetime 30 seconds, and does the same limit apply on sharded clusters? **documented.** Yes, 30 seconds, stated once without any single-shard/multi-shard qualifier: limitations page, "MongoDB execution limitations": "Maximum transaction lifetime: 30 seconds." The engine gateway hard-codes the same default: TRANSACTION_TIMEOUT_DEFAULT_SEC = 30 (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs:26), and open issue documentdb/documentdb#756 (filed 2026-10-05) shows the value cannot currently be changed because the GUC is never registered ("the gateway always runs with the compiled defaults (30 s transaction, 120 s request)"). The how-to-transactions page does not mention the limit at all (doc gap, see doc_fixes). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17), MongoDB execution limitations; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs line 26; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/756
- Which operations are refused inside a transaction (the only documented per-transaction restriction)? **engine_source.** On the current main branch the gateway refuses eleven commands inside a multi-document transaction with code 263 OperationNotSupportedInTransaction, message "Cannot run '{command}' in a multi-document transaction.": RequestType::is_blocked_in_transaction (request_type.rs:470-484) lists ReIndex, CreateIndex, CreateIndexes, CreateSearchIndexes, DropIndexes, RenameCollection, ListCollections, Drop, CurrentOp, KillOp and MoveCollection; the unit test at request_type.rs:770-783 asserts the same eleven and asserts that find, insert and commitTransaction are not blocked. validation.rs:41-92 adds, with the same code 263: killCursors as the first statement of a transaction ("Cannot run command KillCursors at the start of a transaction"); aggregate/findAndModify/update/insert/count/distinct/find/getMore against the config, admin or local databases ("Cannot perform data operation against database {db} inside a transaction"); and those commands against system.profile or any system.* collection ("Cannot run command against system collections in transaction."). The Learn troubleshooting error-code table lists "263 | OperationNotSupportedInTransaction" with no list of affected commands. The engine's bulkWrite stub additionally errors "the bulkwrite procedure cannot be used in transactions." (bulk_write.c). The shorter four-command list (reIndex, createIndexes, dropIndexes, drop) reported in open issue #770 reflects release/1.0-RC2, not main; #770 also notes collMod and dropDatabase are wrongly accepted inside a transaction, and neither is in the main list either. No Learn page lists these restrictions (doc gap). Numeric caps on operations, documents or bytes per transaction are NOT documented anywhere; see open item. Caveat: which gateway build Azure runs is not published, so the list a client meets on Azure may be the RC2 one or the main one. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/request_type.rs lines 470-484 and 770-783 (fetched 2026-11-25); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/validation.rs lines 41-92; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-common-issues (ms.date 2025-06-02, updated_at 2026-04-27), Error Codes table; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/bulk_write.c; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/770
- Which writeConcern w values are honored, with and without HA? **engine_source.** writeConcern is validated by the gateway and then ignored by the engine; it never changes durability. Gateway write_concern.rs (module doc): "The value is validated but not retained: this deployment behaves as a standalone node, so any acknowledgement stronger than w: 1 is rejected at validation time". Accepted: w:0, w:1, and the string "majority" ("only the special 'majority' tag is accepted"). Rejected: numeric 2..=50 -> BadValue "w value {n} exceeds the number of data-bearing voting members"; numeric outside 0..=50, NaN, Inf, bool or array -> FailedToParse; any other string tag -> BadValue; unknown sub-field -> UnknownBsonField. Validation is on by default (enable_write_concern_validation default true, dynamic.rs:129-131). The engine lists "writeConcern" among IgnoredCommonSpecFields (commands_common.c:105, comment: insert, update, delete, createIndex, dropIndex). Durability is instead a cluster property: with HA, "Writes are acknowledged only after being successfully committed to both shards" (DR under the hood) / "Writes are persisted on both primary and standby shards before the service returns a response" (reliability page); without HA, each shard uses LRS storage with three synchronous copies. The insert command reference documents writeConcern only as "(Optional) A document expressing the write concern" with no value list. Defaults: getDefaultRWConcern is marked supported and setDefaultRWConcern unsupported on the compatibility page; what getDefaultRWConcern returns is settled separately below. Caveat: gateway behaviour is read from the open-source gateway; the Azure service runs the same gateway lineage (issue #339 was reproduced against the Azure service and matches the OSS code) but identity is not proven. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/write_concern.rs (module doc, validate, validate_w, check_w_range); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs lines 127-131; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c lines 89-105; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/availability-disaster-recovery-under-hood (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-10-29), In-region high availability; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb (ms.date 2026-09-21, updated_at 2026-11-23), Behavior when all zones are healthy; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/commands/query-and-write/insert (ms.date 2025-12-30, updated_at 2026-01-28); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-06-13), Administrative Commands rows getDefaultRWConcern / setDefaultRWConcern
- What does getDefaultRWConcern return? **engine_source.** A constant document. The gateway's process_get_rw_concern (processor/constant.rs:63-93, dispatched from process.rs:148) returns {defaultReadConcern: {level: "majority"}, defaultWriteConcern: {w: "majority", wtimeout: 0}, defaultReadConcernSource: "implicit", defaultWriteConcernSource: "implicit", ok: 1}. It is accepted only on the admin database (otherwise Unauthorized "Only the admin database can process getDefaultRWConcern."), and any top-level field other than getDefaultRWConcern, inMemory, comment, lsid or $db returns UnknownBsonField "Not a valid value for getDefaultRWConcern: {field}". The reported w: "majority" is itself validated-and-ignored per write_concern.rs, so the response describes what drivers may send, not what the engine applies. The compatibility page marks getDefaultRWConcern Yes and setDefaultRWConcern No; Learn does not show the response. Same caveat as the other gateway items: the Azure gateway is assumed to match the open-source gateway. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/constant.rs lines 63-93 (fetched 2026-11-25); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/process.rs line 148; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/write_concern.rs (module doc); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-06-13), Administrative Commands rows getDefaultRWConcern / setDefaultRWConcern
- Is writeConcern j (journal) honored? **engine_source.** Accepted and ignored. The gateway validates j (and legacy fsync) as a boolean-like flag: "j: non-boolean, non-numeric, non-null -> TypeMismatch" (write_concern.rs validate_flag); nothing downstream reads it, and the engine ignores the whole writeConcern document (commands_common.c:105). No Learn page mentions j. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/write_concern.rs; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c line 105
- Is wtimeoutMS / wtimeout honored? **engine_source.** Accepted and ignored. write_concern.rs: "wtimeout: numeric greater than i32::MAX -> FailedToParse ('writeConcern wtimeout must not exceed the maximum 32-bit integer'); non-numeric values are ignored." Negative values and NaN are accepted. The value is never applied (module doc: "there is nothing further to apply downstream"). No Learn page mentions wtimeout. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/write_concern.rs (validate_wtimeout, wtimeout_exceeds_max)
- Which readConcern levels are honored? **engine_source.** The gateway parses level strictly (case-sensitive: local, available, majority, linearizable, snapshot; anything else -> BadValue "Invalid readConcern level") and then: local and majority are accepted; available and linearizable are rejected with CommandNotSupported "'Available' read concern is not supported" / "'Linearizable' read concern is not supported" (validation.rs:126-136); snapshot is rejected with CommandNotSupported "'Snapshot' read concern is not supported" unless the gateway's allow_transaction_snapshot configuration is on (validation.rs:111-124); a readConcern sent after a transaction has started -> InvalidOptions "Read concern cannot be defined after transaction has started" (validation.rs:99-106). The engine ignores readConcern (commands_common.c:89, comment findAndModify). Issue #803 (closed 2026-10-20 as duplicate of open #736) reproduces the snapshot error on documentdb-local; whether Azure clusters enable snapshot is undocumented (see open). Also: read_concern.rs doc says afterClusterTime -> IllegalOperation and atClusterTime -> InvalidOptions, while issue #770 (release/1.0-RC2) reports both "silently accepted"; this is a version divergence inside the engine, not a Learn statement. Learn documents none of this. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/read_concern.rs; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/validation.rs lines 99-136; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c line 89; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/803; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/736; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/770
- Which readPreference values are honored, with and without HA, and what is the default? **engine_source.** readPreference never routes a read anywhere: the gateway either rejects it or discards it, and the engine lists "readPreference" in IgnoredCommonSpecFields (commands_common.c:90). Gateway read_preference.rs (main, fetched 2026-11-25): ReadPreference::parse returns Result<()> and stores nothing. Modes are matched case-insensitively; primary, primaryPreferred, secondaryPreferred and nearest are accepted and then dropped. Rejected: mode secondary -> FailedToSatisfyReadPreference "no server available for query with ReadPreference secondary" (lines 160-165); any tags field, whatever the mode -> FailedToSatisfyReadPreference "no server available for query with specified tag set list" (lines 125-131); hedge {enabled: true} -> BadValue "hedged reads are not supported" (lines 167-172); missing mode, unknown mode ("Unsupported read preference mode '...'"), mode primary combined with maxStalenessSeconds or with hedge, negative or non-integer maxStalenessSeconds -> FailedToParse. Unit tests parse_document_bytes_rejects_secondary, _rejects_tags and _rejects_hedge_enabled_true confirm this (lines 245-262). The default when nothing is sent is therefore the only possible target, the primary shard. Learn: "Standby shards don't receive client requests unless their primary shard fails" and "The primary shards serve all client requests" (reliability page); "Reads aren't served from high availability standby shards" (query-from-any-node, Limitations); with query from any node, "Client connections go to the primary physical shards. Standby shards don't serve client traffic." Reads from a cross-region or same-region replica cluster use that cluster's own connection string: "applications can use the replica cluster self connection string to perform reads from the cluster replica" (cross-region-replication). The gateway advertises itself as a mongos (hello msg: "isdbgrid", ismaster.rs:90), so drivers attach $readPreference to commands; a client configured with readPreference=secondary or with tag sets fails outright rather than silently reading from the primary. No Learn page states any of this (doc gap). Same caveat: Azure gateway assumed to match the open-source gateway. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/read_preference.rs lines 17-40, 54-176 and 220-270 (fetched 2026-11-25); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c line 90; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/ismaster.rs line 90; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb (ms.date 2026-09-21, updated_at 2026-11-23), Resilience to availability zone failures; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-from-any-node (ms.date 2026-11-06, updated_at 2026-11-09), Interaction with other features and Limitations; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/cross-region-replication (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-10-29), Continuous writes, read operations on cluster replicas, and connection strings
- Does the compatibility page's `bulkWrite: No` refer only to the MongoDB 8.0 server-level bulkWrite command, with collection-level bulkWrite() working? **documented.** Yes. The compatibility page marks the server command "bulkWrite" as No for v5.0-v8.0 under "Query and Write Operation Commands" and states "This article lists only the supported server-side commands and doesn't include client-side wrapper functions ... Any function that relies on supported server commands is compatible"; insert, update, delete and findAndModify are Yes. MongoDB's reference says the bulkWrite command is "New in version 8.0" and that "The existing db.collection.bulkWrite() method only allows you to modify one collection in one request"; the Node driver docs say client-level bulk writes "require ... MongoDB Server version 8.0 or later, Node.js driver version 6.10 or later" while collection-level ones need server 3.2+. Engine: pg_documentdb/src/commands/bulk_write.c is a "Phase 1 stub" that raises CommandNotSupported "bulkWrite is not yet implemented"; issue #770 says the gateway returns 59 CommandNotFound because the name is not in RequestType::from_str (as of release/1.0-RC2). Issue #108 (closed 2026-04-28) was closed on a demo of collection-level db.t.bulkWrite() working on documentdb-local 0.110, which exercises insert/update/delete, not the 8.0 command. Observed: run 02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233 ran collection-level coll.bulkWrite(ops, { ordered: false }) against the Azure cluster and logged ok:1, nUpserted:200, writeConcernErrors:[]; a later flush raised MongoBulkWriteError "Duplicate key violation on the requested collection: Index 'customer_event_unique'" (expected application-level duplicate, code 11000 per the insert reference). Ask Learn to footnote the row as the 8.0 database command (see doc_fixes). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-06-13), Database commands table and the note above it; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/reference/command/bulkWrite/; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/node/current/crud/bulk-write/; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/bulk_write.c; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/770; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/108; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233/events.jsonl (run_command results, observation)
- What is the per-batch write limit and does the hello response advertise it? **documented.** Learn: "A batch refers to a single request to the server. Maximum writes per batch operation: 25,000 writes. Batch operations exceeding 25,000 writes will fail. No limit on the total number of batch operations." (limitations and max-document-size pages agree). Engine: DEFAULT_MAX_WRITE_BATCH_SIZE 25000 in pg_documentdb/src/configs/system_configs.c:74-77 with the comment "TODO: Raise this back to 100,000 once we can optimize sub-transaction handling with multi-node clusters"; batches are applied in sub-transactions of 512 (DEFAULT_BATCH_WRITE_SUB_TRANSACTION_COUNT, "to line up with Mongo spark client"). The gateway's hello/isMaster reply includes maxWriteBatchSize from dynamic configuration with default 100_000 (ismaster.rs:93-96; dynamic.rs:195-197), i.e. the advertised driver split size defaults to 100,000 while the engine refuses more than 25,000: see the open item on the value Azure advertises. The MCP server encodes the 25,000 cap correctly (src/config.ts:136,145). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17), Batch limits; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/max-document-size (ms.date 2025-11-12, updated_at 2026-04-27); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/configs/system_configs.c lines 74-81 and 292-300; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/ismaster.rs lines 90-96; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs lines 195-197; documentdb-mcp clone src/config.ts:136-145
- Is the createIndexes `blocking: true` option current for all server versions, and what does it do? **documented.** Yes, it is a DocumentDB-specific createIndexes option with no server-version qualifier. Learn: "For scenarios in which the index should be created before data is loaded, the blocking option should be used to block incoming writes until the index build completes. Setting { "blocking": true } is useful in migration utilities where indexes are created on empty collections before data writes commence." with the db.runCommand({ createIndexes: ..., blocking: true }) example; the sharding page repeats it for the shard-key index. Engine: create_indexes.c parses "blocking" as a boolean-like top-level field (lines 1875-1882), default false (line 1827), and when true builds non-concurrently (buildIndexesConcurrently &= !createIndexesArg.blocking, line 1076); the wire-protocol version (5.0-8.0) is a gateway setting and does not gate this parsing. Learn does not label the option as DocumentDB-specific (MongoDB has no such option); see doc_fixes. The kit's sharding-how-to-commands.md:109 description ("makes the index build foreground (predictable but locks writes)") matches Learn. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-create-indexes (ms.date 2025-05-28, updated_at 2026-04-27), Prioritizing Index Builds over new Write Operations using the Blocking Option; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/partitioning (ms.date 2026-07-27, updated_at 2026-08-25), How to shard a collection; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_indexes.c lines 1074-1077 and 1827, 1875-1882; documentdb-agent-kit clone skills/sharding/sharding-how-to-commands.md:95-109
- Does the server return the TransientTransactionError / UnknownTransactionCommitResult labels that drivers' withTransaction retry relies on? (cross-reference from topic 2 and L08) **engine_source.** No, not as of the latest evidence. Open issue documentdb/documentdb#339 (filed 2025-09-30, labels enhancement, gateway; reproduced against the Azure service, "CosmosDB for MongoDB vCore flavor 7.0, Architecture HA"): a write conflict returns "Code 112, Message: Could not acquire lock within transaction, Labels: []" so the Go driver does not retry and "one of the transactions fails and we lose an update", whereas MongoDB returns the same code 112 with Labels: [TransientTransactionError]. Issue #836 (filed 2026-11-25) confirms the losers "failed correctly with WriteConflict (112), without the TransientTransactionError label (see #339)". Consequence for the kit and Learn: withTransaction will not auto-retry write conflicts on Azure DocumentDB; applications must catch code 112 themselves. The how-to-transactions page says only that "The callback can run more than once if the driver retries the transaction", which presumes labels. Observed in runs: no run hit a conflict; the audits for 03-core-banking-transaction-ledger and 05-appointment-scheduling-assistant flagged "no automated retry logic for transient transaction errors". Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/339; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/836; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-transactions (ms.date 2023-08-28, updated_at 2026-11-18); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/03-core-banking-transaction-ledger/20261124-200452/05-audit.json; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/05-appointment-scheduling-assistant/20261124-193733/05-audit.json
- Were multi-document transactions observed working against the Azure service in the experiment? **observed_in_runs.** Yes, on a single-shard cluster. The audit for 03-core-banking-transaction-ledger/20261124-200452 records that posting "runs inside an Azure DocumentDB multi-document transaction ... session.withTransaction" and that "verification ran the demo successfully per builder_report"; the audit for 05-appointment-scheduling-assistant/20261124-193733 records withTransaction-based create/reschedule/cancel and "demo verification scripts that were run against the live cluster". Nine runs wrote transaction code (startSession/withTransaction); none set readConcern, writeConcern or readPreference explicitly, none observed a transaction error or label, and none used the blocking index option. Sources: /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/03-core-banking-transaction-ledger/20261124-200452/05-audit.json; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/05-appointment-scheduling-assistant/20261124-193733/05-audit.json; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/09-trade-settlement-tracker/20261124-201323/events.jsonl

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Are there numeric limits on operations, documents or bytes per transaction (beyond the 30-second lifetime)? Why open: The limitations page lists only the 30-second lifetime; the how-to-transactions page lists no limits; the engine source exposes no per-transaction operation or size GUC (only transaction_timeout_sec and the 25,000-writes-per-command batch cap, which applies per write command, not per transaction). Best guess: No fixed operation/document cap; the practical bound is the 30-second lifetime plus the 16 MB document size and 25,000 writes per command. Filings should say 'keep transactions short and small; no documented count or size cap' and ask Learn to state whether one exists.
2. What error does a client receive when a transaction exceeds 30 seconds on Azure (code 251 NoSuchTransaction, as issue #756 implies, or something else), and is it labelled? Why open: No Learn page documents the expiry error; issue #756 mentions 'an idle transaction aborts (code 251) after the timeout' only as the expected test outcome; the troubleshooting error-code table omits 251, 112 and 59; no experiment run hit the timeout. Best guess: Code 251 NoSuchTransaction on the next operation or commit, without a TransientTransactionError label (consistent with #339). Filings should describe it as 'the transaction is aborted server-side after 30 s and later statements fail' without asserting the code.
3. Does the Azure service accept readConcern level snapshot (allow_transaction_snapshot on)? Why open: The gateway gates snapshot behind the dynamic configuration key allow_transaction_snapshot (dynamic.rs:88, validation.rs:111-124); documentdb-local rejects it (issues #803/#736); no Learn page or run shows the Azure value. (The getDefaultRWConcern half of this item is now settled at engine-source level: the gateway returns a constant majority/majority document.) Best guess: Snapshot is likely accepted on multi-node Azure clusters (the gateway flag exists for that purpose and #736 is scoped to single-node local) but unverified; filings should recommend omitting readConcern/writeConcern in transaction options and letting the service's defaults apply, which is what the Learn how-to code does.
4. Does the Azure gateway advertise hello.maxWriteBatchSize = 25,000 (matching the engine cap) or the gateway's compiled default of 100,000? Why open: The gateway reads maxWriteBatchSize from dynamic configuration with default 100,000 while the engine refuses more than 25,000; Learn says only that batches over 25,000 writes fail; no run logged a hello response or hit the cap. Best guess: Azure sets the dynamic value to 25,000 so drivers split correctly; if it were 100,000, a driver-side insertMany of more than 25,000 documents would fail with the documented batch error instead of being split. A one-line hello check settles it; filings should tell agents to chunk at or below 25,000 (ideally far lower) regardless.
5. Is there any server-side _etag or concurrency token on documents? Why open: No Learn page under azure/documentdb mentions _etag, optimistic concurrency or a version token (site search returns only Cosmos DB RU and Blob Storage pages); the compatibility pages list no such feature; the engine repo has no etag concept (code search hits are substrings such as metadata/delegate). Absence of a statement is not a primary-source denial. Best guess: No. Azure DocumentDB stores plain BSON; optimistic concurrency is an application pattern (own version field with a conditional findOneAndUpdate or updateOne filter), exactly as on MongoDB. Filings may state this as 'not a service feature; use the MongoDB pattern' and ask Learn to say so explicitly.
6. What starting batch size per M-series tier should the kit recommend for bulk loads? Why open: Learn gives only the 25,000-writes hard cap (limitations) and, on the migration-options page (ms.date 2025-09-17, updated_at 2026-10-23; the former /migration-best-practices URL returns 404), a method rather than a figure: 'Run a trial migration on a small but representative dataset ... A representative sample gives better estimates than synthetic test data.' and 'Extrapolate trial results to estimate Compute Tier, Storage Tier, and number of shards.', plus 'Scale up during migration, then scale down after cutover' (example M200) and 'Choose Premium SSD v2 disks for faster writes'; no per-batch or per-tier figure exists for DocumentDB. The 1,000-documents-per-insertMany figure lives on the Azure Cosmos DB for MongoDB (RU) page, a different service. The engine's 512-write sub-transaction size is an implementation hint, not guidance. Best guess: Start at 1,000 operations per insertMany/bulkWrite call with ordered:false, a handful of parallel workers, stay well under 25,000 per request, create secondary indexes after the load (how-to-create-indexes) or use blocking:true on an empty collection, and tune from there; present it as a starting point, not a Learn figure, and cite migration-options for the trial-and-extrapolate method.
7. Are unique-index builds still foreground and write-blocking on Azure (as the limitations page says), or non-blocking as the engine default now allows? Why open: Learn limitations (ms.date 2026-11-04) says 'Unique index builds are done in the foreground and block writes in the collection'; the compatibility page says they 'block read and write operations'; the release-notes page (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated_at 2026-06-02) says March 18, 2024 '[Preview] Ability to build indexes in background (except Unique indexes).' and October 14, 2024 'Index builds to run in background by default.' The engine's feature_flag_configs.c lines 607-610 define '/* Added in v0.114, enabled in v0.114, remove after v1.2 */ #define DEFAULT_ENABLE_NON_BLOCKING_UNIQUE_INDEX_BUILD true', registered at lines 1343-1349 as enableNonBlockingUniqueIndexBuild ('Whether to enable non-blocking background builds of unique indexes.'), so the open-source default contradicts both Learn statements; which engine version and flag value Azure runs is not published. Best guess: Learn is stale and unique index builds no longer block writes on current Azure clusters, but filings should keep the conservative wording ('expect a unique index build to block writes; create it before loading data') and ask Learn/engineering to confirm and to reconcile 'block writes' versus 'block read and write operations'.
8. Does the Azure gateway behave identically to the open-source gateway for writeConcern/readConcern/readPreference validation and the blocked-in-transaction command list (the basis for several settled items above)? Why open: The settled answers on w, j, wtimeout, readConcern, readPreference, getDefaultRWConcern and the 263 command list come from the open-source gateway source and issues; issue #339 was reproduced against the Azure service and matches the OSS behaviour, but no Learn page states what the Azure service does with these fields, and main and release/1.0-RC2 already differ on the blocked-command list (eleven vs four) and on afterClusterTime/atClusterTime handling (#770). Best guess: Identical to some recent gateway build (Azure DocumentDB is 'powered by the open-source DocumentDB project' per the release notes). Filings should phrase these as 'the DocumentDB gateway validates and discards these options; durability is set by the cluster's HA configuration' and ask Learn to document it, and should give the DDL-in-transaction restriction as 'DDL and admin commands such as createIndexes, dropIndexes, drop, renameCollection and listCollections are refused with code 263' rather than an exact list.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-transactions (ms.date 2023-08-28, updated_at 2026-11-18): States ACID support and shows driver code but omits every service-specific fact: the 30-second lifetime (limitations page), cross-shard support (partitioning page), which commands are refused inside a transaction (code 263: on main createIndexes, createSearchIndexes, dropIndexes, reIndex, drop, renameCollection, listCollections, currentOp, killOp, moveCollection, plus data operations on admin/config/local and system.* collections), that readConcern cannot be set after the transaction starts, that WriteConflict (112) is returned without the TransientTransactionError label so withTransaction does not auto-retry (issue #339), and which readConcern/writeConcern values are accepted. The sentence 'The callback can run more than once if the driver retries the transaction' presumes labels the server does not send.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-06-13): The 'bulkWrite | No' row needs a footnote that it is the MongoDB 8.0 database-level bulkWrite command (multi-namespace, used by MongoClient.bulkWrite); collection-level db.collection.bulkWrite() maps to insert/update/delete and works. Also, the unique-index note says the build 'block[s] read and write operations' while the limitations page says it 'block[s] writes'; and the Session Commands rows (killSessions Yes, killAllSessions No) disagree with compatibility-features, which lists killSessions among restricted commands. getDefaultRWConcern is marked Yes but the page does not say the response is a constant (majority/majority, source implicit) that the engine does not act on.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17): 'Unique index builds are done in the foreground and block writes in the collection' conflicts with the compatibility page ('block read and write operations'), with the Oct 14, 2024 release note 'Index builds to run in background by default', and with the engine default enableNonBlockingUniqueIndexBuild=true; needs engineering confirmation. 'Maximum transaction lifetime: 30 seconds' should be cross-linked from the transactions how-to. The Batch limits section should name the error a client sees when a request exceeds 25,000 writes and state whether hello.maxWriteBatchSize reflects 25,000.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-create-indexes (ms.date 2025-05-28, updated_at 2026-04-27) and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/partitioning: The createIndexes 'blocking: true' option is DocumentDB-specific (MongoDB has no such option) but is not labelled as such, and no page states its default (false) or that it applies on every supported wire-protocol version. The how-to also says Azure DocumentDB 'responds to a createIndex operation only after the index is fully built' without reconciling that with the Oct 2024 release note 'Index builds to run in background by default'.
- No Learn page documents writeConcern (w, j, wtimeout), readConcern or readPreference semantics for Azure DocumentDB: The insert reference lists writeConcern only as 'A document expressing the write concern'. Learn should state that the service validates these options and does not act on them (w:0/1/'majority' accepted, w>=2 rejected with BadValue; j and wtimeout accepted and ignored; readConcern available/linearizable rejected with CommandNotSupported, snapshot subject to configuration; readPreference primary/primaryPreferred/secondaryPreferred/nearest accepted but reads always go to primary shards, while readPreference secondary, tag sets and hedged reads are rejected with FailedToSatisfyReadPreference/BadValue; replica-cluster reads require the replica's own connection string), and that durability is determined by HA (synchronous standby) rather than by w. getDefaultRWConcern returns a constant majority/majority document that does not reflect applied behaviour.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/troubleshoot-common-issues (ms.date 2025-06-02, updated_at 2026-04-27): The error-code table omits codes a transaction, bulk-write or read-preference client meets: 112 WriteConflict ('Could not acquire lock within transaction'), 251 NoSuchTransaction, 59 CommandNotFound (returned for the 8.0 bulkWrite command per issue #770), 133 FailedToSatisfyReadPreference ('no server available for query with ReadPreference secondary'), and lists 263 OperationNotSupportedInTransaction without saying which commands trigger it; it also does not say that none carry MongoDB's transient-error labels.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-features (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated_at 2026-05-22): Says users 'retain control over ... getShardMap, balancerStart, balancerStatus, balancerStop' while compatibility-query-language marks all four N/A (Azure-managed). Lists killSessions as restricted while compatibility-query-language marks it Yes. Also lists $listSearchIndexes as 'Not prioritized' although it is used by the full-text search pages (already noted under topic 7).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-documentdb (ms.date 2026-09-21, updated_at 2026-11-23) vs https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability (ms.date 2026-10-28): Noticed in passing (topic 2/HA): the reliability page says 'Azure DocumentDB doesn't support same-zone HA deployments' while the newer high-availability page says 'You can explicitly request same-zone HA by setting highAvailability.targetMode to SameZone'. The high-availability page is newer; engineering should confirm which is right.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq (ms.date 2026-11-06) and compare-mongodb-atlas (ms.date 2026-11-08) vs high-availability (2026-10-28) and high-availability-replication-best-practices (2026-10-28): Noticed in passing: FAQ/comparison say '99.995% availability SLA for highly available configurations'; the HA pages say 99.99% with in-region HA and 99.995% only when combined with a cross-region replica.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility (linked from the kit) and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/migration-best-practices: Both return HTTP 404; the live pages are compatibility-query-language / compatibility-features and migration-options (whose 'Migration best practices' section holds the trial-migration guidance). Redirects would fix the kit's and any external links.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit: skills/natural-language-querying/SKILL.md:261-262`: Points to https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/documentdb/compatibility as 'the authoritative list of supported features'; that URL returns 404. Should link compatibility-query-language (and compatibility-features).
- `documentdb-agent-kit: skills/connection/SKILL.md:207`: 'Use nearest read preference for geo-distributed setups' has no Learn basis for Azure DocumentDB and the underlying model is wrong: the gateway parses $readPreference and discards nearest/primaryPreferred/secondaryPreferred (read_preference.rs), rejects secondary and tag sets outright with FailedToSatisfyReadPreference and hedged reads with BadValue, standby shards never serve reads (reliability page; query-from-any-node Limitations), and a cross-region replica is a separate cluster reached through its own connection string (cross-region-replication). readPreference cannot route reads anywhere, and a client configured with readPreference=secondary (or tags) fails rather than falling back to the primary; the correct advice is to leave readPreference at its default and point read-heavy clients at the replica cluster's connection string.
- `documentdb-agent-kit: skills/sharding/sharding-logical-vs-physical.md:90`: 'Multi-document / multi-shard transactions are supported but cost more than single-shard transactions' - the support claim matches Learn (partitioning page), but the cost claim is not stated on any Learn page; keep it as reasoning, not a sourced fact.
- `documentdb-agent-kit: skills/ (all), notably skills/driver and skills/connection`: Gap rather than error: no skill mentions the 30-second transaction lifetime, that WriteConflict (112) comes back without the TransientTransactionError label (so withTransaction will not auto-retry and the app must retry code 112), that DDL and admin commands (createIndexes/createSearchIndexes/dropIndexes/drop/reIndex/renameCollection/listCollections/currentOp/killOp/moveCollection on main) are refused inside a transaction (263), that writeConcern/readConcern are validated and ignored and readPreference secondary is rejected, or the 25,000-writes-per-request cap for insertMany/bulkWrite. The MCP server does encode the 25,000 cap (src/config.ts:136-145) and cites the Learn sentence correctly.
- `documentdb-agent-kit: skills/sharding/sharding-how-to-commands.md:109 and skills/sharding/sharding-shard-key-selection.md:120`: Not an error: the 'blocking: true' usage and the description 'makes the index build foreground (predictable but locks writes)' match how-to-create-indexes and the engine (create_indexes.c). Recorded so filings do not flag it.

_Notes: Repair pass 2026-11-25 after the skeptic's review. Two settled items were re-settled from the main-branch gateway source re-fetched today: (1) the blocked-in-transaction list is eleven commands (request_type.rs:470-484, test at 770-783) plus the validation.rs:41-92 rules on killCursors-at-start, admin/config/local data operations and system.* collections, all code 263; the earlier four-command list was issue #770's release/1.0-RC2 snapshot. (2) readPreference is not uniformly accepted-and-ignored: secondary and any tags field -> FailedToSatisfyReadPreference, hedge enabled -> BadValue, malformed/unknown/primary+maxStaleness -> FailedToParse; only primary/primaryPreferred/secondaryPreferred/nearest are discarded. One new settled item: the gateway answers getDefaultRWConcern with a constant majority/majority 'implicit' document (constant.rs:63-93, process.rs:148), admin-only; the snapshot readConcern half of that open item remains open. Open items updated with the sources the skeptic pinned: migration-options (ms.date 2025-09-17, updated_at 2026-10-23) for the trial-and-extrapolate text (the /migration-best-practices URL 404s), and release-notes (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated_at 2026-06-02) plus feature_flag_configs.c:607-610/1343-1349 for the unique-index contradiction. Everything the skeptic accepted is kept verbatim. Standard of evidence: Learn pages fetched 2026-11-25 with ms.date/updated_at recorded; engine facts from documentdb/documentdb main-branch source and issues #108, #339, #717, #736, #756, #770, #803, #836; MongoDB facts from mongodb.com manual and Node driver docs; observations from runs/02-real-time-event-stream-ingestion/20261124-203233 (collection-level bulkWrite ok:1 nUpserted:200; MongoBulkWriteError duplicate key) and the audits of runs/03-core-banking-transaction-ledger/20261124-200452 and runs/05-appointment-scheduling-assistant/20261124-193733 (withTransaction executed against the live single-shard cluster). No run set w/j/wtimeout/readConcern/readPreference, used blocking:true, hit the 25,000 cap or observed a transaction error, so those items rest on documentation and engine source only. All gateway-derived answers carry the caveat that the Azure gateway is assumed to match the open-source gateway, and that main and release/1.0-RC2 already differ (blocked-command list, afterClusterTime handling), so filings should describe categories of refused commands rather than exact lists. Issue #767 (retryable writes, topic 1) is related: with no retry labels and no write de-duplication, every transient-failure story for this topic reduces to 'make writes idempotent and retry in application code', which is also the Learn reliability page's guidance ('design write operations to be idempotent so that retrying them is safe'). Learn pages 'compatibility' (linked by the kit) and 'migration-best-practices' return 404. ## Schema validation, TTL, indexing and query behaviour (SME topic 10): researched 2026-11-25, 12 settled, 5 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Does $jsonSchema support enum, additionalProperties, allOf/anyOf/oneOf/not, patternProperties and dependencies today? **engine_source.** Learn (current) says none of them are supported: the $jsonSchema reference (ms.date 2025-12-30, updated_at 2026-01-28) lists additionalProperties, allOf, anyOf, dependencies, enum, maxProperties, minProperties, not, oneOf, patternProperties (plus description, title) under 'Unsupported Keywords' and says to use pattern/$in or application logic instead. The open-source engine main branch is ahead of that page: pg_documentdb/src/jsonschema/bson_json_schema_tree.c now parses `enum` (ParseEnum, PR merged 2026-07-23) and `oneOf` (ParseOneOf, PR merged 2026-07-16), while additionalProperties, allOf, anyOf, not, patternProperties, dependencies, minProperties and maxProperties fall through to the 'Unknown $jsonSchema keyword: %s' error (lines 150-400; the full dispatch list is properties, required, type, bsonType, encrypt, encryptMetadata, description, title, enum, oneOf, multipleOf, maximum, exclusiveMaximum, minimum, exclusiveMinimum, maxLength, minLength, pattern, items, additionalItems, maxItems, minItems, uniqueItems; $ref/$schema/default/definitions/format/id raise 'not supported at this time'; type 'integer' and dot-notation property paths are also rejected). Filings should keep `pattern` (works everywhere) and may say enum/oneOf are in the engine but not yet documented for the service. Separately, collection-level validators are a gated preview: release notes 2025-10-10 '[Preview] Add schema validation support for $jsonSchema during rule creation or modification'; the compatibility page says 'Schema validation supports: insert, update, findAndModify, and the $merge / $out stages in aggregation. Use bypassDocumentValidation to skip validation if needed.'; and pg_documentdb/src/metadata/collection.c returns CommandNotSupported 'validator not supported yet' for validator, validationAction and validationLevel when the EnableSchemaValidation GUC is off (lines 2273-2276, 2319-2322, 2362-2365). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/operators/evaluation-query/$jsonschema; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/jsonschema/bson_json_schema_tree.c (dispatch lines 150-400; ParseEnum ~786; ParseOneOf ~833; commits d2dbc98c 2026-07-23 'support json schema keyword enum', e80419e1 2026-07-16 'support json schema keyword oneof'); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/metadata/collection.c (ParseAndGetValidatorSpec lines 2270-2277)
- Is { unique: true, partialFilterExpression: { key: { $exists: true } } } accepted? **engine_source.** The engine accepts unique indexes with a partialFilterExpression, and accepts $exists in a partial filter only when it evaluates to true. Regression tests create `{"key": {"a": 1}, "unique": true, "partialFilterExpression": {"b": {"$gt": 0}}}` and `{"key": {"partialTest": 1}, "unique": true, "partialFilterExpression": {"a": {"$gt": 1}}}` successfully (numIndexesAfter increments, ok: 1). create_indexes.c's partial-filter walker allows $exists only with a truthy right-hand side ('$exists is supported only when rhs evaluates to true', otherwise ThrowUnsupportedPartFilterExprError) and allows $type exact matches, $and/$or at top level and $in with scalar arrays; it also rejects mixing partialFilterExpression with sparse ('cannot mix "partialFilterExpression" and "sparse" options'). Learn's indexing page says only 'Partial indexes don't support ORDER BY or UNIQUE unless the filter qualifies' (ms.date 2024-09-23, updated_at 2026-11-17), which does not define 'qualifies'; the engine source and tests settle that `{ $exists: true }` is an accepted filter for a unique partial index. Not observed in this experiment's runs. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/test/regress/expected/commands_create_indexes_background_unique_tests.out (lines 314-319, idx_a_pfe_b_gt_0); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/test/regress/expected/bson_aggregation_stage_merge_tests.out (lines 1432-1436, index_8); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_indexes.c ($exists handling ~lines 4557-4585; sparse+pfe error ~line 3201); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/indexing (Partial indexes > Limitations)
- TTL semantics: single-field only, non-Date values ignored, handling of a missing field, deletion lag (about 60 seconds?) **engine_source.** Single-field only: create_indexes.c raises 'TTL indexes work only on single fields, and compound indexes are incompatible with TTL functionality.' (line 2793) and 'The 'expireAfterSeconds' option for a TTL index must be a numeric value' (line 2297); a 2dsphere TTL index is also rejected. Non-Date and missing values: the purger deletes with `bson_dollar_lt(document, {<key>: <BSON Date = now - expireAfterSeconds*1000>})` (ttl_index.c ~lines 640-646 and 745-756, expiryField.value_type = BSON_TYPE_DATE_TIME); Because the predicate is a BSON $lt against a Date-typed value and BSON comparison is type-bracketed, numbers, strings and missing fields do not match and are not deleted (this consequence is an inference from the Date-typed predicate in the source, not a statement quoted from any source or Learn page). A partialFilterExpression on the index is also applied to the TTL delete (ttl_index.c comment ~line 630). Lag: the engine schedules `documentdb_ttl_task` with pg_cron '* * * * *' (every minute) and bounds each run with GUC defaults TTLTaskMaxRunTimeInMS = 60000, SingleTTLTaskTimeBudget = 20000, MaxTTLDeleteBatchSize = 1000, TTLPurgerStatementTimeout = 60000 (background_job_configs.c lines 83-100); Learn release notes 2025-10-10 say '[Preview] ... TTL index performs batch deletions continuously for up to 60 seconds, instead of once every 60 seconds.' So 'about 60 seconds, not exact' is the right wording. Observation: in runs/12-hospital-bed-occupancy-tracker/20261124-194835 a document with a TTL index (expireAfterSeconds: 0) and a Date 5 s in the future was still present after 30 s of polling ('TTL delete not observed within 30s'), consistent with a lag of up to about a minute. No Learn page under azure/documentdb documents TTL semantics; the compatibility page only lists the TTL index property as supported. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_indexes.c (lines 2289-2303, 2789-2793); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/ttl/ttl_index.c (DeleteExpiredRowsForIndexCore, lines 603-760); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/configs/background_job_configs.c (lines 79-124); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/sql/documentdb--0.102-0--0.102-1.sql (cron.schedule('documentdb_ttl_task', '* * * * *', ...)); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes (October 10, 2025, Engine enhancements); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (Index properties table); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/12-hospital-bed-occupancy-tracker/20261124-194835/events.jsonl (node src/ttl_check.js output; observation)
- Does allowDiskUse change behaviour or is it a no-op? **engine_source.** No-op. pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c lists `allowDiskUse` in IgnoredCommonSpecFields, introduced by the comment 'This is a list of command options that are not currently supported. At runtime, we ignore these optional fields.' (lines 51-62). The option is accepted without error (observed: runs/02-ai-powered-product-recommendations/20261124-181835 ran aggregate(pipeline, { allowDiskUse: true }) successfully). Memory behaviour is governed by the tier instead: the limitations page says 'The maximum memory size for MongoDB queries depends on the tier. For example, for M80, the query memory size limit is approximately 150 MiB' and 'Sorting is done in memory and doesn't push down to the index.' The Learn aggregate command page (ms.date 2025-12-30) lists allowDiskUse among the options without saying it is ignored. Filings should drop the 'set allowDiskUse: true on heavy pipelines' rule or state that the service accepts and ignores it. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/commands/commands_common.c (lines 51-70); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (Maximum MongoDB query size; General indexing limitations); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/commands/aggregation/aggregate; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/02-ai-powered-product-recommendations/20261124-181835/events.jsonl (observation)
- Does the suggested $setWindowFields example (chained stages, $last in $group, cumulative documents window) run? (component support) **observed_in_runs.** Every component is supported individually. Compatibility page (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated 2026-06-13): `$setWindowFields` stage supported v5.0-v8.0; window operators `$sum`, `$max`, `$min`, `$first`, `$last`, `$avg`, `$push`, `$count`, `$rank`, `$shift`, `$derivative`, `$integral`, `$linearFill`, `$locf` etc. supported; `$last` accumulator in `$group` supported. Release notes: 2024-08-05 `$setWindowFields` with `$sum`; 2024-09-16 `$count`, `$addToSet`, `$push`, `$avg`, `$rank`, `$denseRank`; 2024-10-14 `$integral`, `$derivative`, `$expMovingAvg`, `$linearFill`, `$locf`, `$documentNumber`, `$shift`, plus `$first`/`$last`/`$top`/`$bottom` in `$group`. Engine regression tests exercise `$sum` and `$avg` with `window: {documents: [-1, 0]}`, `[-1, 1]` and `[unbounded, current]` (bson_aggregation_pipeline_tests_sumavg_group.out) and `$first`/`$last` with partitionBy+sortBy (bson_aggregation_pipeline_tests_firstlast_group_pg18.out); no cited test covers a cumulative `$max`.
