Observation: runs/07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018 ran `$match -> $setWindowFields { partitionBy: {...}, sortBy: {timestamp: 1}, output: { movingAvg: { $avg, window: { documents: [-4, 0] } } } } -> $project` and got 9 rows back; explain on the same pipeline returned explainVersion 2 output. The cumulative `[unbounded, current]` window is shown on the Learn `$push` window-operator page. The Learn stage page documentdb/query/operators/aggregation/$setwindowfields returns 404 (doc gap already in L08). The exact two-stage pipeline with a cumulative `$max` was not executed by any run; see open items. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (Window Operators; Accumulators; Aggregation Pipeline Stages); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes (2024-08-05, 2024-09-16, 2024-10-14); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/operators/array-update/$push; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/test/regress/expected/bson_aggregation_pipeline_tests_sumavg_group.out; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/07-lab-results-aggregator/20261124-194018/events.jsonl (python scripts/run_agg.py, 'Results count: 9'; documentdb_explain_operation; observation); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/test/regress/expected/bson_aggregation_pipeline_tests_firstlast_group_pg18.out
- Is sortBy required for every window except unbounded? **engine_source.** Yes. pg_documentdb/src/aggregation/bson_aggregation_window_operators.c EnsureSortRequirements (lines 508-548): a range window requires exactly one ascending sortBy field ('Expected a single sort by field for range-based window', 'Expected ascending sortBy field definition'); a documents window with no sortBy errors 'Missing sortBy field for document-based window' unless both bounds are unbounded (FRAMEOPTION_START_UNBOUNDED_PRECEDING and END_UNBOUNDED_FOLLOWING). An output with no window and no sortBy is allowed (regression test '$first/$last without sortBy in $setWindowFields'). Operator-specific rules: `$derivative`/`$integral` need a non-compound sortBy ('%s requires a sortBy', '%s needs a non-compound sortBy parameter'); `$shift` and `$expMovingAvg` need sortBy ('sortBy parameters must be specified for $shift'); `$linearFill`, `$locf`, `$rank`, `$denseRank`, `$documentNumber` need a single-element top-level sortBy ('... must be provided along with a top-level sortBy expression that contains exactly one element'). The Learn `$rank` page states sortBy 'Must be specified for $rank'. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/aggregation/bson_aggregation_window_operators.c (lines 508-548, 1736-1748, 2144-2150, 2170-2175); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb/src/test/regress/expected/bson_aggregation_pipeline_tests_firstlast_group_pg18.out (test 24); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/operators/window-operators/$rank
- Is 120 seconds the default query execution limit? **documented.** Yes. Limitations page (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17), 'MongoDB execution limitations': 'Default query execution limit: 120 seconds. This limit can be overridden on a per-query basis using maxTimeMS in the respective MongoDB driver.' with example db.collection.find({...}).maxTimeMS(5000). Same list: 'Maximum transaction lifetime: 30 seconds.' and 'Cursor lifetime: 10 minutes.' Note the MCP server sends its own maxTimeMS (default MONGODB_MAX_TIME_MS = 30000 ms, src/config.ts:221), so MCP calls time out at 30 s, not 120 s. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/documentdb-mcp/src/config.ts:221
- Is the claim that wide rows are detoasted on read before projection accurate and worth stating? **engine_source.** Accurate as a description of the engine: each document is one `bson` varlena column, and every engine function that receives it calls PG_DETOAST_DATUM on the whole datum (pg_documentdb_core/include/io/pgbson.h: `#define DatumGetPgBson(n) ((pgbson *) PG_DETOAST_DATUM(n))`, not the sliced variant), so a filter or projection function sees the fully detoasted document; engine issue #695 ($ne in large row data performance, open, 2026-07-30) reports from a flame graph that 'the performance of the $ne operation is heavily spent on UNTOAST', and the maintainer response points to index-metadata tracking as the mitigation. No Learn page states this. Whether to put a PostgreSQL storage detail in a user-facing kit rule is a maintainer decision, not an SME fact; the safe phrasing is 'a projection reduces what is sent over the wire, not what the server reads from storage'. Sources: https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/pg_documentdb_core/include/io/pgbson.h (DatumGetPgBson); https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/695
- Does checking for COLLSCAN anywhere in explain output work with DocumentDB's explain layout? **documented.** Yes, provided the walker descends into `stages[].$cursor` for aggregate explains. Learn 'Read query explain output in Azure DocumentDB' (ms.date 2026-06-22, updated_at 2026-06-30): `winningPlan.stage` 'Common values are COLLSCAN, IXSCAN, and FETCH'; stage table lists COLLSCAN, IXSCAN, FETCH, SORT, COUNT_SCAN, COUNT, GROUP, LIMIT, DISTINCT_SCAN, DISTINCT_UNWIND, UNIQUE, PARALLEL_MERGE; executionStats fields nReturned, totalKeysExamined, totalDocsExamined, executionTimeMillis; for aggregate 'The output includes a stages array ... Inspect the first stage for COLLSCAN or IXSCAN'. The older how-to-index page shows the DocumentDB-specific find layout: explainVersion 2, queryPlanner.winningPlan { stage: 'COLLSCAN', runtimeFilterSet, estimatedTotalKeysExamined } or FETCH -> IXSCAN with indexName, isBitmap, indexFilterSet. Observed on the shared cluster: find explain in runs/01-anti-money-laundering-pattern-detector/20261124-200112 returned queryPlanner.winningPlan.stage 'COLLSCAN' with collectionPlannerStatistics; aggregate explain in runs/12-cross-border-payment-audit-trail/20261124-201744 returned stages[0].$cursor.queryPlanner.winningPlan.stage 'COLLSCAN'. This contradicts the kit's claim that DocumentDB never emits COLLSCAN (see kit errors). The K32 template's recursive walk over `stage` keys including the `stages` array therefore matches the real layout for find and aggregate; a cosmosSearch vector pipeline explain was not observed. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-read-explain-output; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-index; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes (2024-03-03: allShardsQueryPlan, allShardsExecution explain modes); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/01-anti-money-laundering-pattern-detector/20261124-200112/events.jsonl (documentdb_explain_operation; observation); /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/runs/12-cross-border-payment-audit-trail/20261124-201744/events.jsonl (documentdb_explain_operation; observation)
- Monitoring: are diagnostic logs unavailable on M30 and lower and on free tiers? **documented.** Yes per the diagnostics how-to (ms.date 2023-10-31, updated_at 2026-04-27), Important box: 'This feature is not available with compute tier M30 or lower tiers, or free-tier SKUs.' The limitations page (updated 2026-11-17) free-tier list says 'Diagnostic logging not supported (available only in paid tiers)', which is looser (it implies M10-M30 paid tiers have it); report the two as a documentation inconsistency and use the stricter 'M40 and above' in filings. Related: monitor-metrics (ms.date 2025-06-02) says system metrics are 'available on all cluster tiers' but the Database metric 'Mongo request duration' needs 'the M40 cluster tier or higher'. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-monitor-diagnostics-logs; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (Free tier limitations); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/monitor-metrics
- Monitoring: the vCoreMongoRequests category and table **documented.** The diagnostic-setting category is `vCoreMongoRequests` (Azure Monitor supported-logs reference for Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters, ms.date 2026-07-14: 'Costs to export: Yes; Log table: AzureDiagnostics; Supports basic log plan: No; Supports ingestion-time transformation: No'). With a resource-specific destination the data lands in the `VCoreMongoRequests` table (Azure Monitor tables reference, ms.date 2026-07-27: resource type microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters, columns include ActivityId, ClientIp, ClusterName, CollectionName, DatabaseName, DurationMs, ErrorCode, OperationName, PiiCommandText, RegionName, RequestLength, ResponseLength, UserAgent, UserId, TimeGenerated), and the Learn how-to's KQL samples query `VCoreMongoRequests` (ErrorCode != 0; percentile(DurationMs, 99) by OperationName; count by UserAgent) under 'This section assumes that you are using a Log Analytics workspace with resource-specific logs.' There is no separate slow-query category or toggle; filter VCoreMongoRequests on DurationMs. Inconsistency: the supported-logs reference lists only AzureDiagnostics as the log table while the resource-specific table exists. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/reference/supported-logs/microsoft-documentdb-mongoclusters-logs; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/reference/tables/vcoremongorequests; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-monitor-diagnostics-logs
- Monitoring: metric names CpuPercent, MemoryPercent, StoragePercent, IOPS **documented.** Confirmed. Azure Monitor supported-metrics reference for Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters (ms.date 2026-07-31), 'Name in REST API' column: `CpuPercent` (Percent), `MemoryPercent` (Percent), `StoragePercent` (Percent), `IOPS` (Count), plus `CommittedMemoryPercent`, `StorageUsed` (Bytes), `AutoscaleUtilizationPercent`, `NetworkBytesEgress`, `NetworkBytesIngress` (Bytes) and `MongoRequestDurationMs` (Milliseconds, dimensions Authentication, CollectionName, DatabaseName, ErrorCode, Operation, Protocol, ServerName, StatusCode, StatusCodeClass, StatusText). Saturation and traffic metrics have dimension `ServerName`, time grain PT1M, aggregations Average/Maximum/Minimum, exportable via diagnostic settings. The how-to-manage-alerts page (ms.date 2025-01-10) uses display names only ('Storage percent', threshold 85; suggested disk alerts at 75/85/95 %). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/reference/supported-metrics/microsoft-documentdb-mongoclusters-metrics; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/monitor-metrics; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-manage-alerts

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Which engine build the Azure service runs, i.e. whether `enum` and `oneOf` (engine main since July 2026) are live on managed clusters, and what the self-serve path is to enable collection validators (validator/validationLevel/validationAction) on a cluster. Why open: Learn's $jsonSchema page (updated 2026-01-28) still lists enum/oneOf as unsupported and no Learn page documents an enablement step; the ARM reference's previewFeatures enum lists only 'GeoReplicas' (while FullTextSearch and ShardRebalancer are used elsewhere), and the only enablement claims ('SchemaValidation' preview feature via ARM PATCH; backend rollout; GA 'by end of June or early July') come from Microsoft Q&A threads 5891204 and 5901887, which are supporting evidence only. The engine gate (EnableSchemaValidation GUC -> 'validator not supported yet') is settled. Best guess: Validators are a preview feature enabled per cluster (release notes 2025-10-10); filings should say 'preview, enablement not yet documented on Learn, expect CommandNotSupported "validator not supported yet" until enabled' and keep `pattern`/`$in` instead of `enum`.
2. Does the exact L08 example (two chained $setWindowFields stages with a cumulative documents: ['unbounded','current'] window on $sum and $max, followed by $group with $last) run as written on the managed service? Why open: Each component is documented or observed (see settled), but no run executed the combined pipeline and no Learn page shows chained window stages or a cumulative $max. Best guess: Runs; engine window functions map to PostgreSQL window clauses and the regression suite covers bounded documents windows for $sum/$min/$max and $first/$last.
3. Explain layout for a cosmosSearch ($search / vector) aggregate pipeline, and whether the stage names the K32 template flags (COLLSCAN, SORT) appear there. Why open: No run captured an explain of a vector pipeline; the Learn explain page documents stage names generically and the stages array for aggregate only. Best guess: Same `stages[].$cursor.queryPlanner` layout with an IXSCAN on the vector index; the template's recursive walk will not break but may report no stage names it recognises.
4. Is the diagnostic-log gate 'M30 and lower lack it' (how-to) or 'free tier only lacks it' (limitations page)? Why open: Two current Learn pages disagree; the how-to's Important box is explicit about M30-or-lower, the limitations page (newer updated_at 2026-11-17) says 'available only in paid tiers'. Best guess: The how-to is right (M40 and above); filings use that and flag the limitations-page wording.
5. Actual TTL purge cadence on managed clusters (every-minute cron with up-to-60 s continuous batches is the open-source default; the release note marks the continuous behaviour as [Preview]). Why open: Engine defaults are settled from source, but service-side GUC values are not published and the only observation (no delete within 30 s of a 5 s expiry) does not bound the lag. Best guess: Expiry within about one to two minutes under normal load; filings say 'about 60 seconds, not exact'.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/operators/evaluation-query/$jsonschema: Lists `enum` and `oneOf` as unsupported although the engine main branch parses both (PRs merged 2026-07-16 and 2026-07-23); the syntax block shows createCollection with validator/validationLevel/validationAction without saying collection validators are a gated preview that returns 'validator not supported yet' until enabled, and does not link the release note or an enablement step.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters (and 2026-06-15-preview): properties.previewFeatures is described as 'List of private endpoint connections' and allows only 'GeoReplicas', while the page's own Terraform sample sets 'ShardRebalancer' and the full-text-search overview uses 'FullTextSearch'; no value is documented for schema validation.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/commands/aggregation/aggregate: Lists `allowDiskUse` as an aggregate option without stating that the engine accepts and ignores it (commands_common.c IgnoredCommonSpecFields); readers assume it changes memory behaviour.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/indexing: 'Partial indexes don't support ORDER BY or UNIQUE unless the filter qualifies' never defines 'qualifies'; the engine creates unique partial indexes with $gt and $exists:true filters. Also no TTL section (expireAfterSeconds, Date-only, single-field, lag), so Learn search lands on the RU-based Cosmos DB for MongoDB `_ts` page.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/documentdb/query/operators/aggregation/$setwindowfields: 404; there is no $setWindowFields stage page describing partitionBy/sortBy/window syntax, the sortBy requirement (required for every bounded documents window and all range windows; errors 'Missing sortBy field for document-based window', 'Expected a single sort by field for range-based window'), or a running-total example.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/reference/supported-logs/microsoft-documentdb-mongoclusters-logs: Shows AzureDiagnostics as the only log table for category vCoreMongoRequests, while the resource-specific table VCoreMongoRequests exists and is what the DocumentDB how-to queries.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations: Free-tier bullet 'Diagnostic logging not supported (available only in paid tiers)' conflicts with how-to-monitor-diagnostics-logs ('not available with compute tier M30 or lower tiers, or free-tier SKUs'); the limitations page is the newer one (updated 2026-11-17) but the how-to is the specific one. Also 'HNSW ... available in M40+' is stale (already recorded in topic 7).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-read-explain-output: Describes the aggregate output as 'a stages array' without showing that the plan sits at stages[0].$cursor.queryPlanner.winningPlan, and does not mention DocumentDB-specific fields that how-to-index shows (explainVersion 2, runtimeFilterSet, indexFilterSet, isBitmap, estimatedTotalKeysExamined, collectionPlannerStatistics) or the allShardsQueryPlan/allShardsExecution modes from the release notes.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes: Note 'Items tagged as [Preview] require a support request to enable them on your cluster' is stale for self-serve previews (full-text search) and gives no path for the schema-validation preview; TTL 'continuously for up to 60 seconds' behaviour is documented only here, not on an indexing page.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/monitoring/monitoring-metrics-alerts.md:40`: Bicep alert uses metricName 'cpu_percent'; the Azure Monitor REST name is 'CpuPercent' (others: MemoryPercent, StoragePercent, IOPS, StorageUsed, CommittedMemoryPercent, MongoRequestDurationMs).
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/monitoring/monitoring-slow-query-log.md:11,25,31-32,41`: Step 2 'Enable slow-query / operation logs in the settings' describes a toggle that does not exist (the only category is vCoreMongoRequests); the KQL queries a placeholder table DocumentDBSlowQueries_CL with columns DocsExamined, Namespace, QueryShape that do not exist. Use the VCoreMongoRequests table (DurationMs, OperationName, DatabaseName, CollectionName, ErrorCode, PiiCommandText) and state that diagnostic logs are not available on M30 or lower or free tiers; link how-to-monitor-diagnostics-logs instead of the landing page.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/scripts/perf-advisor.sh:406-407 and documentdb-agent-kit/testing/scenarios/ecommerce-missing-index/SCENARIO.md:21`: States 'DocumentDB (Postgres-backed) never emits a literal COLLSCAN stage ... falls back to a full IXSCAN over the _id_ primary key'. Learn how-to-index and how-to-read-explain-output document a COLLSCAN stage, and this experiment observed winningPlan.stage 'COLLSCAN' for both find and aggregate explains on the managed service. The script's _id_ heuristic may still be useful for the local container but the claim is wrong.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/indexing/index-ttl-expiry.md:12,42,92 and documentdb-agent-kit/skills/indexing/index-pattern-cookbook.md:238`: 'A background thread deletes expired documents roughly every 60 seconds' and '~60s' describe the old behaviour; release notes 2025-10-10 say the TTL task runs batch deletions continuously for up to 60 seconds (preview) on a per-minute schedule. Wording should be 'expiry lags by up to about a minute'. The rule cites only MongoDB docs; cite the DocumentDB release note and the engine source (single-field error, Date-only predicate) since Learn has no TTL page.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/query-optimization/query-explain-plan.md:23,28`: Suggests reading a 'shards[]' array to detect scatter-gather; no Learn page documents such a field for Azure DocumentDB (the documented cross-shard view is the allShardsQueryPlan / allShardsExecution explain mode). The rule links only MongoDB's explain docs; it should link https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-read-explain-output and mention the stages[].$cursor layout for aggregate.
- `findings/phase4/filings/K32-merged.md (proposed rule query-allow-disk-use.md, lines 89, 100, 150-152)`: Proposes 'allowDiskUse: true on any pipeline with $group/$sort/$bucket/$facet'. The engine ignores the option (commands_common.c IgnoredCommonSpecFields). Drop the rule or replace it with 'the service accepts and ignores allowDiskUse; memory limits are per tier (about 150 MiB on M80), so bound the input with an indexed $match and $limit'.
- `documentdb-mcp/src/tools/document-tools.ts:321-324`: The aggregate tool exposes allow_disk_use described as 'Allow pipeline stages to write to disk'; the server ignores allowDiskUse, so the description overstates its effect (minor).

_Notes: Final after skeptic review (2026-11-25): the skeptic checked all 12 SETTLED items, rejected none, downgraded none and found no missed sources, so every settled item, open item, doc fix and kit/MCP error from the original report is kept. Two wording corrections from the skeptic's caveats were applied: (a) the $setWindowFields item no longer claims the regression suite exercises $min/$max with an [unbounded, unbounded] window (the cited sumavg_group.out covers $sum/$avg with [-1,0], [-1,1] and [unbounded,current]); (b) the TTL item now labels 'numbers, strings and missing fields are not deleted' as an inference from the Date-typed $lt predicate rather than a quoted source. The skeptic also re-attempted the OPEN items: schema-validation enablement has no Learn page and only Microsoft Q&A answers (one community-accepted answer says service-side rollout with no customer toggle, contradicting an AI-generated answer about preview registration), so it stays open; the vector-search page (updated 2026-11-25) shows no explain output; TTL purge cadence, the exact L08 chained pipeline and the diagnostic-log tier conflict have no further public source. Evidence standard: Learn pages were fetched 2026-11-25 and their ms.date/updated_at recorded above; engine facts come from the documentdb/documentdb main branch as of 2026-11-25 (files downloaded to the scratchpad engine-src folder); run observations come from runs/*/events.jsonl and are labelled as observations. Microsoft Q&A threads 5891204 (2026-05-14, 'validator not supported yet' on server 8.0, M20) and 5901887 (2026-05-25) were read as supporting evidence only. The compatibility page's note 'Schema validation supports: insert, update, findAndModify, and the $merge / $out stages ... Use bypassDocumentValidation' is the only Learn statement about validator enforcement scope. The MCP server's explain_operation passes verbosity 'executionStats' and its aggregate explain uses the same stages[].$cursor layout the K32 template walks. No connection strings, cluster names or e-mail addresses are included._

## CLI, IaC and server versions (SME topic 11): researched 2026-11-25, 17 settled, 5 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Is `az cosmosdb mongocluster` (cosmosdb-preview extension) deprecated, or only superseded by `az documentdb mongocluster`? **engine_source.** Not deprecated: it is a live Preview group with no deprecation marker. The Learn reference (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/cosmosdb/mongocluster, ms.date 2026-09-01, updated_at 2026-04-07) says "This command group is in preview and under development"; all 12 commands show Status "Preview", none "Deprecated", and the page never mentions `az documentdb mongocluster`. In the extension source, `setup_mongocluster_commands` registers `command_group('cosmosdb mongocluster', ..., is_preview=True)` with no `deprecate_info` (src/cosmosdb-preview/azext_cosmosdb_preview/commands.py lines 437-449; by contrast `cosmosdb dts` on line 300 carries `deprecate_info=self.deprecate(redirect='cosmosdb copy', hide=True)`, so the owners do use redirects elsewhere). Locally (azure-cli 2.89.1, cosmosdb-preview 1.7.0) `az cosmosdb mongocluster --help` prints only the preview warning. It is superseded in practice: every Learn DocumentDB page uses `az documentdb mongocluster` (quickstart-cli, how-to-scale-cluster, development-loop); the legacy group's vendored SDK pins API 2024-03-01-preview (vendored_sdks/azure_mgmt_mongocluster/_configuration.py line 37) and lacks list-connection-strings, restore, replica, identity and microsoft-entra-user. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/cosmosdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/cosmosdb-preview/azext_cosmosdb_preview/commands.py (lines 300, 437-449); https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/cosmosdb-preview/azext_cosmosdb_preview/vendored_sdks/azure_mgmt_mongocluster/_configuration.py (line 37); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-cli
- Are both command groups still Preview? **engine_source.** `az cosmosdb mongocluster`: yes, Preview (Learn reference and commands.py `is_preview=True`). `az documentdb mongocluster`: the Learn reference (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster, ms.date 2026-09-01, updated_at 2026-11-17) says "Command group 'az documentdb' is in preview and under development" and marks all commands Preview, but that page is generated from the preview 1.1.0bX line. The extension's own history (src/documentdb/HISTORY.rst) reads: 1.0.0 "Stable release of the Azure DocumentDB extension" (PR Azure/azure-cli-extensions#10316 "Move DocumentDB extension to GA", merged 2026-09-16); 1.1.0b1 "Regenerate the extension with the 2026-06-15-preview API. Return the extension and its command surface to Preview status"; 1.1.0b2 current source (setup.py VERSION = '1.1.0b2', azext_metadata.json azext.isPreview true). The extension index (https://aka.ms/azure-cli-extension-index-v1) lists 1.0.0 with no isPreview flag and 1.0.0b1, 1.0.0b2, 1.1.0b1 and 1.1.0b2 with isPreview true, and Azure CLI installs the stable version by default (dynamic_install.py: "Preview version of extension is disabled by default for extension installation"). Observed locally 2026-11-25: installed documentdb 1.0.0 reports Preview False, `az documentdb mongocluster --help` shows no preview banner, and `az extension list-available` shows 1.1.0b2 preview=true as an available upgrade. PR #10605 (open, 2026-11-20) proposes releasing 1.1.0 as Stable on management API 2026-09-01 with all 29 commands classified Stable. Filings should therefore say: the reference docs show Preview; the version a fresh `az extension add --name documentdb` installs today (1.0.0) is GA-marked; do not describe the group as unconditionally GA or unconditionally Preview. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/HISTORY.rst; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/pull/10316; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/pull/10605; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/azext_metadata.json; https://aka.ms/azure-cli-extension-index-v1 (extensions.documentdb entries 1.0.0b1, 1.0.0b2, 1.0.0, 1.1.0b1, 1.1.0b2); local observation 2026-11-25: az version 2.89.1, az extension list, az extension list-available
- Is Azure CLI 2.75.0 the right minimum for the `documentdb` extension? **documented.** Yes. The Learn reference note reads: "This reference is part of the documentdb extension for the Azure CLI (version 2.75.0 or higher). The extension will automatically install the first time you run an az documentdb mongocluster command." The extension index lists azext.minCliCoreVersion 2.75.0 for every published documentdb version (1.0.0b1, 1.0.0b2, 1.0.0, 1.1.0b1, 1.1.0b2) and the source azext_metadata.json says "azext.minCliCoreVersion": "2.75.0". For comparison, cosmosdb-preview needs 2.17.1, Bicep deployment needs 2.20.0 (bicep/deploy-cli), and the `ValidationLevel` what-if switch needs 2.76.0 (same page). The kit's examples/azure-deployment/README.md line 29 says "Azure CLI 2.60 or later", which is enough for Bicep deploys but not for the extension the kit should name. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest; https://aka.ms/azure-cli-extension-index-v1; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/azext_metadata.json; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-resource-manager/bicep/deploy-cli
- Does first-use dynamic extension install prompt in non-interactive shells? **engine_source.** No prompt: without a TTY the Azure CLI installs the extension and continues. Source (Azure/azure-cli src/azure-cli-core/azure/cli/core/extension/dynamic_install.py): `_get_extension_use_dynamic_install_config` calls `verify_is_a_tty()`; on `NoTTYException` the comment reads "without prompt when it's not in tty" and `default_value = 'yes_without_prompt'`; with a TTY the default is 'yes_prompt'. In the no-prompt branch the CLI logs "The command requires the extension %s. It will be installed first." and installs (lines 214-216). Only if `extension.use_dynamic_install` is explicitly set to `yes_prompt` and no TTY exists does it exit with NoTTYError: "Unable to prompt for extension install confirmation as no tty available. Run 'az config set extension.use_dynamic_install=yes_without_prompt' ..." (lines 230-236). A second nuance: `dynamic_install_allow_preview` defaults to unset, logging "Preview version of extension is disabled by default for extension installation, enabled for modules without stable versions", so the stable 1.0.0 is what gets auto-installed. The Learn extensions page (updated_at 2025-05-21) documents the three config values and that dynamic install is on by default since 2.12.0, but never states the no-TTY behaviour. Observation: no experiment run executed an `az documentdb` command (the harness ran with Azure writes off), so there is no run log of the prompt; the project's loop/provision.sh installs the extension explicitly (`az extension add -n documentdb`, line 3 comment). Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli/blob/dev/src/azure-cli-core/azure/cli/core/extension/dynamic_install.py (lines 112-148, 212-236); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/azure-cli-extensions-overview; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/loop/provision.sh (line 3)
- Can clusters created with the old `az cosmosdb mongocluster` group be managed with `az documentdb mongocluster`? **arithmetic_or_definition.** Yes by definition: both groups are thin clients over the same ARM resource type `Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters`. The legacy group drives `vendored_sdks.azure_mgmt_mongocluster.operations.MongoClustersOperations` (commands.py lines 427-429) at API 2024-03-01-preview; the documentdb group's generated commands call the same URL template `/subscriptions/{}/resourcegroups/{}/providers/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters/{}` at api-version 2026-06-15-preview (aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/_show.py lines 26-28). The FAQ (ms.date 2026-11-06) adds: "The rename didn't change existing clusters, connection strings, or APIs." No Learn page states the cross-group compatibility in so many words, and no experiment run exercised it, so filings should phrase it as "same resource type, same control plane" rather than quote a doc. Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/cosmosdb-preview/azext_cosmosdb_preview/commands.py (lines 427-449); https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/_show.py (lines 26-28); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq
- Which `--server-version` values are accepted at create time (5.0, 6.0, 7.0, 8.0)? **documented.** The documented set is 5.0, 6.0, 7.0 and 8.0. FAQ (ms.date 2026-11-06, updated_at 2026-11-07): "Azure DocumentDB supports MongoDB wire protocol versions 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, and 8.0". Terraform azurerm_mongo_cluster docs: "`version` - (Optional) The version for the MongoDB Cluster. Possibles values are `5.0`, `6.0`, `7.0` and `8.0`" (and "version is required when create_mode is Default"), enforced in the provider by `validation.StringInSlice([]string{"5.0","6.0","7.0","8.0"})` (internal/services/mongocluster/resources/mongo_cluster/r_schema.go lines 246-254). The `az documentdb mongocluster create --server-version` argument is a free string with no enum (aaz _create.py lines 165-168; help: "The Mongo DB server version. Defaults to the latest available version if not specified"), and the ARM reference and the service's own OpenAPI spec (stable/2026-09-01/mongoCluster.json, MongoClusterProperties.serverVersion) describe `serverVersion` as `string` with the same default sentence. The legacy `az cosmosdb mongocluster create` hard-codes `server_version="5.0"` as its default (custom.py line 201; Learn page "Default value: 5.0"). Release notes date the versions: "v7 released" August 05, 2024; "v8 released" May 05, 2025. Whether the control plane still accepts a brand-new 5.0 or 6.0 cluster today was not observed in any run (see open item). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq; https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/azurerm/latest/docs/resources/mongo_cluster; https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/internal/services/mongocluster/resources/mongo_cluster/r_schema.go (lines 246-254); https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/_create.py (lines 165-168); https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/cosmosdb-preview/azext_cosmosdb_preview/custom.py (line 201); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters; https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/2026-09-01/mongoCluster.json; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes
- Is 8.0 the right default server version for the kit's templates? **documented.** Yes. The service default when the property is omitted is "the latest available version" (ARM reference `serverVersion`: "Defaults to the latest available version if not specified"; identical CLI help and OpenAPI description), and 8.0 is the newest version listed by the FAQ. Every current Learn provisioning page sets 8.0 explicitly: quickstart-cli (ms.date 2026-08-13) `--server-version 8.0`; quickstart-bicep (updated_at 2026-11-05) `serverVersion: '8.0'` on `Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters@2026-06-01`; quickstart-terraform `version = "8.0"`; quickstart-portal "MongoDB version | Select 8.0"; development-loop (updated_at 2026-11-23) `--server-version "8.0"`; high-performance-storage (updated_at 2026-11-05) `serverVersion: '8.0'` in Bicep, Terraform and REST. The kit's `serverVersion` default of '8.0' (skills/azure-deployment/references/bicep-cluster-template.md line 55, examples/azure-deployment/main.bicep line 48, terraform template `mongo_version` default "8.0") matches. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-cli; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-bicep; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-terraform; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-portal; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/development-loop; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-performance-storage
- Can a cluster's major server version be upgraded in place? **documented.** Yes. Learn how-to-upgrade-cluster (ms.date 2024-07-22, updated_at 2026-04-27): "Azure DocumentDB provide customers with a convenient self-service option to upgrade to the latest MongoDB version. This feature ensures a seamless upgrade path with just a click, allowing businesses to continue their operations without interruption." Steps: Overview blade, Upgrade button, "choose the MongoDB version you wish to upgrade to"; "The upgrade button will stay disabled if you're already using the latest version." Programmatically: `az documentdb mongocluster update` accepts `--server-version` (Learn reference; the update is a PATCH, "Only the properties specified in the request are modified"), and the azurerm provider updates in place: `version` has no ForceNew and r_update.go lines 112-113 set `payload.Properties.ServerVersion` on `HasChange("version")`. The Learn page does not list target versions, give a CLI example, or state whether downgrade is possible (see open item). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-upgrade-cluster; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest#az-documentdb-mongocluster-update; https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/internal/services/mongocluster/resources/mongo_cluster/r_update.go (lines 112-113); https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/internal/services/mongocluster/resources/mongo_cluster/r_schema.go (lines 246-254)
- Exact JSON paths in `az documentdb mongocluster show` output for serverVersion, compute.tier, sharding.shardCount, authConfig.allowedModes and highAvailability.targetMode **observed_in_runs.** The output is the ARM resource body, not flattened: `properties.serverVersion`, `properties.compute.tier`, `properties.sharding.shardCount`, `properties.authConfig.allowedModes` (array), `properties.highAvailability.targetMode`. Source: the generated schema in aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/_show.py declares `_schema_on_200.properties = AAZObjectType()` with no `client_flatten` flag (line 172; only the nested privateEndpointConnections element is flattened, lines 339-340) and maps `serialized_name="serverVersion"` (269-270), `compute.tier` (294-295), `sharding.shard_count serialized_name="shardCount"` (414-416), `auth_config.allowed_modes serialized_name="allowedModes"` (281-282), `high_availability.target_mode serialized_name="targetMode"` (322-323). Observed 2026-11-25 with the installed documentdb 1.0.0: `az documentdb mongocluster list -o json` returned those exact keys (properties.serverVersion '8.0', properties.compute.tier 'M30', properties.sharding.shardCount 1, properties.authConfig.allowedModes ['NativeAuth'], properties.highAvailability.targetMode 'Disabled', plus properties.storage.sizeGb/type, properties.provisioningState, properties.clusterStatus, properties.connectionString). Other sources already use these paths: Learn how-to-scale-cluster REST bodies (`properties.compute.tier`, `properties.storage.sizeGb`, `properties.highAvailability.targetMode`, `properties.sharding.shardCount`), loop/provision.sh (`--query "properties.connectionString"`), and filing H11's probe_cluster. Filing K20's phrase "read `serverVersion` from the output" should say `properties.serverVersion` (or `--query properties.serverVersion`). Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/blob/main/src/documentdb/azext_documentdb/aaz/latest/documentdb/mongocluster/_show.py (lines 172, 269-270, 281-282, 294-295, 322-323, 339-340, 414-416); local observation 2026-11-25: az documentdb mongocluster list -o json (documentdb extension 1.0.0, azure-cli 2.89.1); key structure only; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster; /Users/julietai/claude/azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/loop/provision.sh (line 48)
- Does Azure CLI compile Bicep on the client? **documented.** Yes. bicep/install (ms.date 2026-07-23): "When you use Azure CLI with Bicep, you have everything you need to deploy and decompile Bicep files. Azure CLI automatically installs the Bicep CLI when a command is executed that needs it. You must have Azure CLI version 2.20.0 or later"; "The Azure CLI installs a self-contained instance of the Bicep CLI ... The Azure CLI doesn't add Bicep CLI to your PATH." bicep/overview (ms.date 2026-01-08): "During deployment, the Bicep CLI converts a Bicep file into a Resource Manager JSON template." bicep/deploy-cli (ms.date 2026-05-27): "The Azure CLI doesn't currently support deploying remote Bicep files. You can use the Bicep CLI to build the Bicep file to a JSON template and then load the JSON file to a remote location", which only makes sense because the transpile happens on the client. This confirms filing H09's position that `az bicep version` (not `which bicep`) is the right preflight and that `az bicep build`/`lint` are local operations. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-resource-manager/bicep/install; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-resource-manager/bicep/overview; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-resource-manager/bicep/deploy-cli
- Billing fact: compute is charged per shard (node) and the per-node price is hourly **documented.** Per shard: compute-storage (ms.date 2026-07-27): "The selected compute and storage values apply to each shard in the cluster." how-to-scale-cluster (updated_at 2026-11-05): "Each physical shard in a cluster always has the same compute and storage configuration." high-availability (ms.date 2026-10-28): "When HA is enabled, Azure DocumentDB runs one standby shard for each primary shard in the cluster. Each primary and standby shard has the same compute and storage configuration." Pricing page (https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/documentdb/): "Each Azure DocumentDB cluster has one or more nodes", the compute table is headed "Price per Node" with "vCores per Node" and "Memory in GiBs per Node", and "HA can be disabled in dev/test scenarios to reduce the cost by 50%." Hourly: the pricing page shows prices with a "Display pricing by: Hour / Month" toggle, and the autoscale page states "Billing Frequency: Costs are calculated and billed hourly" (autoscale tier only). No Learn DocumentDB page writes the sentence "regular tiers are billed per node per hour"; the FAQ pricing answer says only "pricing is based on two things: compute (the cluster tier, in vCores and memory) and storage". Filings may state per-shard, per-node-hour pricing citing the pricing page, and should ask Learn to add the sentence. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compute-storage; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability; https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/documentdb/; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/autoscale; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq
- Billing fact: storage can grow but not shrink **documented.** Confirmed on three current pages. how-to-scale-cluster (ms.date 2026-10-28, updated_at 2026-11-05), Important box: "Storage size can only be increased, not decreased." compute-storage (ms.date 2026-07-27), Important: "You can only increase storage. After you scale up the storage size for a cluster, you can't decrease it." FAQ (updated_at 2026-11-07): "Can I decrease the storage size of my cluster? No. Storage size can only be increased, not decreased." Related constraints: compute tier can go up or down ("increase from M50 to M60 or decrease M50 to M40") except "Downgrade from regular compute tier to a burstable tier isn't supported"; only one physical shard can be added at a time; on Premium SSD v2, storage capacity "can be adjusted up to four times within a 24-hour period" and at most three times in a new cluster's first 24 hours (high-performance-storage, updated_at 2026-11-05). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compute-storage; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-performance-storage
- Billing fact: Free tier **documented.** free-tier (ms.date 2026-05-18, updated_at 2026-06-02): "The free tier lasts for the lifetime of your account"; "a dedicated MongoDB cluster with 32-GB storage"; "Users can provision a single free database server per subscription"; "available in limited set of regions only" (list on the page); restrictions: "High availability, Microsoft Entra ID, Backup / Restore, HNSW & DiskANN vector indexes, and Diagnostic Logging aren't supported", "Free tier clusters are paused after 60 days of inactivity", "Transition from a paid tier account to a free tier accounts isn't supported"; upgrade to a paid tier keeps data, connection string and network rules. FAQ agrees ("Each Azure subscription can have one free tier cluster", "You can upgrade an Azure DocumentDB free tier cluster to any paid tier in place"). The Terraform provider and the kit's terraform template accept `compute_tier = "Free"`. The limitations page's free-tier section (updated_at 2026-11-17) partly disagrees on which paid tier unlocks what (see doc_fixes). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/free-tier; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/azurerm/latest/docs/resources/mongo_cluster
- Billing fact: M25 is a burstable tier **documented.** Yes, and so are M10 and M20. compute-storage table (ms.date 2026-07-27): M10 "1 (burstable)" vCore, 2 GiB; M20 "2 (burstable)", 4 GiB; M25 "2 (burstable)", 8 GiB; M30 2 vCores, 8 GiB (not burstable). "Provisioning a burstable tier is as straightforward as provisioning regular tiers; you only need to choose 'M10,' 'M20,' or 'M25' in the cluster tier option." limitations (updated_at 2026-11-17), Tier limits: "M10, M20, and M25 service tiers ... Supports one physical shard (node) only. Designed for Dev/Test use cases; in-region high availability (HA) isn't supported. Supported storage sizes include 32 GiB, 64 GiB, and 128 GiB. Once cluster is scaled to M30 tier or higher, the cluster can't be scaled back down to M10, M20, or M25." Replication "isn't available on clusters with burstable compute or Free tier". The Azure pricing page tags only M25 as "2 (Burstable)" and shows M10 and M20 without the tag (doc inconsistency, see doc_fixes). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compute-storage; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster; https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/documentdb/
- Which create-time parameters are required by `az documentdb mongocluster create` (relevant to the kit's one-shot and to K34) **documented.** Required: `--admin-password`, `--admin-user`, `--cluster-name/--name`, `--high-availability {Disabled, SameZone, ZoneRedundantPreferred}`, `--resource-group`, `--shard-count`, `--storage-size`, `--tier`. Optional: `--server-version`, `--storage-type {PremiumSSD, PremiumSSDv2}`, `--auth-allowed-modes` (NativeAuth, MicrosoftEntraID), `--public-network-access`, `--preview-features`, `--location` (defaults to the resource group's), `--data-api-mode`, `--encryption`, `--mi-user-assigned`, `--network-bypass-mode`, `--no-wait`, `--tags`. Confirmed locally with `az documentdb mongocluster create --help` on 1.0.0 (`--high-availability [Required]`). The quickstart-cli example passes all of these plus `--preview-features GeoReplicas --no-wait` then `az documentdb mongocluster wait --created`. `list-connection-strings` takes `--cluster-name` (no `-n`); `restore` takes `--source-cluster`, `--restore-time`, `--storage-type` and explicitly does not accept server-version, tier, storage-size or shard-count overrides. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster?view=azure-cli-latest#az-documentdb-mongocluster-create; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-cli; local observation 2026-11-25: az documentdb mongocluster create --help (documentdb 1.0.0)
- Is 2026-09-01 a real, published stable api-version for Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters, and what changed in it? **engine_source.** Yes. Azure/azure-rest-api-specs PR #47576 "[Microsoft.DocumentDB] MongoCluster stable API 2026-09-01 for October 2026 SDK release" merged 2026-11-17T16:29Z; its body states "New stable API version: `2026-09-01`", "Target SDK release: October 2026, stable SDKs" and "this adds a stable version and retains all previous versions". On main, specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/ holds 2024-07-01, 2025-09-01, 2026-06-01 and 2026-09-01, and main.tsp declares the version list through v2026_09_01 plus a @previewVersion v2026_10_15_preview. The 2026-09-01 mongoCluster.json has info.version 2026-09-01, `serverVersion` as an untyped string ("Defaults to the latest available version if not specified"), `HighAvailabilityMode` enum ['Disabled','SameZone','ZoneRedundantPreferred'] (modelAsString true), `previewFeatures` described as "List of private endpoint connections." with `PreviewFeature` enum ['GeoReplicas'] (modelAsString true), and one substantive change versus 2026-06-01: MongoCluster.tsp (StorageProperties) removes the old `type` at v2026_09_01 (`@removed`/`@renamedFrom` on `oldType`) and re-adds `type` with the new description "The type of storage to provision the cluster servers with. Defaults to 'PremiumSSDv2' when omitted during cluster creation. Omitting this property during an update leaves the storage type unchanged." (the 2026-06-01 spec's description is just "The type of storage to provision the cluster servers with."). The Learn ARM reference (updated_at 2026-10-20) still lists 2026-06-01 as the latest stable and 2026-06-15-preview as the latest preview, with no 2026-09-01 entry, so Learn lags the spec repo; PR Azure/azure-cli-extensions#10605 (open) targets this 2026-09-01 API for the documentdb extension 1.1.0. The `previewFeatures` copy-paste description originates in MongoCluster.tsp line 203, so that fix belongs in the spec repo, not only on Learn. Not observed: no run or local command exercised api-version 2026-09-01 against the control plane. Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/pull/47576; https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/2026-09-01/mongoCluster.json (info.version; definitions MongoClusterProperties.serverVersion, MongoClusterProperties.previewFeatures, StorageProperties.type, HighAvailabilityMode, PreviewFeature); https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/2026-06-01/mongoCluster.json (definitions StorageProperties); https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/MongoCluster.tsp (lines 203-205, 299-313, 345-358); https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/main.tsp (version enum, lines 50-67); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters; https://github.com/Azure/azure-cli-extensions/pull/10605
- Is `SameZone` part of the service's published HighAvailabilityMode contract? **engine_source.** Yes. The TypeSpec source of the resource provider (MongoCluster.tsp lines 345-358, `@added(Versions.v2024_07_01) union HighAvailabilityMode`) defines three values with doc comments: "Disabled" ("High availability mode is disabled. This mode is can see availability impact during faults or maintenance and is not recommended for production."), "SameZone" ("High availability mode is enabled, where each server in a shard is placed in the same availability zone.") and "ZoneRedundantPreferred" ("High availability mode is enabled and preferences ZoneRedundant if availability zones capacity is available in the region, otherwise falls-back to provisioning with SameZone."). The newest stable OpenAPI (2026-09-01) carries the same enum, the Learn ARM reference lists `targetMode` as 'Disabled' | 'SameZone' | 'ZoneRedundantPreferred', the CLI `--high-availability` accepts SameZone, and Learn high-availability and how-to-scale-cluster say "You can explicitly request SameZone through supported nonportal clients or APIs". This settles that SameZone is in the contract and that Learn's wording matches the spec. It does not settle whether the control plane accepts an explicit SameZone request today, because terraform-provider-azurerm r_schema.go (line 204) says "Confirmed with service team the SameZone is currently not supported" and allows only Disabled and ZoneRedundantPreferred (see open item). Sources: https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/MongoCluster.tsp (lines 345-358); https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/stable/2026-09-01/mongoCluster.json (definitions.HighAvailabilityMode); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-availability; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-scale-cluster; https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm/blob/main/internal/services/mongocluster/resources/mongo_cluster/r_schema.go (line 204)

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Do the cosmosdb-preview owners plan to mark `az cosmosdb mongocluster` deprecated with a redirect to `az documentdb mongocluster` (and when)? Why open: No issue or PR in Azure/azure-cli-extensions mentions deprecating or redirecting the mongocluster group (gh search for "mongocluster" issues returned nothing; the only mongocluster PRs are #5860, which added the group, and the documentdb extension's), and the cosmosdb-preview HISTORY.rst mentions the group only at 0.24.0 "Create and manage mongo clusters"; the group is registered with is_preview=True and no deprecate_info. Best guess: File X01 as a request: propose `deprecate_info=self.deprecate(redirect='documentdb mongocluster')` on the group, falling back to a cross-link in both groups' help text; keep the word 'superseded' and drop 'deprecated'.
2. Is a server-version downgrade (for example 8.0 to 7.0) blocked by the control plane, and what error does it return? Why open: how-to-upgrade-cluster only describes upgrading to a newer version and says the button is disabled on the latest version; neither it, the CLI reference, the ARM reference, the OpenAPI spec nor the Terraform docs state that downgrade is rejected. The strongest public statement is a Microsoft Q&A accepted answer (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/answers/questions/5860936/..., answered 2026-04-16 by an account labelled "Microsoft External Staff - Moderator"): "Azure Cosmos DB for MongoDB vCore clusters do not support version downgrades" and "Version changes for vCore clusters are upgrade-only and cannot be reversed"; the asker's Free-tier cluster was left in clusterStatus UpdateFailed / provisioningState Failed with 409 Conflict on every ARM operation and port 10260 timing out, a second user reported the same on 2026-06-16, and the answer cites no documentation. Q&A is supporting evidence only, and no run attempted a downgrade. Best guess: Treat `--server-version` as upgrade-only in kit text ("set at create time, upgradable to a newer version later; do not lower it") and ask Learn to state the downgrade rule and the UpdateFailed failure mode on how-to-upgrade-cluster.
3. Does the control plane still accept `serverVersion` 5.0 and 6.0 for brand-new clusters, or only for existing ones? Why open: The FAQ lists 5.0, 6.0, 7.0 and 8.0 as supported and the Terraform provider validates all four, but the CLI argument is an unvalidated string and Learn gives no per-version lifecycle or retirement dates (WebSearch restricted to learn.microsoft.com found none). Supporting, not settling: the Learn ARM reference's Terraform AzAPI sample (updated_at 2026-10-20) creates a `Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters@2025-09-01` cluster with `serverVersion = "5.0"` (M30, shardCount 1, previewFeatures ['ShardRebalancer']), and the spec repo's own stable/2026-09-01 example MongoClusters_Create.json uses `"serverVersion": "5.0"`; both are samples, not lifecycle statements, and no run created a non-8.0 cluster. Best guess: Kit templates may list the four values as allowed (K20) but should default to 8.0 and say 'check the FAQ for the current list'.
4. Which api-version should the kit's Bicep templates pin: 2025-09-01 (kit today), 2026-06-01 (Learn quickstart-bicep, how-to-scale-cluster and high-performance-storage REST bodies, azurerm provider) or 2026-09-01 (newest stable in Azure/azure-rest-api-specs, merged 2026-11-17)? Why open: The existence of 2026-09-01 is now settled (see settled item), so this is a choice, not a fact: the Learn ARM reference (updated_at 2026-10-20) and every Learn sample still use 2026-06-01, no Learn page documents 2026-09-01 or its new storage.type default sentence, the azurerm provider and the GA documentdb CLI extension 1.0.0 are not yet on 2026-09-01 (CLI PR #10605 is open), and no run exercised 2026-09-01 against the control plane. Best guess: Move the kit to 2026-06-01 now to match the Learn quickstart, the Learn ARM reference and the azurerm provider; switch to 2026-09-01 once the Learn ARM reference lists it (and then rely on its documented storage.type default).
5. Is `SameZone` HA a supported request value today? The contract and Learn say yes; the azurerm provider source says the service team confirmed it is not supported. Why open: The published contract (MongoCluster.tsp HighAvailabilityMode, stable 2026-09-01 enum, Learn ARM reference, CLI `--high-availability`) and Learn high-availability / how-to-scale-cluster (both updated 2026-10/11: "You can explicitly request SameZone through supported nonportal clients or APIs") include SameZone, while terraform-provider-azurerm r_schema.go line 204 carries "Confirmed with service team the SameZone is currently not supported" and allows only Disabled and ZoneRedundantPreferred; the schema file's November 2026 commits (#33730, #33963, #33972) kept that comment and no issue or PR in hashicorp/terraform-provider-azurerm discusses SameZone for mongo_cluster. Two primary sources disagree on behaviour and no run tried SameZone. Best guess: Kit keeps recommending ZoneRedundantPreferred and notes SameZone as 'defined by the API and accepted by the CLI, not exposed by azurerm'; raise the contradiction with the service team.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/cosmosdb/mongocluster (az cosmosdb mongocluster reference, updated_at 2026-04-07): Never mentions `az documentdb mongocluster` or Azure DocumentDB; `--server-version` shows "Default value: 5.0" while the current group defaults to the latest version; parameter names differ (--shard-node-tier, --administrator-login). Add a 'superseded by az documentdb mongocluster (documentdb extension)' note and cross-link (filing X01). Generated from extension help text, so the fix is in Azure/azure-cli-extensions.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/documentdb/mongocluster (az documentdb mongocluster reference, updated_at 2026-11-17): Shows every command as Preview because it is generated from the 1.1.0bX preview line, while `az extension add --name documentdb` installs stable 1.0.0 (no preview banner). Add a note on which version the page documents, and cross-link the legacy group. The group help 'Manage Azure DocumentDB mongo clusters.' could name the resource type Microsoft.DocumentDB/mongoClusters.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cli/azure/azure-cli-extensions-overview (updated_at 2025-05-21): Lists use_dynamic_install=yes_prompt as the value that enables dynamic install (on by default since 2.12.0) but never states that the effective default becomes yes_without_prompt when no TTY is present (azure-cli dynamic_install.py), nor that preview extension versions are skipped unless dynamic_install_allow_preview is set. Scripts and CI authors need both facts.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-upgrade-cluster (ms.date 2024-07-22): Portal-only; does not list the selectable target versions, does not show `az documentdb mongocluster update --server-version` or the Terraform `version` change, and does not state whether downgrade is supported or what happens when it is attempted (Q&A 5860936 reports a cluster stuck in UpdateFailed with 409 Conflict). The title/description say 'to latest version' while the UI lets you pick a version.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations (updated_at 2026-11-17), Free tier limitations: "Backup / Restore not supported (available in M25+)" conflicts with the FAQ (updated_at 2026-11-07): "point-in-time restore for up to 35 days for Production tiers (M30 and larger SKUs)". Same section still says HNSW "available in M40+" while the vector limits on the same page and the release notes say M30+ (already recorded under topic 7). Which tier is right is an engineering question.
- https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/documentdb/: Compute table tags only M25 as "2 (Burstable)"; Learn compute-storage marks M10, M20 and M25 as burstable. Also the only hourly-billing statement on Learn is on the autoscale page; neither the pricing page text nor the FAQ states in words that regular tiers are billed per node per hour (the pricing page only shows an Hour/Month toggle).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/faq (updated_at 2026-11-07), 'How does pricing work': Says pricing is compute plus storage but not that the chosen tier and storage are billed for every physical shard (and again for every standby shard with HA). Add the per-shard sentence from compute-storage and the HA 50% statement from the pricing page.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/templates/microsoft.documentdb/mongoclusters (updated_at 2026-10-20): Does not list stable api-version 2026-09-01 (merged in Azure/azure-rest-api-specs PR #47576 on 2026-11-17) or preview 2026-10-15-preview, so 'Latest' still resolves to 2026-06-15-preview / 2026-06-01. `previewFeatures` is described as "List of private endpoint connections." (copy-paste error) and enumerates only 'GeoReplicas', while Learn pages use FullTextSearch and ShardRebalancer and the CLI says names are free-form strings. `serverVersion` is an untyped string with no list of accepted values. The page's own AzAPI sample pins 2025-09-01 and serverVersion "5.0" while every Learn how-to uses 2026-06-01 and 8.0.
- https://github.com/Azure/azure-rest-api-specs/blob/main/specification/mongocluster/resource-manager/Microsoft.DocumentDB/MongoCluster/MongoCluster.tsp (line 203) and every generated mongoCluster.json including stable/2026-09-01: `previewFeatures` carries the doc comment "List of private endpoint connections." (copied from privateEndpointConnections); this is the origin of the same error on the Learn ARM reference and in the generated SDKs. The `PreviewFeature` union lists only GeoReplicas although Learn documents FullTextSearch and ShardRebalancer as preview-feature names (modelAsString true, so they are accepted, but undocumented in the contract).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/high-performance-storage (updated_at 2026-11-05) and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compute-storage (updated_at 2026-07-27): Neither page states which storage type a cluster gets when `storage.type` is omitted. The stable 2026-09-01 OpenAPI spec now says "Defaults to 'PremiumSSDv2' when omitted during cluster creation. Omitting this property during an update leaves the storage type unchanged." Learn should state the default (and whether it applies to Free and burstable tiers, since a Q&A thread reports Free tier rejecting PremiumSSDv2) so the kit's 'Premium SSD v2 is the default' sentence has a Learn citation.
- https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/azurerm/latest/docs/resources/mongo_cluster: `version` lists possible values and says it is required when create_mode is Default, but not that changing it performs an in-place PATCH (source: no ForceNew, r_update.go sets ServerVersion) and that the service default is 'latest available'; `high_availability_mode` omits SameZone with no explanation, contradicting the API contract (MongoCluster.tsp) and Learn's statement that SameZone can be requested through non-portal clients.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated_at 2026-06-02): No entry records the documentdb Azure CLI extension (1.0.0b1 2026-07, GA 1.0.0 2026-09), the Terraform azurerm_mongo_cluster resource, or the stable management API versions 2026-06-01 and 2026-09-01; the 'Items tagged [Preview] require a support request' note is stale (already recorded under topic 7).
- Learn URL hygiene: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-azure-cli: Returns 404; the CLI quickstart lives at /azure/documentdb/quickstart-cli (ms.date 2026-08-13). Filings and the kit should link quickstart-cli.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/storage/storage-premium-ssdv2-limitations.md:28-33`: `az documentdb mongo-cluster restore` uses a hyphenated group token that does not exist (the group is `mongocluster`), and the comment "In the restore template, set storage.type = 'PremiumSSDv2'" is stale: `restore` takes `--storage-type PremiumSSDv2` directly and its help says overrides for server version, tier, storage size and shard count are not supported.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/azure-deployment/SKILL.md:214-245 (Step 6b) and :15-45 (Step 0)`: The CLI one-shot uses `az resource create --api-version 2025-09-01` instead of `az documentdb mongocluster create`; the strings `az documentdb mongocluster`, `az extension add --name documentdb` and the 2.75.0 floor appear nowhere in the kit; the preflight never checks the extension or `az bicep version`. Learn's own quickstart-cli and development-loop use the extension (K34).
- `documentdb-agent-kit/examples/azure-deployment/README.md:29`: "Azure CLI 2.60 or later" is below the 2.75.0 minimum of the documentdb extension (Learn reference note; index.json azext.minCliCoreVersion). Adequate only for Bicep deploys (2.20.0).
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/azure-deployment/SKILL.md:157, references/bicep-cluster-template.md:54-55, references/terraform-cluster-template.md:90-94`: `serverVersion`/`mongo_version` default '8.0' is correct, but no allowed list; the documented set is 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, 8.0 (FAQ; azurerm StringInSlice) and the service default is 'latest available'. Add `@allowed` / `validation` and the note that the version is per cluster and upgradable in place, upgrade-only (K20).
- `documentdb-agent-kit/examples/azure-deployment/main.bicep:53,77 and skills/*/... (10 places pin mongoClusters@2025-09-01)`: Learn quickstart-bicep (updated 2026-11-05), how-to-scale-cluster and high-performance-storage REST/Bicep bodies and the azurerm provider all use api-version 2026-06-01, and the spec repo has since published stable 2026-09-01 (PR #47576, 2026-11-17). The kit's 2025-09-01 is a valid stable version but two stable releases behind. Not wrong, but worth aligning to 2026-06-01 when the templates are next touched (see open item on 2026-09-01).
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/storage/storage-premium-ssdv2-limitations.md:7 and skills/storage/SKILL.md:16`: "Premium SSD v2 is the default choice for new Azure DocumentDB clusters" / "the default recommendation" is stated without a source. It is now backed by the service contract (stable 2026-09-01 spec: storage.type "Defaults to 'PremiumSSDv2' when omitted during cluster creation") but by no Learn page, and the kit should distinguish 'service default when omitted' from 'our recommendation'; the kit's own templates set `type` explicitly, which is the safe pattern given the 2026-06-01 spec says nothing about a default.
- `azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/findings/phase4/filings/K20-document-the-serverversion-choice-in-the-azure-deployme.md:45`: Says to "read `serverVersion` from the output" of `az documentdb mongocluster show`; the key is `properties.serverVersion` (observed; aaz schema does not flatten properties). The `az resource show --query properties.serverVersion` form in the same cell is correct.
- `azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/findings/phase4/filings/H11-run-environment-note-and-inventory-state-the-pinning-po.md:93-96,131`: Open question 1 can be closed: the probe_cluster paths compute.tier, sharding.shardCount, serverVersion, authConfig.allowedModes, highAvailability.targetMode under `properties` match both the generated _show.py schema and the live output observed 2026-11-25.
- `azure-documentdb-agent-readiness/findings/phase4/filings/X01-az-cosmosdb-mongocluster-help-text-mark-it-superseded-b.md`: Constraint 'keep both groups described as Preview' needs nuance: the documentdb extension 1.0.0 that installs by default is Stable (PR #10316), only the 1.1.0bX line and the generated Learn page are Preview, and PR #10605 proposes a stable 1.1.0. Wording should be 'the reference page currently shows Preview'.

Observation scope unchanged: no experiment run executed an `az documentdb` or `az cosmosdb mongocluster` command (Azure writes were off); live observations on 2026-11-25 were read-only (`az version` 2.89.1; extensions documentdb 1.0.0, cosmosdb-preview 1.7.0; --help banners; az extension list-available; key structure of `az documentdb mongocluster list -o json` with identifying values redacted).
