## Local gateway container (documentdb-local): feature parity, tag pinning, canonical URI, MCP README example (SME topic 12): researched 2026-11-25, 10 settled, 3 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Does the documentdb-local image support cosmosSearch vector indexes of all three kinds (IVF, HNSW, DiskANN) like the managed service? **engine_source.** IVF and HNSW yes; DiskANN no. The open-source engine registers exactly two vector index kinds, 'vector-ivf' (access method ivfflat) and 'vector-hnsw' (access method hnsw), in a table with three empty trailing slots (pg_documentdb/src/vector/vector_index_kind_impl.c:109-133, engine clone at commit a5f1636, 2026-11-18). DiskANN appears in the OSS tree only as feature-counter names and error strings (pg_documentdb/include/utils/feature_counter.h:353,513; pg_documentdb_gw/.../responses/pg.rs:399-401); no DiskANN access method is installed. The documentdb-local image is built by packaging/gateway/docker/Dockerfile_documentdb_local on debian:trixie-slim (build_gateway.yml:104,112) and installs only postgresql-NN, -cron, -pgvector, -postgis-3 and -rum from the PGDG apt repository (Dockerfile_documentdb_local:89-94; the extension .deb's Depends line lists the same set, packaging/deb/common/control:9). The OSS reference page for $vectorSearch documents only 'vector-hnsw' (m 2-100, default 16; efConstruction 4-1000, default 64) and 'vector-ivf' (numLists 1-32768, default 100) and does not mention DiskANN. The Learn OSS overview (ms.date 2025-02-07, updated_at 2026-04-27) says vector search is 'Powered by the pg_vector PostgreSQL extension'. The Learn limitations page (ms.date 2026-11-04, updated_at 2026-11-17) says 'HNSW and DiskANN are available on M30 and greater cluster tiers' for Azure and says nothing about the local image. (A run-log observation quoted in the first draft was withdrawn: runs/17-ai-survey-response-analyzer/20261124-205518/02-readiness.md contains no statement about local DiskANN parity; its DiskANN mentions, Q08 and S04, concern the Azure cluster.) Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/reference/operators/aggregation/$vectorsearch/; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/oss; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/vector/vector_index_kind_impl.c:109-133; engine clone pg_documentdb/include/utils/feature_counter.h:353,513; engine clone packaging/gateway/docker/Dockerfile_documentdb_local:89-94; engine clone packaging/deb/common/control:9; engine clone .github/workflows/build_gateway.yml:104,112
- Which vector compression modes (half, pq) and dimension caps actually apply on documentdb-local by default? **engine_source.** Caps (documented on the OSS $vectorSearch page): 'An uncompressed index tops out at 2000', 'half' allows 4000 and 'pq' allows 16000 dimensions; exceeding the cap fails with 'field cannot have more than 2000 dimensions for vector index'; a queryVector 'cannot be longer than 16000 elements'. The page says 'Both compression modes are gated behind server configuration' and half 'additionally requires a pgvector build with half-vector support'. Engine source (a5f1636) identifies that configuration: two GUCs whose compiled defaults are both true, DEFAULT_ENABLE_VECTOR_COMPRESSION_HALF true and DEFAULT_ENABLE_VECTOR_COMPRESSION_PQ true (pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c:156-161, registered as <prefix>.enableVectorCompressionHalf and .enableVectorCompressionPQ at 769-779). create_indexes.c:3905-3938 raises 'Compression type 'half' is not enabled.' only when the GUC is false, then 'The compression type 'half' is currently unsupported.' only when IsPgvectorHalfVectorAvailable() is false; that function checks for public.vector_to_halfvec, 'introduced in 0.7.0' (pg_documentdb/src/vector/vector_utilities.c:464-470). The documentdb-local image takes pgvector from the PGDG apt package on debian:trixie (Dockerfile_documentdb_local:84-92), and the trixie-pgdg index served postgresql-15..18-pgvector 0.8.6/0.8.7 on 2026-11-25 (observed), so half-vector support is present. (The git pin PGVECTOR_VERSION=v0.8.1 in scripts/setup_versions.sh:45 feeds the Build-Ubuntu CI image and source builds, not the published local image; both are >= 0.7.0.) 'pq' passes the GUC check but is rejected per kind: 'Compression type 'pq' is not supported for ivf index' and '... for hnsw index' (vector_index_kind_impl.c:252-257, 330-335), and no DiskANN kind exists, so pq is unusable on documentdb-local. documentdb-local/conf and documentdb-local/scripts contain no override of these GUCs (grep, 2026-11-25). Net: documentdb-local supports IVF and HNSW up to 2,000 dimensions uncompressed and 4,000 with compression 'half' by default; pq and 16,000 dimensions are Azure-only (DiskANN). Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/reference/operators/aggregation/$vectorsearch/; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c:156-161,769-779; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_indexes.c:3905-3938; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/vector/vector_utilities.c:464-470; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/vector/vector_index_kind_impl.c:252-257,330-335; engine clone packaging/gateway/docker/Dockerfile_documentdb_local:84-92; engine clone scripts/setup_versions.sh:45; https://apt.postgresql.org/pub/repos/apt/dists/trixie-pgdg/main/binary-amd64/Packages.gz (observed 2026-11-25: pgvector 0.8.6-1.pgdg13+2 and 0.8.7-1.pgdg13+1/+2 for PG15-18)
- Does the documentdb-local image support createSearchIndexes and the full-text $search stage? **engine_source.** No. The OSS $search reference says '$search in DocumentDB is a vector search stage. It does not accept text-search operators'; the stage 'carries exactly one search operator - cosmosSearch, or the deprecated knnBeta', and a text operator is rejected with 'Unrecognized $search option: text'. In the gateway, the default DataClient implementation of execute_create_search_indexes and execute_wait_for_search_index returns ErrorCode::CommandNotSupported with the message "Command 'createSearchIndexes' not supported." (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/postgres/data_client.rs:528-548); the request type is parsed (requests/request_type.rs:69,305) but has no OSS backend. The engine's search operator enum lists only COSMOS_SEARCH, KNN_BETA and 'SEARCH_OPERATOR_EXTENDED' described as 'any operator provided by extension layers' (pg_documentdb/include/aggregation/bson_aggregation_search.h:27-36), i.e. the full-text operators come from a hosted-only layer. On Azure, full-text search is a preview feature created with createSearchIndexes and queried with $search (Learn limitations page, 'Full-text search limitations (preview)'). Note the error text differs: the local gateway says "Command 'createSearchIndexes' not supported." while an Azure cluster without the preview enabled returned code 115 'Search index creation is not supported yet' (observed, runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049, recorded in DECISIONS.md topic 7). Both are code 115 CommandNotSupported. Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/reference/operators/aggregation/$search/; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/postgres/data_client.rs:528-548; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/request_type.rs:69,305; engine clone pg_documentdb/include/aggregation/bson_aggregation_search.h:27-36; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations; runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049 (via findings/phase4/DECISIONS.md)
- Does the documentdb-local image support multi-document transactions? **engine_source.** Yes with the default read concern; no with readConcern level 'snapshot'. The OSS transaction-commands reference documents commitTransaction and abortTransaction ('Drivers start a transaction by adding transaction metadata to its first operation, not by sending a separate begin command'; a missing active transaction produces NoSuchTransaction). Snapshot read concern is rejected by default: the gateway reads 'mongoAllowTransactionSnapshot' with default false (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/pg_configuration.rs:416-417) and validation then returns code 115 CommandNotSupported "'Snapshot' read concern is not supported" (requests/validation.rs:110-120; open engine issue documentdb/documentdb#736, labels gateway, documentdb-local, documentdb-1.0, bug, filed 2026-09-03; maintainer comment 2026-10-05: 'The fix is the gateway default for mongoAllowTransactionSnapshot (currently false) ... Targeting 1.0; this needs a gateway change'; still open, last bot ping 2026-11-17 unanswered). Issue #803 (closed 2026-10-20 as duplicate of #736) reports against image digest sha256:4a341d... (DocumentDB 1.0.0) that 'Transactions with the default read concern work: commit and abort both behaved correctly', and that the gateway reports hello.msg 'isdbgrid' so drivers treat it as a mongos and enable transactions, whereas directConnection=true makes the Ruby driver refuse transactions as a standalone. Azure caps transaction lifetime at 30 seconds (Learn limitations page); no source states the local gateway's limit. Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/reference/commands/sessions/transaction-commands/; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/736; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/803; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/pg_configuration.rs:416-417; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/requests/validation.rs:110-120; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations
- Does the documentdb-local image support change streams? **engine_source.** No. The OSS $changeStream reference states 'DocumentDB does not support $changeStream' and that any pipeline using it, including db.collection.watch(), fails with 'Stage $changeStream is not supported yet in native pipeline' (CommandNotSupported, code 115). In source, HandleChangeStream raises exactly that error unless IsChangeStreamFeatureAvailableAndCompatible() is true, which returns false whenever no hosting hook is installed (pg_documentdb/src/aggregation/bson_aggregation_pipeline.c:5322-5328; pg_documentdb/src/api_hooks.c:583-590), and the gateway's enableChangeStreams setting defaults to false (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs:109-111). Feature request documentdb/documentdb#50 (opened 2025-02-02) is still open; maintainers wrote on 2026-06-24 'targeted for Q3 2026 (July to September)' and a user asked for an update on 2026-10-06 with no reply. On Azure DocumentDB change streams are documented as available (Learn change-streams page, ms.date 2026-11-16, updated_at 2026-11-17), with multi-shard change streams and updateDescription marked preview; that page does not mention local development. Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/reference/operators/aggregation/$changestream/; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/aggregation/bson_aggregation_pipeline.c:5322-5328; engine clone pg_documentdb/src/api_hooks.c:583-590; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/configuration/dynamic.rs:109-111; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/issues/50; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/change-streams
- Does the documentdb-local image support Microsoft Entra ID (MONGODB-OIDC) authentication? **engine_source.** No. The OSS gateway's AuthenticationManager registers only the SCRAM-SHA-256 handler (pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/auth/manager.rs:159-166; the auth module contains scram.rs and no OIDC handler) and exposes register_scheme() for extension layers; the string 'MONGODB-OIDC' occurs only in a unit test (service/connection_loop/routing.rs:146). The hello/isMaster response advertises saslSupportedMechs ['SCRAM-SHA-256'] only (processor/ismaster.rs:118,193). The OSS docs describe the gateway as supporting 'SCRAM-SHA-256 authentication and TLS' (documentdb.io Docker quick start) and the Learn OSS page lists 'Authentication mechanism: Includes SCRAM'. Entra ID is documented only as an Azure cluster feature (Learn RBAC page, ms.date 2026-03-11, updated_at 2026-11-23; Learn limitations: free tier 'Microsoft Entra ID not supported', no local mention). The documentdb-local runtime settings table has no identity-provider option (documentdb-local/scripts/documentdb_local_settings.sh:29-48). Sources: engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/auth/manager.rs:159-166; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/ismaster.rs:118,193; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/service/connection_loop/routing.rs:146; engine clone documentdb-local/scripts/documentdb_local_settings.sh:29-48; https://documentdb.io/docs/getting-started/docker/; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/oss; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-role-based-access-control; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations
- Which versioned tags exist for ghcr.io/documentdb/documentdb/documentdb-local, what does :latest point to, and is pinning recommended? **documented.** The OSS DocumentDB Local page states: 'latest and 1.2 point to pg17-1.2.0'; 'Images for PostgreSQL 15 to 18 are tagged pg15-1.2.0 to pg18-1.2.0, for Linux amd64 and arm64'; 'Use a versioned tag when you need a reproducible setup.' The 1.2 release notes say 'DocumentDB v1.2-0 was released on 2026-11-12' and 'start the 1.2 image on the 1.0 volume; it updates the extensions at start. Going back to 1.0 on that volume isn't supported.' The 1.0 release notes say 'pg15-1.0.0 to pg18-1.0.0; latest is pg17-1.0.0'. Registry observation (anonymous GHCR tags/list and manifest HEADs, 2026-11-25): 435 tags; named tags are latest, stable, 1.0, 1.2, test, and pg{15,16,17,18}-{0.109.0 ... 0.117.0, 1.0-rc1, 1.0-rc2, 1.0.0, 1.0.1, 1.2.0}; there are no bare 1.0.0 or 1.2.0 tags (404). latest, 1.2 and pg17-1.2.0 share manifest-list digest sha256:1d40546c...; stable, 1.0, pg17-1.0.0 and pg17-1.0.1 all share sha256:5c3df9c6... The inference that pg17-1.0.0 was re-pointed at the 1.0.1 build and that 'stable' tracks 1.0.x rests on these identical digests, not on any documented statement; neither stable nor 1.0 is mentioned on the OSS page. The release workflow tags from the control-file version (documentdb_release.yml:58-68). The older image name ghcr.io/microsoft/documentdb/documentdb-local still exists (87 tags) but its newest tags are dated 2025-08-25 and its 'latest' is a 2025 build; the kit CHANGELOG (2026-10-19) moved the kit off it. Learn's development-loop tutorial (ms.date 2026-04-15, updated_at 2026-11-23), the engine README and the OSS quick start all still show ':latest'. Sources: https://documentdb.io/docs/documentdb-local/; https://documentdb.io/docs/getting-started/release-notes-1.2; https://documentdb.io/docs/getting-started/release-notes-1.0; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/releases/tag/v1.2-0; GHCR tags/list and manifests for documentdb/documentdb/documentdb-local and microsoft/documentdb/documentdb-local (observed 2026-11-25); engine clone .github/workflows/documentdb_release.yml:58-68; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/development-loop; kit clone CHANGELOG.md:3-11
- Is authMechanism=SCRAM-SHA-256 required in the canonical local connection string? **engine_source.** Not required; harmless if present. The gateway advertises saslSupportedMechs ['SCRAM-SHA-256'] only and registers only that handler (ismaster.rs:118,193; auth/manager.rs:159-166), and the MongoDB connection-string reference states 'Without an explicit authMechanism, MongoDB tries SCRAM-SHA-256 and falls back to SCRAM-SHA-1 if that fails', so negotiation lands on SCRAM-SHA-256. Learn's development-loop tutorial gives the local string without it: mongodb://<admin-username>:<admin-password>@localhost:10260/?tls=true&tlsAllowInvalidCertificates=true; the engine README uses the same form. The OSS DocumentDB Local page shows it in one connection-string example and uses --authenticationMechanism SCRAM-SHA-256 in its mongosh examples. Related TLS fact: the image default is --tlsMode allowTLS and 'With allowTLS the gateway accepts both plain and TLS connections; disabled behaves the same way. requireTLS rejects plain connections, so every client must connect with tls=true' (documentdb-local page; settings default at documentdb-local/scripts/documentdb_local_settings.sh:44). The self-signed certificate is generated on first start, valid 365 days, not renewed automatically; --cert-path and --key-file supply your own. Sources: engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/processor/ismaster.rs:118,193; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/documentdb_gateway_core/src/auth/manager.rs:159-166; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/reference/connection-string-options/; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/development-loop; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/README.md; https://documentdb.io/docs/documentdb-local/; engine clone documentdb-local/scripts/documentdb_local_settings.sh:44
- Should the microsoft/documentdb-mcp README show the 10260 gateway example instead of mongodb://localhost:27017? **documented.** The facts are settled; the change is a maintainer decision. The MCP README on main (last commit b967704, 2026-09-16, still the head of main on 2026-11-25) uses "uri":"mongodb://localhost:27017" in all three client quickstarts (README.md:73,95,115), in the legacy SCRAM profile example (README.md:231) and in the VS Code install badge, and the Learn MCP Toolkit page (ms.date 2026-05-18, updated_at 2026-11-23) repeats the same value while its topology table says the local row uses 'connectionString to local DocumentDB'. The DocumentDB local gateway listens on 10260 by default (Learn development-loop; OSS docs; gateway SetupConfiguration.json GatewayListenPort 10260; documentdb_local_settings.sh:30), requires a username and password ('Without one the container exits at startup', OSS DocumentDB Local page) and presents a self-signed certificate, so the published example cannot reach a default documentdb-local container; it only works if the operator remaps the port (engine README: 'You can use port 27017 ... be sure to update the port number in both your docker run command and your connection string'). The kit repeats the 27017 example in skills/mcp-setup/SKILL.md:88, README.md:156-160, GEMINI.md:23-27 and install.sh:527-528 while its own local-deployment skill prescribes 10260 with TLS and SCRAM. Observation: in run 20-store-locator-with-geospatial-search/20261124-185729 the generated app/.env.example used an RU-API style 'host:27017/?ssl=true&replicaSet=...' URI, not the vCore gateway. Sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/main/README.md; MCP clone README.md:73,95,115,227-232; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/development-loop; https://documentdb.io/docs/documentdb-local/; https://github.com/documentdb/documentdb/blob/main/README.md; engine clone pg_documentdb_gw/SetupConfiguration.json:9-10; kit clone skills/mcp-setup/SKILL.md:88; README.md:156-160; GEMINI.md:23-27; install.sh:527-528; runs/20-store-locator-with-geospatial-search/20261124-185729/app/.env.example
- Is ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO (Learn) or TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO (MCP source) the variable that enables stdio without Entra, and where does the Learn name come from? **engine_source.** TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO is the only variable the server reads today; ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO is the variable's former name, renamed before the Learn page was published. On main (b967704, 2026-09-16) src/config.ts:225 reads 'trustLocalStdio: parseBoolean(process.env.TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO, false)', .env.example sets TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO='false' with the comment 'stdio uses a trusted local process boundary and should only be enabled for trusted local development', and the string ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO occurs nowhere in the clone. The GitHub history of src/config.ts shows the old name present from commit 475700b (2026-04-29) through 4c5582a (2026-05-15) and replaced in commit 82e4359 (2026-05-21), whose diff removes 'allowUnauthenticatedStdio: parseBoolean(process.env.ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO, false)' and adds the TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO line; that commit shipped in PR microsoft/documentdb-mcp#83 'add default connection profile and stdio rate limit', merged 2026-05-27. The Learn MCP Toolkit page carries ms.date 2026-05-18 (three days before the rename) and uses ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO in all three quickstarts and in its 'MCP authentication' configuration table ('Permit unauthenticated stdio (development only)'); its updated_at 2026-11-23 did not pick up the rename. Two open repository items still use the old name: PR #32 (opened 2026-05-05, state OPEN, mergeable CONFLICTING, defaults ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO=true behind a new --stdio flag) and issue #52 (2026-05-13, README decision table). With the Learn value, trustLocalStdio stays false and the stdio transport is refused when AUTH_REQUIRED defaults to true. No rename back is tracked; the rename already happened in #83. Sources: MCP clone src/config.ts:225; .env.example:27-28; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/main/.env.example; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/pull/83; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/commit/82e4359 (src/config.ts diff); GitHub API commits?path=src/config.ts for microsoft/documentdb-mcp (observed 2026-11-25; file contents checked at each of 15 commits); https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/pull/32; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/issues/52; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Will the documentdb-local gateway default mongoAllowTransactionSnapshot to true (issue #736), and when? Why open: Issue documentdb/documentdb#736 is open with a maintainer saying 'Targeting 1.0; this needs a gateway change', but 1.0, 1.0.1 and 1.2 shipped without it and the last bot ping (2026-11-17) is unanswered. Best guess: Treat snapshot read concern as unsupported locally; kit text should say transactions work with default read concern and link #736.
2. What is the current target date for change streams in the open-source engine and therefore in documentdb-local (issue #50)? Why open: The maintainers' stated Q3 2026 target has passed; the issue is open with no newer date, and the engine CHANGELOG for 1.2/1.3 (lines 43,55,56) mentions only an internal 'change-stream heartbeat task' without any user-facing enablement. Best guess: State 'not supported in documentdb-local today (code 115)' and point to #50; do not promise a date.
3. Which exact tag or digest should the kit's CI pin? Why open: Primary sources settle the available tags and the 'use a versioned tag' recommendation but no source names a preferred pin; the choice between pg17-1.2.0, a digest, or the floating 1.2 is a maintainer decision. The kit's current digest sha256:4a341d... still resolves on GHCR as an amd64+arm64 manifest list but is no longer what any pgNN-1.0.x tag points to (pg17-1.0.0 now resolves to the 1.0.1 build, sha256:5c3df9c6...). Best guess: Pin pg17-1.2.0 (the build 'latest' resolves to today) by digest sha256:1d40546c..., and bump deliberately on each DocumentDB release; keep ':latest' only in copy-paste quickstarts.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/development-loop: Pulls and runs ghcr.io/documentdb/documentdb/documentdb-local:latest with no versioned-tag guidance (OSS docs say 'Use a versioned tag when you need a reproducible setup'), and says nothing about parity gaps versus the Azure cluster the tutorial migrates to (no DiskANN, no pq compression or 16,000-dimension vectors, no full-text search/createSearchIndexes, no change streams, no Entra ID, snapshot read concern rejected). Container name shown is 'documentdb' while every other Microsoft source uses 'documentdb-local'.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/oss: Stale (ms.date 2025-02-07, updated_at 2026-04-27): 'Getting Started' builds from source via .devcontainer/Dockerfile and connects with psql on 9712, and never mentions the prebuilt documentdb-local image or port 10260 that the development-loop tutorial uses; architecture names pg_documentdb_core and pg_documentdb_api while the repository ships pg_documentdb, pg_documentdb_core and pg_documentdb_gw; the title has a missing space ('DocumentDBis'). Inconsistent with development-loop (newer, updated_at 2026-11-23), which is the one to follow.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit: All three quickstart configs and the 'MCP authentication' table use ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO, the variable's pre-release name; it was renamed to TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO in microsoft/documentdb-mcp PR #83 (commit 82e4359, 2026-05-21, merged 2026-05-27), three days after the page's ms.date 2026-05-18, and the page's updated_at 2026-11-23 did not pick up the change. With the Learn value, src/config.ts:225 leaves trustLocalStdio false and the stdio transport is refused while AUTH_REQUIRED defaults to true. The local profile example mongodb://localhost:27017 also does not match a default documentdb-local container (port 10260, credentials required, self-signed TLS) although the topology table calls it 'connectionString to local DocumentDB'.
- https://documentdb.io/docs/documentdb-local/: Tag section lists latest, 1.2 and pg15-1.2.0..pg18-1.2.0 but omits the published 'stable' and '1.0' tags (both resolve to the same digest as pg17-1.0.1 on GHCR) and does not say that pg17-1.0.0 now resolves to that same 1.0.1 digest; a reader pinning 'pg17-1.0.0' does not get the original 1.0.0 image. The page also leaves the vector compression defaults implicit ('gated behind server configuration') although the image enables 'half' by default and cannot use 'pq'.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations: Has no statement that feature availability differs on the local container (DiskANN, pq compression, full-text preview, change streams, Entra ID are cluster-only); the free-tier line still says HNSW needs M40+ while the vector-search section says M30+ (already recorded under topic 7).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/how-to-connect-visual-studio-code-extension: 'Set up DocumentDB Local' is described only by a link to the extension's GitHub Pages manual, which does not name the image or tag it pulls; Learn should state the image (ghcr.io/documentdb/documentdb/documentdb-local), the default port search starting at 10260 and that sample data is loaded, since the OSS page says sample data is opt-in.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `kit skills/local-deployment/local-sample-data-management.md:7-14`: Claims the image 'seeds a sampledb database on first start (users, products, orders, analytics)' and that SKIP_INIT_DATA defaults to false (data loaded unless skipped). The image's dataset is StoreData (stores, ratings; documentdb-local/sample-data/), loading is opt-in ('add --init-data true'; --init-data|INIT_DATA default empty in documentdb_local_settings.sh:37), and SKIP_INIT_DATA is a 'Legacy alias for --init-data false' per documentdb.io/docs/documentdb-local/.
- `kit skills/local-deployment/local-connection-config.md:7,13,18-25`: States that a connection without --tls 'fails' / 'server rejects' and lists --authenticationMechanism SCRAM-SHA-256 as a minimum requirement. The image default --tlsMode allowTLS 'accepts both plain and TLS connections' (documentdb.io DocumentDB Local page; documentdb_local_settings.sh:44), and the mechanism is negotiated because the gateway advertises only SCRAM-SHA-256 (ismaster.rs:193). The TLS claim is true only under --tlsMode requireTLS.
- `kit skills/mcp-setup/SKILL.md:88; README.md:156-160; GEMINI.md:23-27; install.sh:527-528`: Give mongodb://localhost:27017 as the 'Local MongoDB / DocumentDB' URI with no credentials or TLS, contradicting the kit's own local-deployment skill (port 10260, username/password, tls=true&tlsAllowInvalidCertificates=true) and the Learn development-loop tutorial.
- `kit skills/local-deployment/local-docker-compose-setup.md:10,34; local-ports-and-bindings.md:28,42,50,60; local-choose-deployment-method.md:38; SKILL.md:11`: Rule text says 'Pin the image (or at minimum use :latest consciously, knowing it will drift)' but every example uses :latest with pull_policy: always; OSS docs recommend a versioned tag (pg17-1.2.0 today) for reproducible setups.
- `kit .github/workflows/diagnostic-regression.yml:23; docs/TESTING-MACOS.md:100; CHANGELOG.md:9`: CI pins digest sha256:4a341daca29b... described as 'the pg17-1.0.0 digest'. The digest still resolves on GHCR (manifest list, amd64+arm64), but pg17-1.0.0, pg17-1.0.1, 1.0 and stable all now resolve to sha256:5c3df9c6..., so the pin is an orphaned 1.0.0 build no named tag points to; it is also two releases behind latest (1.2.0, 2026-11-12).
- `kit skills/local-deployment/local-env-driven-config.md:68`: Production example host suffix 'mongocluster.documentdb.azure.com' does not exist in any Learn page; Learn quickstarts use <cluster>.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com or <cluster>.global.mongocluster.cosmos.azure.com. Observed as a readiness finding in runs/retail-semantic-search/20261122-152144/02-readiness.md (also 05-audit.json and doc-gaps.md in that run, and runs/retail-semantic-search/20261115-153948/02-readiness.md).
- `kit skills/local-deployment/local-choose-deployment-method.md:45; local-docker-compose-setup.md:73`: Reference links point at https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb while the image and docs now live under the documentdb GitHub organization (ghcr.io/documentdb/documentdb/documentdb-local; documentdb.io); the kit's own CHANGELOG 2026-10-19 records the move.
- `kit skills/vector-search (local parity) and skills/local-deployment/SKILL.md`: The kit's vector-search skill advertises DiskANN, product quantization 'up to 16,000 dims' and fp16 without noting that on documentdb-local only vector-ivf and vector-hnsw exist, 'pq' is rejected ('Compression type 'pq' is not supported for ivf/hnsw index'), and the caps are 2,000 uncompressed / 4,000 with 'half'; the local-deployment skill's dev/prod parity guidance does not list these differences.
- `MCP README.md:73,95,115,231 and VS Code install badge (README.md:1)`: Local profile example mongodb://localhost:27017 (no credentials, no TLS) cannot connect to a default documentdb-local container, which listens on 10260, exits without a password and serves a self-signed certificate; the README never mentions the DocumentDB local image. The README's TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO is correct; the mismatch with Learn's ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO is a Learn defect (name renamed in PR #83, 2026-05-27), though open PR #32 and issue #52 in the MCP repo still carry the old name and should be reconciled.

_Notes: Final report after skeptic review. Written to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-julietai-claude/40f65f7c-640c-44c6-8eab-a8434709e1ff/scratchpad/phase4/sme/topic-12.final.json. Changes from the first draft: (1) dropped the run-17 'managed DiskANN parity must not be assumed' observation (not in that file; verified by grep) and kept the DiskANN verdict on primary sources, re-citing the image build at packaging/gateway/docker/Dockerfile_documentdb_local:89-94 and packaging/deb/common/control:9 instead of the CI/deb-builder Dockerfiles; (2) moved the compression/dimension question from OPEN to SETTLED after confirming every engine line the skeptic cited (feature_flag_configs.c:156-161,769-779; create_indexes.c:3905-3938; vector_utilities.c:464-470; vector_index_kind_impl.c:252-257,330-335) and the OSS caps text, with one correction to the skeptic's reasoning: the published image gets pgvector from the PGDG apt package on debian:trixie (0.8.6/0.8.7 observed 2026-11-25), not from the setup_versions.sh v0.8.1 git pin, which feeds the Build-Ubuntu CI image; both exceed the 0.7.0 half-vector threshold so the conclusion holds; (3) moved the stdio-variable question from OPEN to SETTLED and went further than the skeptic: the GitHub history of src/config.ts (15 commits, file contents checked at each) shows ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO was the real name from 2026-04-29 to 2026-05-15 and was renamed to TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO in commit 82e4359 / PR #83 (merged 2026-05-27), so Learn (ms.date 2026-05-18) documents the pre-rename name; the skeptic's statement that #52 and #32 are 'unrelated' is wrong: open PR #32 (CONFLICTING) and open issue #52 both still use ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO; (4) corrected --init-data to documentdb_local_settings.sh:37 and the host-suffix observation to runs/retail-semantic-search/20261122-152144 (and 20261115-153948); (5) added a kit parity gap on vector compression; (6) marked the 're-pointed' tag wording as an inference from identical digests. Clones used: kit at 4f41c15 (2026-11-23), MCP at b967704 (2026-09-16, equal to origin/main on 2026-11-25; the clone is shallow, so history came from the GitHub API), engine at a5f1636 (2026-11-18). No Learn page under /azure/documentdb/ documents a local-only limitation; every parity answer rests on documentdb.io and engine source, so filings should cite those and ask Learn to add a parity section._

## Encryption, compliance and framework integrations (SME topic 13): researched 2026-11-25, 11 settled, 5 open

Settled from primary sources by a research agent and re-checked by a second agent that re-opened every citation. Kind: documented = current Learn, CLI, Terraform or driver docs; engine_source = github.com/documentdb/documentdb; observed_in_runs = this experiment's run folders.

**Settled.**

- Is explicit client-side field-level encryption (CSFLE) supported on Azure DocumentDB? **documented.** Learn says yes, with no how-to. The comparison page 'Azure DocumentDB vs MongoDB Atlas' (ms.date 2026-11-08, updated 2026-11-09), Security and compliance table: 'Client-side field-level encryption | ✅ Yes | ✅ Yes' with an empty Notes cell. The release notes (ms.date 2025-11-20, updated 2026-06-02), October 10, 2025, Engine enhancements (1017): '[Preview] Add schema enforcement with CSFLE (client side field level encryption) integration.' The engine source confirms the server side of that: CHANGELOG.md:278-281, heading 'documentdb v0.107-0 (October 22, 2025)': 'Support schema enforcement with CSFLE integration *[Preview]*' (the flag's source comment says 'Added in v0.108, enabled in v0.108', a one-version mismatch with the CHANGELOG); feature_flag_configs.c:600-601 '#define DEFAULT_ENABLE_SCHEMA_ENFORCEMENT_FOR_CSFLE true'; bson_json_schema_tree.c:680-700 parses the $jsonSchema 'encrypt' keyword when EnableSchemaEnforcementForCSFLE is set. Explicit CSFLE itself is a driver-side feature that MongoDB documents as working even on Community Edition ('MongoDB Community Edition | 4.2 | No | Yes' for automatic/explicit, CSFLE compatibility page), so nothing server-side beyond storing BSON binary subtype 6 is required. No Learn page documents how to use it, which drivers/libmongocrypt versions were validated, or key-vault (KMS) provider support; the security, encryption-at-rest, limitations, compatibility-features and FAQ pages do not mention it. Our runs never exercised it, only searched for it: no autoEncryption/ClientEncryption driver calls appear in any events.jsonl, while runs 07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub and 19-care-coordination-multi-agent contain the string CSFLE only inside learn_microsoft_docs_search tool queries; run 19 05-audit.json P31 suggested CSFLE and found no source (observation). Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compare-mongodb-atlas; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes; engine clone engine-src/documentdb/CHANGELOG.md:278-281 (engine commit a5f1636, 2026-11-18); engine clone engine-src/documentdb/pg_documentdb/src/configs/feature_flag_configs.c:600-601,1228-1232; engine clone engine-src/documentdb/pg_documentdb/src/jsonschema/bson_json_schema_tree.c:680-700; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/csfle/reference/compatibility.md; runs/19-care-coordination-multi-agent/20261124-195730/05-audit.json (P31, observation: CSFLE suggested, no source found); runs/07-voice-of-customer-sentiment-hub/20261124-204049/events.jsonl and runs/19-care-coordination-multi-agent/20261124-195730/events.jsonl (observation: CSFLE appears only in learn_microsoft_docs_search queries, never in driver calls)
- Is Queryable Encryption (QE) supported on Azure DocumentDB? **engine_source.** No, by engine source. QE requires encrypted collections created with 'encryptedFields'. MongoDB 'Encrypted Fields and Enabled Queries': 'Queryable Encryption requires a server-side encryption schema to enforce encryption of specific fields in a collection', 'you define encrypted fields at the collection level using an encryption schema', and 'For every encrypted collection, MongoDB creates two metadata collections'; the compatibility page adds 'You can use Queryable Encryption on a MongoDB Server 7.0 or later replica set or sharded cluster'. The engine rejects that option: pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_collection_view.c:526-529 — else if (strcmp(key, "encryptedFields") == 0) ereport(ERROR, (errcode(ERRCODE_DOCUMENTDB_COMMANDNOTSUPPORTED), errmsg("encryptedFields not supported yet"))). No Learn page mentions Queryable Encryption at all (security, limitations, compatibility-query-language, compatibility-features, FAQ, compare-mongodb-atlas, database-encryption-at-rest all checked). The compatibility table lists the QE maintenance command 'compactStructuredEncryptionData' as 'N/A' with footnote 2 ('Azure fully manages ... a PaaS service'), which is misleading given the engine does not support encrypted collections (see doc_fixes). Sources: engine clone engine-src/documentdb/pg_documentdb/src/commands/create_collection_view.c:526-529 (engine commit a5f16362, 2026-11-18); https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/queryable-encryption/fundamentals/encrypt-and-query.md; https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/core/queryable-encryption/reference/compatibility.md; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language (ms.date 2025-11-05, updated 2026-06-13)
- Is documentdb-mcp-server published on npm by Microsoft? (kit mcp.json runs it with npx; Learn says no package yet) **documented.** Microsoft has NOT published it. Learn 'MCP Toolkit - Azure DocumentDB' (ms.date 2026-05-18, updated 2026-11-23), Limitations > Compatibility: 'No published npm package yet. Install from source (git clone https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp.git && npm install && npm run build) or run directly with npx -y github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp.' Upstream README on main (latest commit 2026-09-16, same as the clone): 'The server is not yet published to a public package registry, so install it directly from this repository.' microsoft/documentdb-mcp#49 (open, 2026-05-13) plans v2 as '@azure/documentdb-mcp-server' and records that 'The unscoped documentdb-mcp-server was published then unpublished'; Azure/documentdb-agent-kit#20 (open) records 'npm view documentdb-mcp-server → npm error 404 Unpublished on 2025-04-17' and tracks swapping every plugin config to 'github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp'. There is no .github/workflows directory and no release in the MCP repo. OBSERVED 2026-11-25 on registry.npmjs.org: a package named 'documentdb-mcp-server' now exists again, version 0.1.0 published 2026-11-24T22:56Z by an npm user not identifiable as Microsoft, with no 'repository' or 'homepage' field, no README, two files, bin name 'documentdb-mcp-local', description 'Compact implementation of the stdio contract of microsoft/documentdb-mcp'; its index.js (read via unpkg, not executed) reads CONNECTION_PROFILES/CONNECTION_PROFILES_FILE and opens MongoDB connections. Whether it is benign is unknown; it is not the Microsoft server. The kit's 'npx -y documentdb-mcp-server' therefore no longer 404s but runs third-party code with the cluster credentials. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp (README.md line 21 on main, commit 2026-09-16); https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/issues/49; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/issues/48; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/pull/34; https://github.com/Azure/documentdb-agent-kit/issues/20; https://registry.npmjs.org/documentdb-mcp-server (saved to scratchpad/phase4/sme/npm/documentdb-mcp-server.json; observation 2026-11-25)
- Does the Azure MCP Server have a DocumentDB namespace today? **documented.** No. The Azure MCP Server tools page (ms.date 2026-08-11, updated 2026-10-16) lists Databases namespaces 'cosmos' (Azure Cosmos DB accounts, databases, containers, and documents), 'mysql', 'postgres', 'redis', 'sql'; no 'documentdb' or 'mongocluster' namespace, and the repo tree (microsoft/mcp main) has no DocumentDB tool area. microsoft/mcp#774 '[MCP Server Request] DocumentDB previously known as Azure Cosmos DB for MongoDB (vCore)' (labels server-Azure.Mcp, tools-CosmosDB, onboarding) was closed 2026-04-27 with 'Closing this as per discussion ..., blocked due to AOT issues, team investigating alternative approach to ship.' A maintainer comment in that thread said 'these tools should have a new namespace. I am in favor of documentdb'. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-mcp-server/tools/; https://github.com/microsoft/mcp/issues/774
- Is 'Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit' the canonical product name? **documented.** Yes for the Microsoft product name; the repository calls itself 'DocumentDB MCP Server'. Learn page title 'MCP Toolkit - Azure DocumentDB', first sentence: 'The Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit is an open-source Model Context Protocol (MCP) server ... and ships from the microsoft/documentdb-mcp repository.' DocumentDB blog, 2026-06-02, title 'Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit: Agentic AI for MongoDB-Compatible Workloads', 'the Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit in public preview' (supporting). The repo README H1 is 'DocumentDB MCP Server' and contains zero occurrences of 'Toolkit'; package.json name is 'documentdb-mcp-server'. Both Learn and the README mark it Public Preview. Recommended phrasing: 'the Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit (the microsoft/documentdb-mcp server)'. Note the distinct 'Azure Cosmos DB MCP Toolkit' (learn.microsoft.com/azure/cosmos-db/gen-ai/model-context-protocol-toolkit) is a different product. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit; https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp (README.md H1; package.json); https://devblogs.microsoft.com/documentdb/azure-documentdb-mcp-toolkit/ (supporting)
- Which LangChain Python package should the kit recommend: langchain-azure-cosmosdb or langchain-azure-ai? **engine_source.** langchain-azure-cosmosdb with class AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch. langchain-ai/langchain-azure PR #870 (merged 2026-07-28): 'Move Mongo vCore vectorstore to langchain-azure-cosmosdb and add deprecation shims in langchain-azure-ai'. On main (and in langchain-azure-ai 1.4.0, released 2026-11-17, tag libs/azure-ai/v1.4.0) libs/azure-ai/langchain_azure_ai/vectorstores/azure_cosmos_db_mongo_vcore.py begins: 'Vector Store for Azure DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) — DEPRECATED. This module has moved to langchain_azure_cosmosdb. pip install langchain-azure-cosmosdb; from langchain_azure_cosmosdb import AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch' and emits DeprecationWarning for AzureCosmosDBMongoVCoreVectorSearch. PyPI langchain-azure-cosmosdb 1.0.1 (2026-09-24) README table: 'Vector Store (MongoDB compatibility) | AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch | N/A | Vector search for Azure DocumentDB clusters with MongoDB compatibility'; changelog 1.0.1: 'We introduced the AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch integration and compatibility aliases for MongoDB-compatible vector search'. Learn ai-frameworks (updated 2026-11-25) already links the Python reference to reference.langchain.com/python/langchain-azure-cosmosdb/_vectorstore_documentdb/AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch. Learn build-rag-applications (ms.date 2026-05-06, updated 2026-05-08) is the stale one: it teaches 'pip install langchain-azure-ai' and 'from langchain_azure_ai.vectorstores.azure_cosmos_db import AzureCosmosDBMongoVCoreVectorSearch' — a module path (azure_cosmos_db.py) that has no history in the repo; the module is azure_cosmos_db_mongo_vcore and is deprecated. Caveat for the kit: langchain-azure-cosmosdb 1.0.1 pulls azure-cosmos, langgraph and langgraph-checkpoint as hard dependencies. Sources: https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain-azure/pull/870; https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain-azure/blob/main/libs/azure-ai/langchain_azure_ai/vectorstores/azure_cosmos_db_mongo_vcore.py; https://pypi.org/project/langchain-azure-cosmosdb/ (1.0.1, 2026-09-24); https://pypi.org/project/langchain-azure-ai/ (1.4.0, 2026-11-17); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/ai-frameworks; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/build-rag-applications
- Is there a LlamaIndex TypeScript vector store for DocumentDB? **engine_source.** Yes. run-llama/LlamaIndexTS packages/providers/storage/azure (npm '@llamaindex/azure', latest 0.2.1, modified 2026-06-05) exports AzureCosmosDBMongoDBVectorStore from src/index.ts; the class docstring reads 'Azure Cosmos DB for MongoDB vCore vector store ... You also need an index on the collection, which is by default be created automatically using the createIndex method'; createIndex() issues createIndexes with cosmosSearchOptions kind 'vector-hnsw' | 'vector-diskann' | 'vector-ivf' (file last changed 2025-12-02). The same package also has AzureCosmosMongovCore DocumentStore, IndexStore, KVStore and ChatStore. No Learn how-to covers it: query-knowledge-base (ms.date 2026-05-06, updated 2026-11-25) says 'The vector store integration is Python-only. If you're building a TypeScript app, use the mongodb driver', while ai-frameworks (same dates) ticks TypeScript/JavaScript for LlamaIndex — the code shows the tick is right and the how-to note is wrong. The developers.llamaindex.ai API-reference URL cited in the K30 draft (…/classes/azurecosmosdbmongodbvectorstore) returns 404; cite the GitHub source or npm instead. Sources: https://github.com/run-llama/LlamaIndexTS/blob/main/packages/providers/storage/azure/src/vectorStore/AzureCosmosDBMongoVectorStore.ts; https://github.com/run-llama/LlamaIndexTS/blob/main/packages/providers/storage/azure/src/index.ts; https://www.npmjs.com/package/@llamaindex/azure (0.2.1); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-knowledge-base; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/ai-frameworks
- Does the documented LlamaIndex 0.9.0 behaviour (constructor creates an HNSW index by default; pre_filter instead of MetadataFilters) still hold? **engine_source.** Yes, and 0.9.0 is still the current release. PyPI llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo latest = 0.9.0 (requires llama-index-core>=0.13.0,<0.15 and pymongo>=4.6.1,<5); repo pyproject.toml on main says version = "0.9.0". base.py: __init__ ends with self._create_vector_search_index(); that method does kind = self._cosmos_search_kwargs.get("kind", "vector-hnsw") and db.command(createIndexes ...) immediately; _query() reads kwargs.get("pre_filter", {}) for every kind and the file contains no reference to query.filters or MetadataFilters, so LlamaIndex MetadataFilters are ignored. Learn query-knowledge-base states the same: 'In llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo version 0.9.0, the vector-store constructor issues a createIndexes command before you ingest documents ... Without a kind option, the integration defaults to HNSW, even if index_name contains diskann' and 'use the integration-specific pre_filter argument rather than LlamaIndex MetadataFilters'. The quickstart note 'HNSW ... M30 or higher' means the default kind fails on M10/M20/M25 and free tier (limitations page). Sources: https://pypi.org/project/llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo/ (0.9.0); https://github.com/run-llama/llama_index/blob/main/llama-index-integrations/vector_stores/llama-index-vector-stores-azurecosmosmongo/llama_index/vector_stores/azurecosmosmongo/base.py (lines 118-146, 295-315); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-knowledge-base; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/limitations
- What do primary sources say about pointing PHI workloads at an Azure OpenAI / Foundry deployment (facts only; the wording decision itself is open)? **documented.** Learn 'Data, privacy, and security for Foundry Models sold by Azure' (ms.date 2026-05-18, updated 2026-06-05): prompts, completions, embeddings and training data 'are NOT available to other customers', 'are NOT available to OpenAI or other providers', 'are NOT used ... to improve their models or services', 'are NOT used to train any generative AI foundation models without your permission or instruction'; 'Prompts and responses are processed within the customer-specified geography (unless you are using a Global or DataZone deployment type)'; 'Foundry is an Azure service; learn more about applicable Azure compliance offerings'. Learn 'HIPAA - Azure Compliance' (ms.date 2023-04-05): Microsoft 'offers a HIPAA BAA as part of the Microsoft Product Terms ... for use of such in-scope Azure services'; 'For Microsoft cloud services in scope for the HIPAA BAA coverage, see Cloud services in audit scope'; 'Does having a BAA with Microsoft ensure my organization's compliance with HIPAA? No.' No Azure DocumentDB Learn page mentions HIPAA, PHI or LLM data flows (security, FAQ, limitations checked). Observed in runs: 09-clinical-notes-de-identification doc-gaps.md '[high] PHI and external LLMs' (no product guidance found; OpenAI run mode added without a compliance guardrail); 19-care-coordination 05-audit.json P31 'PHI and logging' not_assessable. Sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/openai/data-privacy; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/compliance/offerings/offering-hipaa-us; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/security (ms.date 2025-09-29, updated 2026-04-27); runs/09-clinical-notes-de-identification/20261124-194349/doc-gaps.md (observation); runs/19-care-coordination-multi-agent/20261124-195730/05-audit.json P31 (observation)
- Do the scoped npm names proposed for the official MCP package (@azure/documentdb-mcp-server, @microsoft/documentdb-mcp-server) exist on the registry today? **documented.** No. OBSERVED 2026-11-25: GET https://registry.npmjs.org/@azure%2Fdocumentdb-mcp-server and https://registry.npmjs.org/@microsoft%2Fdocumentdb-mcp-server both return HTTP 404 (not reserved, not published), while https://registry.npmjs.org/documentdb-mcp-server returns 200 with dist-tags latest 0.1.0, time entries '1.0.0': 2025-04-15 (the original Microsoft publish later unpublished, per microsoft/documentdb-mcp#49 and Azure/documentdb-agent-kit#20), '0.0.0-stage' and '0.1.0': 2026-11-24T22:56Z, bin 'documentdb-mcp-local', no repository field, fileCount 2, description 'Stdio MCP server for Azure DocumentDB / MongoDB-compatible clusters. Compact implementation of the stdio contract of microsoft/documentdb-mcp.' This confirms no official scoped package exists and that the only package under the unscoped name is the third-party one; it does not settle what name Microsoft will eventually use (see OPEN). Sources: https://registry.npmjs.org/@azure%2Fdocumentdb-mcp-server (HTTP 404, observation 2026-11-25); https://registry.npmjs.org/@microsoft%2Fdocumentdb-mcp-server (HTTP 404, observation 2026-11-25); https://registry.npmjs.org/documentdb-mcp-server (observation 2026-11-25; saved copy scratchpad/phase4/sme/npm/documentdb-mcp-server.json); https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/issues/49
- Was ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO renamed to TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO in the MCP server, and which name does the current server read? **engine_source.** Yes, by microsoft/documentdb-mcp PR #83 'add default connection profile and stdio rate limit' (merged 2026-05-27). Its diff replaces ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO with TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO in .env.example, in every README config snippet, in the VS Code install badge URL and in the sentence 'stdio uses a trusted local process boundary and is blocked unless TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO=true; use it only for trusted local development.' The clone (upstream HEAD b967704, 2026-09-16) reads only the new name: src/config.ts:225 'trustLocalStdio: parseBoolean(process.env.TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO, false)' and src/server.ts:37 exits with 'stdio transport is disabled when AUTH_REQUIRED=true. Set TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO=true only when launched by a trusted local MCP client.' Learn mcp-toolkit (updated 2026-11-23) still uses ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO in its stdio snippets and Configuration reference, so those snippets fail against the current server (see doc_fixes). Sources: https://github.com/microsoft/documentdb-mcp/pull/83 (gh pr diff 83 -R microsoft/documentdb-mcp); MCP clone documentdb-mcp/src/config.ts:225 and src/server.ts:37 (commit b967704, 2026-09-16); https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit

**Still open for engineering.**

1. Is automatic CSFLE (driver autoEncryption with a schemaMap or server-side $jsonSchema 'encrypt', via mongocryptd or crypt_shared) supported and validated on Azure DocumentDB? Why open: Learn only has a bare checkmark ('Client-side field-level encryption ✅ Yes') and a [Preview] release note about 'schema enforcement with CSFLE integration'; no page says whether automatic encryption was validated, whether listCollections returns the 'encrypt' validator to drivers, which drivers/libmongocrypt versions were tested, or how the preview is enabled (the engine GUC defaults to true, but the release-notes banner says [Preview] items need a support request). Best guess: Explicit CSFLE works because it is purely client-side; automatic CSFLE is plausible since the engine parses the $jsonSchema 'encrypt' keyword (preview) but is undocumented and untested. Filings should say 'explicit CSFLE is listed as supported; automatic CSFLE and Queryable Encryption are not documented, and QE encrypted collections are rejected by the engine ("encryptedFields not supported yet")', and ask Learn for a CSFLE page.
2. Is 'for PHI, use an endpoint covered by your organisation's agreements, for example an Azure OpenAI / Foundry deployment (link to the Foundry data-privacy page)' acceptable wording with no compliance claim? Why open: This is a legal/comms judgement for the DocumentDB and Foundry teams, not a fact a public source settles; Microsoft's own HIPAA page stresses that BAA coverage comes via Product Terms for in-scope services and does not by itself make a customer compliant. Best guess: Acceptable as drafted: it makes no HIPAA/BAA claim and cites Microsoft's data-privacy page. If reviewers want a compliance pointer, link the HIPAA offering page and 'Cloud services in audit scope' rather than asserting coverage for DocumentDB or Azure OpenAI.
3. Does the Azure MCP Server team plan a DocumentDB namespace (and when)? Why open: The only public artefact, microsoft/mcp#774, was closed 2026-04-27 as 'blocked due to AOT issues, team investigating alternative approach to ship'; no roadmap item, PR or Learn page has appeared since. The MCP Toolkit Learn page does not mention the Azure MCP Server. Best guess: No namespace in the foreseeable term; the Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit (microsoft/documentdb-mcp) is the data-plane answer and the Azure CLI/ARM remain the control plane. Filings should describe the two as separate and not promise an azmcp 'documentdb' namespace.
4. Which npm name and publisher will the official package use (@azure/documentdb-mcp-server per #49, or @microsoft/...), and will the team claim or report the unscoped 'documentdb-mcp-server' name now re-published by a third party? **(blocking)** Why open: Issue #49 is open with no assignee or date; the unscoped name was re-published on 2026-11-24 by a non-Microsoft account, which the repo owners may not know. Observed 2026-11-25: both scoped names return 404 on the registry, so no official package exists under any name yet. Best guess: Until a scoped package ships, every kit config should use 'npx -y github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp' (as Learn and the repo README do). Raise the squat with the repo owners in the kit PR; filings must not state any npm package name as fact.
5. Which LangChain Python package should Learn standardise on, given ai-frameworks links langchain-azure-cosmosdb while build-rag-applications teaches the deprecated langchain-azure-ai class? Why open: Two current Learn pages disagree; the newer page (ai-frameworks, updated 2026-11-25) and the library source point to langchain-azure-cosmosdb, but the only full how-to still uses langchain-azure-ai and an import path that does not exist in the repository. Microsoft owns both pages and should confirm the direction before the kit copies one. The Microsoft launch post 'Introducing langchain-azure-cosmosdb' (devblogs, 2026-05-01, supporting only) describes the package solely as an Azure Cosmos DB for NoSQL connector and never mentions Azure DocumentDB, the MongoDB vCore store, AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch or any deprecation of langchain-azure-ai, and a Learn-restricted search finds no page naming langchain-azure-cosmosdb beyond the ai-frameworks reference link. Best guess: Recommend langchain-azure-cosmosdb / AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch in the kit, mention that langchain-azure-ai's AzureCosmosDBMongoVCoreVectorSearch is a deprecation shim, and file a Learn fix for build-rag-applications.

**Learn and reference fixes this topic yields.**

- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compare-mongodb-atlas: Security table row 'Client-side field-level encryption ✅ Yes' has no note or link, while no DocumentDB Learn page documents CSFLE (security, database-encryption-at-rest, limitations, compatibility-features, FAQ are silent) and the release notes list the only related engine work as '[Preview] ... schema enforcement with CSFLE integration'. Either add a CSFLE how-to/limitations section (explicit vs automatic, drivers validated, KMS providers, QE not supported) or qualify the checkmark.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/compatibility-query-language: Administrative Commands row 'compactStructuredEncryptionData | N/A²' (footnote: 'Azure fully manages Azure DocumentDB, a PaaS service') implies the Queryable Encryption maintenance command is handled by the platform; the engine rejects QE collections ('encryptedFields not supported yet', create_collection_view.c:526-529). The row should read 'No' and the page should state that Queryable Encryption is not supported.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/security: Data encryption section covers only encryption at rest and TLS; add one sentence on client-side field-level encryption and Queryable Encryption support so builders (and agents) find the answer where they look. Requested in K03/K35.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/mcp-toolkit: Quickstart configurations and the Configuration reference use ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO, but the server on main reads only TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO (src/config.ts:225; renamed in microsoft/documentdb-mcp#83, merged 2026-05-27; its diff replaces the variable in .env.example and every README snippet) and exits with 'stdio transport is disabled when AUTH_REQUIRED=true. Set TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO=true ...' (src/server.ts:37). The Copilot CLI, Claude Code and VS Code snippets on the page therefore fail against the current server. Update the variable name (the repo README already uses TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/build-rag-applications: Teaches 'pip install langchain-azure-ai' and 'from langchain_azure_ai.vectorstores.azure_cosmos_db import AzureCosmosDBMongoVCoreVectorSearch'. The module path azure_cosmos_db has no history in langchain-ai/langchain-azure; the real module azure_cosmos_db_mongo_vcore is a deprecation shim since PR #870 (2026-07-28) that redirects to 'pip install langchain-azure-cosmosdb; from langchain_azure_cosmosdb import AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch'. The newer ai-frameworks page (updated 2026-11-25) already links the langchain-azure-cosmosdb reference, so the two pages disagree; update build-rag-applications and its description/H1 ('langchain-azure-ai vector store').
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/query-knowledge-base: Note says 'The vector store integration is Python-only. If you're building a TypeScript app, use the mongodb driver', but the ai-frameworks page ticks TypeScript/JavaScript for LlamaIndex and LlamaIndexTS ships AzureCosmosDBMongoDBVectorStore in @llamaindex/azure. Replace the note with a pointer to that package (or state that the TS store exists but has no Learn how-to).
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/ai-frameworks: Row 'LangChain on Azure' says RAG apps 'use the langchain-azure-ai vector store' while the LangChain Python reference link in the same page points to langchain-azure-cosmosdb's AzureDocumentDBVectorSearch; the JS reference link uses the old js.langchain.com '.../azure_cosmosdb_mongodb/' slug. Align the row text with the reference links and the deprecation in langchain-azure.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/quickstart-agent-nodejs: Cluster tier table says HNSW 'M30 or higher' and DiskANN 'M40 or higher', while the limitations page (updated 2026-11-17) says 'HNSW and DiskANN are available on M30 and greater cluster tiers' and its free-tier line says HNSW needs M40+. Three different statements; the limitations vector-search line is the one filings use.
- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/documentdb/release-notes: Banner 'Items tagged as [Preview] require a support request to enable them on your cluster' is applied to the CSFLE schema-enforcement item although the engine flag defaults to true (feature_flag_configs.c:600), and the same banner was already found stale for full-text search (topic 7). Clarify per item how each preview is enabled.

**Kit or MCP statements contradicted by a source.**

- `documentdb-agent-kit/mcp.json (lines 4-5) and documentdb-agent-kit/gemini-extension.json (lines 7-8)`: "command": "npx", "args": ["-y", "documentdb-mcp-server"] launches an npm package Microsoft has never published (Learn mcp-toolkit: 'No published npm package yet'; README: 'not yet published to a public package registry'; issues documentdb-mcp#48/#49, agent-kit#20). As of 2026-11-24 the name is occupied by a third-party package of unknown provenance (no repository field, no README, bin 'documentdb-mcp-local') that reads CONNECTION_PROFILES, so the kit now hands cluster credentials to code that is not the Microsoft server. Switch to ["-y", "github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp"] as skills/mcp-setup/SKILL.md, Learn and the repo README already do. Security-relevant; should not wait for the SME answer.
- `documentdb-agent-kit/AGENTS.md:105 and :123`: States the plugin 'bundles the DocumentDB MCP server (documentdb-mcp-server on npm)' and that 'The bundled MCP server runs via npx -y documentdb-mcp-server and refreshes on every agent restart (subject to npm cache)'. Contradicted by Learn mcp-toolkit and the upstream README (no published package). Same claim in CHANGELOG.md:115,121, GEMINI.md:3, docs/installation/updating.md:24 and .codex-plugin/plugin.json:27 ('bundles the documentdb-mcp-server').
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/mcp-setup/SKILL.md:78,136-157 vs documentdb-agent-kit/mcp.json`: The skill correctly uses 'npx -y github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp' and TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO, while the kit's shipped mcp.json/gemini-extension.json use the npm name. Internal inconsistency; the skill's form is the one to keep. (The skill's claim that microsoft/documentdb-mcp 'may be private' is stale: the repo is public and Learn links it.)
- `documentdb-agent-kit/skills/security/SKILL.md and skills/security/security-cmk-encryption.md`: Security skill covers TLS, Private Endpoint, Entra RBAC and CMK only; nothing on client-side field-level encryption, Queryable Encryption, or PHI/LLM data flows, so agents guess (runs 09-clinical-notes-de-identification doc-gaps 'PHI and external LLMs' high; 19-care-coordination P31). K03/K35 add the rule; the one-sentence CSFLE/QE statement can now be drafted from the sources above (explicit CSFLE listed as supported, QE encrypted collections rejected by the engine).
- `documentdb-mcp/package.json (name 'documentdb-mcp-server', version 0.1.0, no repository/publishConfig)`: The unscoped name the manifest declares is now taken on npm by a third party; issue #49 already recommends publishing as @azure/documentdb-mcp-server. Until the manifest is renamed/scoped, any accidental 'npm publish' or user 'npm install documentdb-mcp-server' resolves to the wrong package. Also note Learn's mcp-toolkit page lists 18 tools while the kit's AGENTS.md routing and K27 describe create_index/current_ops gating consistently with Learn — no error there.
- `findings/phase4/filings/K30-add-a-framework-integrations-skill-routing-langchain-an.md (draft 092) sources`: Cites https://developers.llamaindex.ai/typescript/framework-api-reference/classes/azurecosmosdbmongodbvectorstore, which returns 404; replace with the LlamaIndexTS GitHub source path or the @llamaindex/azure npm page. The draft's mapping table should also drop 'langchain-azure-ai (as on Learn today)' as a recommended option and mark it deprecated.

Standard applied as in DECISIONS.md: SETTLED only where a current Learn page, the engine/library source, GitHub issues/PRs or this experiment's run logs state the fact. Sub-questions: (1) explicit CSFLE, (2) automatic CSFLE, (3) Queryable Encryption, (4) PHI/Azure OpenAI wording, (5) npm status of documentdb-mcp-server, (6) Azure MCP Server DocumentDB namespace, (7) canonical name 'Azure DocumentDB MCP Toolkit', (8) LangChain Python package, (9) LlamaIndex TypeScript store, (10) LlamaIndex 0.9.0 behaviour, plus two settled items added from the skeptic's missed sources and re-confirmed here: (11) neither scoped npm name exists (both 404 on 2026-11-25), (12) the ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED_STDIO to TRUST_LOCAL_STDIO rename is in PR #83's diff and the current server reads only the new name. The skeptic's third missed source (MongoDB 'Encrypted Fields and Enabled Queries') was folded into the QE item: QE 'requires a server-side encryption schema' and 'For every encrypted collection, MongoDB creates two metadata collections', which the engine cannot create ('encryptedFields not supported yet'). The fourth (devblogs langchain-azure-cosmosdb launch post) is supporting only and is recorded in the LangChain OPEN item. Three nits applied: the CSFLE CHANGELOG citation is now CHANGELOG.md:278-281 (v0.107-0, October 22, 2025) with the note that the flag comment says 'Added in v0.108'; the run-log claim now reads 'never exercised, only searched for' (runs 07 and 19 contain CSFLE only inside learn_microsoft_docs_search queries); the unverified 'last commit 2026-11-13' remark was dropped from the LlamaIndex item. OPEN items unchanged: automatic CSFLE validation, PHI wording, a planned Azure MCP Server namespace, the official npm name/publisher (blocking, because it decides what the kit's mcp.json may contain), and the Learn direction for the LangChain package. The security-relevant finding stands: the kit's shipped mcp.json/gemini-extension.json launch the unscoped npm name that since 2026-11-24 resolves to a third-party package of unknown provenance (no repository field, bin 'documentdb-mcp-local') that reads CONNECTION_PROFILES; every launch target should become 'npx -y github:microsoft/documentdb-mcp'. Clones: engine a5f1636 (2026-11-18), MCP b967704 (2026-09-16, equal to upstream HEAD), kit 4f41c15 (2026-11-23). No connection strings, cluster names or e-mail addresses are included; the npm publisher is referred to only as a non-Microsoft account. ## Engine tests

Open questions about engine behaviour were tested against documentdb-local on 2026-11-25; results, scripts and raw reports are in `findings/phase4/ENGINE-TESTS.md` and `findings/phase4/engine-tests/`. Headline: one vector index per path (code 85), identical createIndexes is a no-op, listIndexes reports `cosmosSearchOptions` (Learn shows `cosmosSearch`), `$jsonSchema` enum and oneOf are enforced on engine 0.117, write conflicts return code 112 with no error labels, client-level bulkWrite is unsupported (code 59), the 25,000 write cap returns code 16, unindexed scans do not show COLLSCAN, and the npm package `documentdb-mcp-server` that the kit runs with npx is not published by Microsoft.
